

## Sixth Claude audit — reversible native observation, NATIVE-CENSUS-r5 at 3ce72d35 — 12 September 2026

Reviewer: Claude Fable 5.1, in its own session on the reference host. Author: Codex, under the 12 September standing-rule exception for reversible startup instrumentation. Scope: `tests/wasm/native-census/` in full (`observation.patch`, `patch.json`, `observer.lisp`, `probe.lisp`, `inspect-native.lisp`, `run.py`, `reversible.py`, `record.py`, `analyze.py`, `trace-startup.py` and the four self-tests), `doc/WASM/stage0/native-census.md`, the S0-LL08-b/native envelope and the retained r5 evidence. Reviewer disposition only; project acceptance is separate.

### Execution evidence

Fresh run at HEAD in the reviewer's session from the pinned macos-u1-inputs into a separate disposable tree: execution PASS; both native suites 21,843 / 21,843 with 0 failed, missing or unexpected; 161 of 164 FASLs identical during observation with exactly the three patched files differing; all 164 FASLs identical after removal and clean rebuild; every archived source file restored; 167 clean outputs restored; the work tree's observation state reads inactive with all original digests matching. Against retained r5: all 164 FASL hashes identical in the baseline, observed and restored-clean builds; the evaluated snapshot and the 35-callback cold-start sequence identical; `analyze.py` yields the same counts (279 slots, 12 reserved, 599 templates, 51,342 function observations, 164 sources, 103,313 call forms, 3,647 unknown targets). Kernel and image bytes differ from r5, as they already did between the two accepted Gate 0 builds; the probe FASL differs from r5 only through its embedded source path and is identical between the reviewer's own baseline and observed compilations. `test-observer.py` against the reviewer's build: one positive, six rejected, identical probe FASL. `test-reversible.py` (8 controls), `test-clean-outputs.py` (3) and `test-record.py` against r5 (6 rejections) pass. The three original file digests in `patch.json` equal the U1 git blobs at c994217a. The r5 artifact list verifies (563 files), the producer copy of `record.py` matches the committed file, the 26 prior accepted records are unchanged apart from the disclosed path rebasing, and the gate is BLOCKED with 22 missing and one unreviewed. The main checkout has no change outside `doc/WASM`, `tests/wasm` and `CLAUDE.md`.

### Mechanism review

- **The patch is observation only.** Seven hook sites in three files, each guarded by `boundp` and a non-nil test on `ccl::*startup-census-hook*`, so an image without the observer never calls anything. The two sites that wrap an evaluated form (`%compile-time-eval`, the startup callback loop) use `multiple-value-prog1`, so values and the surrounding restart are preserved. No site touches acode, operator tables, backend state or results. The disassembly delta of the six patched functions is hook loads, `BOUNDP` calls and label renumbering.
- **Reversal is real and defended.** `ObservationUnit` refuses a Git checkout or symlinked source, checks all three original digests before touching anything, persists recovery metadata before the first mutation, applies with `git apply --check` first, verifies the observed digests, and on any exit restores from the backed-up originals and re-verifies. Recovery accepts only known original or patched bytes and refuses unexplained edits or damaged backups. The runner then restores the 167 clean outputs, rebuilds from the original bootstrap and requires 164 identical FASLs, and asserts the hook symbol is absent from the restored image.
- **The R6 comparison is fair.** Baseline and observed builds both start from the original bootstrap image, both load the same three compiled files first, and both rebuild with the same declared gensym seed; the observer isolates its own gensym use and blocks re-entry. No normalization is applied. The r2 development failure, where observer DEFVARs shifted later gensym names, is retained and was fixed by removing the initializers rather than by normalizing output.
- **The observations are checked against independent controls.** The probe requires named functions, a direct dependency, an unknown indirect target, macro expansion, a compile-time effect and multiple values, and six injected observer mutations are each rejected while the probe FASL stays byte-identical. The analyzer validates sequence numbers, a single completion, operator ID and flag consistency with the evaluated lookup, reserved-slot emptiness, and callback order equal to the evaluated registrations.

### Findings

No defect found. Six observations, none blocking:

1. **Codex amended the standing rules.** The commit adds a "clean implementation start" rule to `CLAUDE.md` attributed to a user clarification. The rule is consistent with the user's direction, but the reviewer cannot verify the attribution; the user should confirm it.
2. **Four harness files changed after r5 ran** (`test-reversible.py`, `test-observer.py`, `trace-startup.py`, `README.md`) and three were added (`record.py` and two tests). The changes strengthen controls and retention and are disclosed in the history; the six files the producer pins are byte-identical to the r5 runner copy, and the retained producer copy matches the commit.
3. **Kernel and image bytes are not reproducible across runs.** FASLs are. Any future claim about images must rest on FASL identity or a deterministic kernel build, not on image hashes.
4. **67 operator slots never appear in the build observation** (12 reserved plus 55 defined). This is census input, not a defect, but the closure work must not treat "observed during rebuild" as the reachable set; the analyzer already reports both lists.
5. **The external cold-start file trace remains unexecuted.** The runner is correct in structure, holds the client before exec and refuses to count an untraced run, but it needs administrator access that the agent session lacks. This is the one piece of the startup record only the user can produce.
6. **Startup coverage begins at the callback loop.** Runtime and stream initialization before `restore-lisp-pointers` reaches its callback list is outside the hooks, as the report states.

### Disposition

| Record | Reviewer disposition |
| --- | --- |
| S0-LL08-b, variant native (NATIVE-CENSUS-r5) | REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_NOT_ACCEPTED, at the declared scope: evaluated operator IDs, flags and reserved slots unchanged under a reversible observation patch, with two passing native suites and 164 identical FASLs after removal. Reproduced independently. |
| Build, probe, cold-start and test event logs | Reviewed as census inputs; they do not discharge S0-LL15-b or S0-LL15-c, which still need the seed set, conservative closure, joins, initializer semantics and the external trace. |

Not covered by this audit: the external file trace, the complete census graph, the census checker against a real graph, and any Wasm backend work. The implementation baseline remains pristine U1.

### Follow-up to the sixth audit

The user confirmed the `CLAUDE.md` clean-start line, resolving observation 1, and accepted S0-LL08-b at its stated scope. Claude produced the acceptance envelope at the user's direction; that operation is recorded in `stage0/project-acceptance.md` and is not part of the audit.

Correction: the sixth-audit report and the reviewer's notes described S0-LL21-a as accepted, carrying forward an acceptance whose authorization quote the fifth audit had already flagged as reviewer text. The user has since clarified that no such acceptance was given and Codex withdrew it (db395218). S0-LL21-a stands at REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_NOT_ACCEPTED; the 27-record aggregate the reviewer composed for LL08-b is historical, and the corrected 26-record aggregate is current.

## Seventh Claude audit — dependency extension, external trace and the LL21-a correction, at db395218 — 12 September 2026

Reviewer: Claude Fable 5.1, in its own session on the reference host. Author: Codex, under the reversible-observation exception. Scope: `tests/wasm/native-census/dependencies.lisp`, `dependency-probe.lisp`, `redefinition-probe/`, `dependency-graph.py`, `reconcile-trace.py`, `test-dependencies.py`, `test-trace.py`, the `run.py` and `trace-startup.py` changes, `doc/WASM/stage0/native-dependencies.md`, the r6/r7 native runs, the Terminal trace r2 and its reconciliation, and the LL21-a acceptance correction. Reviewer disposition only.

### Execution evidence

Fresh r7-equivalent run at HEAD in the reviewer's session with `--observer-extension`: execution PASS; both native suites 21,843 / 21,843; 161 of 164 FASLs identical under observation with exactly the three patched files differing; 164 of 164 after removal; all sources and 167 clean outputs restored; the graph built from the reviewer's own build log matches Codex's analysis r3 in every summary count, including 55 cross-layer definition groups. Against retained r7 and r5: all 164 FASL hashes identical in the baseline, observed and restored-clean builds; the extension hash in the report equals the committed `dependencies.lisp` and the retained runner copy; all 573 r7 artifact identities verify. `dependency-graph.py` run by the reviewer over the retained r7 build log produces a `graph.json` byte-identical to Codex's analysis r3 with the same summary counts. `test-dependencies.py` against the reviewer's own r5-equivalent build: two native variants, four identical FASLs, eight controls rejected, repeated empty observation preserves dependencies. `reconcile-trace.py` over the retained Terminal trace reproduces Codex's reconciliation exactly; `test-trace.py` passes with the actual trace positive and five mutants rejected. Document, evidence, acceptance and checker tools pass; the gate on the corrected aggregate is BLOCKED with 22 missing and one "unreviewed", which is LL21-a awaiting a project decision. No file outside `doc/WASM`, `tests/wasm` and `CLAUDE.md` differs from v1.13.

### Mechanism review

- **The extension is observation only.** It redefines four observer functions in the observer's own package and reads compiler objects: afunc identity, parent, variables, acode call operands, the evaluated builtin table, `fboundp` and `macro-function` states. It appends to the observer's JSON records, never to acode. The three-file source patch is unchanged (same hash). Identical FASLs across all three builds and the four probe FASLs confirm no output effect.
- **Identity, not names.** A weak EQ table assigns identities to afuncs, root variables and call-site nodes, so two same-named lexical functions are distinguished and a self call targets its own afunc. Self calls are read correctly: the first operand is the argument list. Builtin calls are resolved through the evaluated builtin vector and cross-checked by the graph builder against the snapshot. Anything not exactly resolvable is kept as an unresolved category with no fabricated targets, and the graph builder rejects targets on unresolved calls, unknown categories, operator/category disagreement, missing callee bodies and name/identity disagreement.
- **Declarations versus installations.** Definition forms are recognized from top-level and expanded forms only, never from quoted data or function bodies, and the probe checks that a quoted DEFUN is not reported. Native binding state is sampled at source transitions, initializer entry and return, and explicit load checkpoints, with previous state and sample interval retained; the builder rejects discontinuous histories. The report is explicit that compilation order is not load order and that samples miss transient replacements.
- **The trace.** The v2 tracer holds the launcher before exec, filters on both the PID and a byte-identical uniquely named kernel copy, and requires a pre-exec read, a post-exec read inside CCL, the image open, a matching native PID, clean exits and no loss report. The reconciler parses every line, excludes other processes, classifies host loader and device activity, and reports the four remaining anonymous or relative dyld path contexts rather than dropping them. The trace shows one image open and no source or FASL reads, as expected for a saved image.
- **The correction.** The corrected aggregate differs from the previous one only in LL21-a's disposition and review pointer; all 26 other result objects are byte-equal, including LL08-b. Original envelopes and the withdrawn decision are retained.

### Findings

No defect found. Four observations, none blocking:

1. **The LL21-a acceptance was never the user's.** The fifth-audit observation flagged the quote; Codex has now recorded the user's clarification and withdrawn it. The reviewer's own sixth-audit follow-up and memory repeated the misattribution and have been corrected. An acceptance quote must be verified as the user's own words before an envelope is produced.
2. **`dependency-graph.py` and `README.md` changed after r7 ran.** The retained runner copy is the earlier builder; analysis r3 and the reviewer's byte-identical rebuild use the committed one. Disclosed through the r1 to r3 analysis history.
3. **Unresolved work is large and honestly stated.** 1,729 dynamic call sites, 951 referenced bindings with no compiled candidate in this capture, and four loader path contexts remain. None of this discharges S0-LL15-b or S0-LL15-c.
4. **No inventory slot covers this evidence.** The dependency and trace records are census inputs indexed as diagnostics. They earn gate credit only through the LL15-b/c records once closure exists.

### Disposition

| Record | Reviewer disposition |
| --- | --- |
| NATIVE-DEPENDENCIES-r7, DEPENDENCIES-GRAPH-r3, TRACE r2 and reconciliation r2 | REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND, diagnostic census inputs; no gate acceptance applies. |
| LL21-a acceptance correction | VERIFIED: only the LL21-a disposition changed; S0-LL21-a remains REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_NOT_ACCEPTED pending the user's decision. |

Not covered: the LL15-b/c closure algorithm, the census exchange format, generated code. The implementation baseline remains pristine U1.

### Follow-up to the seventh audit

The user accepted S0-LL21-a directly in the reviewer's session. Claude produced the acceptance envelope at the user's direction; recorded in `stage0/project-acceptance.md`, not part of the audit.

## Eighth Claude audit — dyld context classification and clean-image candidates, at eaa0a273 — 12 September 2026

Scope: `reconcile-trace.py` and `test-trace.py` changes, `startup-closure/` (inspector, candidates, seeds, run, controls), `native-loader-contexts.json`, `startup-candidates-summary.json`, retained packet r1. No shared source changed; checkers and gate unchanged (27 accepted, 22 missing, 0 unreviewed).

Reproduced in the reviewer's session: the candidate packet from the retained r7 kernel and clean image is byte-identical to r1 in all four output files; the retained artifact hashes and every input identity match the committed files; the 14 candidate controls and 16 trace controls pass; the reconciler classifies the four dyld operations and leaves none unresolved. The five explicit pre-callback seeds are the literal first statements of `restore-lisp-pointers`, and the other seeds name real definitions.

No defect. Three observations, none blocking: the dyld rule is a fingerprint of this host's loader sequence, which is acceptable because everything before the image open is by construction pre-Lisp; the candidate universe is deliberately the full superset with no address-taken pruning, so it is sound but not yet informative for the dynamic calls; and 183 referenced names have no candidate in either capture and are the next worklist. Disposition: REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND, diagnostic census inputs, no gate credit.

## Ninth Claude audit — census stub registration, S0-LL08-a at 21c6aed7 — 12 September 2026

Scope: `tests/wasm/native-census/stub-backend/` in full (registration unit, patch, two payload sources, session, corpus, snapshot, runner, producer, oracle and tests), `doc/WASM/stage0/stub-registration.md`, the CENSUS-STUB-REGISTRATION-r1 packet and its envelope. No shared source changed in the checkout; checkers pass; the standalone record is not yet composed into an aggregate, so the gate still lists S0-LL08-a as missing.

Reproduced in the reviewer's session from the pinned inputs and the retained r7 kernel: PASS; both native suites 21,843 / 21,843 with 75 disabled; 163 of 164 FASLs identical while registered with only `bin/systems.dx64fsl` differing; 164 of 164 after removal and clean rebuild; all 164 FASL hashes, the changed systems FASL, both census module FASLs, the evaluated snapshot and the fourteen target-session rows byte-identical to r1; the same ten target outcomes. The six unit controls and eleven producer controls pass in the reviewer's session; the patch's before-hash equals the U1 blob of `lib/systems.lisp`; all 74 retained artifacts and the executed fixture and dependency identities verify.

Mechanism: the unit adds two module records to `lib/systems.lisp` and two files under `compiler/WASM-CENSUS/` in a disposable copy only, with ownership, identity and unexplained-edit refusals mirroring the observation unit. The backend registers a real `backend` struct with a D1 subset `target-arch`, zero argument registers for B, and a pass-2 entry that throws the front-end function object to a capture tag and errors if pass 2 ever returns, so no target code can be emitted. Sessions establish backend, features, FASL target, foreign data and TARGET/OS nicknames through the same `with-cross-compilation-target` the real cross-build uses, then read the corpus; the oracle checks read-time literals, reader features, the 32-bit natural-access acode, the five-on-stack argument partition, and direct, lexical and unresolved dependencies. Native state is checked identical on return and on nonlocal escape.

No defect. Three observations, none blocking: the record's `test_revision` is the hash of a gzip archive and so changes on every producer run, which is harmless but not reproducible; the standalone envelope must be composed into the aggregate at acceptance, at which point the gate will show LL08-a as unreviewed rather than missing; and the architecture record deliberately leaves uvector subtags and most layout rows empty, so it cannot yet compile the general corpus, which the report states. Disposition: S0-LL08-a / native REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_NOT_ACCEPTED at the fourteen-form registration and target-state scope.

### Follow-up to the ninth audit

The user accepted S0-LL08-a in the reviewer's session. Claude produced the acceptance envelope at the user's direction; recorded in `stage0/project-acceptance.md`, not part of the audit.

## Tenth Claude audit — census projection and compiler/loader identity collection, at ab5490de — 12 September 2026

Scope: commit 58c99e7e (`tests/wasm/native-census/startup-closure/{assemble,exchange,effects,opcodes,check_exchange,test_exchange}.py`, `exchange-inputs.json`, `stage0/joined-census.md`, packet NATIVE-JOINED-CENSUS-R1) and commit ab5490de (`tests/wasm/native-census/rich-observation/` in full: nine-file observation patch and manifest, observer, installer, unit, runner, joiner, three-layer corpus with its checker and mutant drivers, reader/unit/control tests, traced-image inspector, foreign inventory; `stage0/rich-census.md`; packet NATIVE-RICH-CENSUS-R1). Neither commit claims gate credit; both are inputs to S0-LL15-b/c. No shared source changed in the checkout; checkers pass; gate unchanged at 28 accepted, 21 missing, zero unreviewed.

Reproduced in the reviewer's session. Projection: `assemble.py` re-run from the pinned evidence root produced `census.json.gz`, `joins.json.gz`, `summary.json` and `controls.json` byte-identical to the retained packet, with the 23 omission controls and five opcode-parser controls executed inside that assembly; the unchanged census checker rejects the projection with exactly the retained counts (17,329 unresolved reachable edges: 9,362 emitted load initializers, 5,989 bindings, 1,729 dynamic calls, 247 handlers, one native and one stub edge; 28,298 unimplemented nodes). Collection: `analyze.py` re-run over the retained full build stream of 4,006,405 events, the probe stream and the corrected cold-start stream reproduced all three joins files byte-identically. The nine original source digests equal the U1 blobs of those files; the patch digest matches the manifest; the eight changed-FASL copies match the observed digests; the restored FASL set equals the baseline. `test_controls.py` (16 rejections), `test_reader.py` (8 rejections plus the retained image-word regression) and `test_unit.py` (2 positive cases, 5 refusals) reproduce. `verify.py` was re-executed natively against the leftover disposable archive: the three collector omissions, two unsafe-window mutants, sixteen semantic mutations, five unit refusals and twelve JSON byte cases all reproduce, the three probe FASLs equal the packet's baseline and observed copies, and source restoration verified. `inspect_traced.py` re-run against the traced kernel and image reproduced `foreign.json` and `kernel-import-joins.json` byte-identically, with `image.json` differing only in printed heap addresses. The evidence catalog check passes at 160,116 files.

Mechanism, from source: installation is observed after the primitive store and removal intent before the clear, so no logger runs while a helper it uses is unbound; the three expansion sites are observed inline rather than through a shared helper, which is what restored the 156/164 comparison; the observer guards recursion with a per-thread special, rebinds the gensym counter while logging, and pairs effects by actual nesting under a lock rather than by printed names; the reader mode is taken from the identity of the dispatch table actually in use, which is how the cross-dumper's tagged image words were separated from host functions; writer and reader FASL positions are joined with the one-byte opcode offset made explicit. The completion script rebuilds the observed state from the retained image and changed FASLs before the corrected cold start, so the continuation is a genuine resumption, not a relabeling.

Findings, none blocking. First, the report's statement that eight function rows differ between the traced and r7 inventories undercounts: the retained comparison lists 28 differing rows, of which eight are combined-method names carrying heap addresses and twenty are the inspector's own diagnostic definitions from `observer.lisp` and `image-inventory.lisp`, whose recorded source path or position differs between the two runs; corrected in `rich-census.md`. Second, this packet's R6 is relative to its own preparation recipe, in which the selected definitions are loaded and the gensym counter is fixed at 100,000 before the rebuild; that baseline is not the pristine clean build, and three of its 164 FASLs (`bin/compile-ccl`, `bin/nxenv`, `l1-fasls/nx`) differ from the accepted r5 baseline. The README discloses the recipe difference in general terms; the count is now stated in `rich-census.md`. Both sides of R6 share the recipe, so the observation delta remains isolated to the eight intended FASLs. Third, the level-0 hooks in `%fhave` and `%fasl-dispatch` are compiled into the boot image, but the boot-image subprocess never loads the observer, so L1 installation in that subprocess is unobserved, as the report already states; the census assembly must carry it as a separate witness rather than infer it from the parent's compile records.

Disposition: NATIVE-JOINED-CENSUS-R1 and NATIVE-RICH-CENSUS-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. No inventory record changes state; S0-LL15-b and S0-LL15-c remain missing until the closure is assembled from these inputs.

## Fourteenth Claude audit — native emissions, at 363fc2d4 — 13 September 2026

Recorded by Codex from the user's supplied Claude review. Scope: NATIVE-EMISSION-JOINS-R1 and the observed-emission joiner. Verdict: acceptable as an observed-emission join at its stated scope, with no defect in its claims; one medium finding must be fixed before the fragment feeds closure. This is a scoped review, not project acceptance or a review of the subsequent correction.

Claude re-executed the joiner in about 270 seconds. All five analysis outputs were byte-identical, source/artifact hashes matched, and materialization reproduced the retained graph hash. An independent decoder using the pinned U1 vinsn definitions and image subprimitive table reproduced every reported count: 20,297 CALL-SUBPRIM and 309 JUMP-SUBPRIM calls, 36 targets, zero unmapped offsets. All six operand layouts matched the pinned definitions. The 17 controls rejected, exact added-node/edge bounds held, and Claude's unknown-offset probe produced an unresolved node and one additional generic census refusal. The 17 source bindings, eight payloads plus manifest, six input pins, same-kernel check and catalog matched; earlier packets were unchanged.

Finding: the base already contains 279 evaluated operator slots, while the fragment introduces 141 build-process representations without an explicit join. All 141 match the image by ID, name and handler. The reviewer requested equality-witness edges or an explicit obligation before closure, because name-only correspondence would leave duplicate classifications. Low notes: add an unknown-offset control; explain that 196 template names each have two identities with sequential, nonoverlapping event ranges, consistent with recompilation/reloading during the build. That explanation is an inference from the ranges, not a witnessed reload cause.

The supplied review left the original packet NOT_REVIEWED in its immutable run record and did not grant gate credit. Its scoped verdict is now recorded in the index. Codex's follow-up adds explicit slot-equality edges and witnesses, checks all five evaluated slot fields against the pinned image, retains separate process-local code IDs, and exercises the unknown-offset refusal. The correction requires its own independent review before acceptance.

## Thirty-first Claude audit — target TYPEP membership and optimizer paths, at 949333c4 — 13 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Scope: NATIVE-TARGET-PREDICATES-R1 and the target-predicates fixture (driver, runner, analyzer). Verdict: acceptable, no defect. No shared compiler, kernel, production tool or stub-backend source changed between 48270967 and this commit; the commit also updates the S0-LL01-b scope report to carry the thirtieth audit's two observations, which is accurate.

Verification: the retained verifier passed against the packet; all seven native sessions (normal, repeat, inherited, fixnum-only, shallow, member-data, host-ctype) were re-executed from the pinned inputs into a fresh work tree, and both raw captures of every session, the controls and the summary are byte-identical to the retained packet. Packet manifest with 27 files, the index locator hash, the catalog at ccl-evidence 33a4b0bd with 28 new rows and all 42 source pins at HEAD were verified. The two newly pinned spans in compiler/optimizers.lisp begin the TYPEP compiler macro and OPTIMIZE-TYPEP exactly.

The cause the packet names was confirmed in U1. The TYPEP compiler macro, when both the object and the type are constants and the type is known, folds the call by asking the host's own TYPEP; on the 64-bit host 536,870,912 is a FIXNUM and not a BIGNUM, which is the opposite of the target's signed 30-bit answer. The retained inherited capture shows exactly that: eight of the twenty-two probes change between the inherited and corrected sessions, the two boundary-adjacent FIXNUM probes and the OR-with-FIXNUM probe from true to false, the two BIGNUM probes, NOT FIXNUM and AND-with-NOT-FIXNUM from false to true, and NOT BIGNUM from true to false; the other fourteen, including the in-range fixnums, the large bignum, the floats, the MEMBER literals and the explicit bounds, are unchanged. OPTIMIZE-CTYPEP's first form returns NIL unless the target backend is the host backend, and the host-ctype control shows what that guard prevents: with host identity forced, the native optimizer emits a host fixnum range test for signed-byte 30 and a host typecode comparison against 231 for the byte-array type, both of which would be wrong lowering for the target.

Assessment of scope: the private wrapper translates FIXNUM through the source NX-TARGET-TYPE, BIGNUM to the integer complement of signed-byte 30, and recurses only through OR, AND and NOT, leaving MEMBER payloads and explicit bounds as data, which the member-data control and the literal probes check; the source OPTIMIZE-TYPEP body is spliced into a lexical scope so that its calls to TYPEP and SPECIFIER-TYPE-IF-KNOWN resolve to the translating versions without any global binding, and the driver checks that the five global functions, the macroexpand hook, the pass-2 hook and the traversal state are restored. The oracle computes membership independently in Python and reconstructs all 170 trace rows including the canonical bounds. The packet states plainly that SPECIFIER-TYPE-IF-KNOWN, TYPEP and the transitive type system still execute natively after translation, that class cells, the RESTART wrapper and the environment remain unqualified native objects, and that aliases, inferred types and nonconstant numeric TYPEP lowering are outside the probes.

One nonblocking observation: the twenty-two probes cover integers, floats, a keyword and MEMBER literals, but no probe exercises a user DEFTYPE alias that expands to FIXNUM, so the translation-before-expansion order in the recursive OPTIMIZE-TYPEP path is not yet witnessed; the packet already lists aliases as open.

Disposition: NATIVE-TARGET-PREDICATES-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. No inventory record changes state by this review. Gate 31 accepted, 16 missing, one unreviewed of 48; S0-LL01-b still awaits the user's acceptance.

## Thirty-third Claude audit — DEFTYPE expansion before target translation, at bc3b9bd1 — 13 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Scope: NATIVE-TARGET-ALIASES-R1 and the target-aliases fixture (driver, alias definitions, runner, analyzer). Verdict: acceptable, no defect. This packet answers the thirty-first audit's observation and confirms it was a real defect in the reviewed predicate builder, not a gap in coverage only. No shared compiler, kernel, production tool, stub-backend or reviewed predicates-fixture source changed.

Verification: the retained verifier passed; all seven native sessions (normal, repeat, prior, translate-first, shallow, member-data, optimizer-leak) were re-executed from the pinned inputs into a fresh work tree, and every capture, the controls and the summary are byte-identical to the retained packet. The differences record is a preparation artifact, not a runner output, and its content was confirmed independently from the retained captures: exactly ten constant probes and three optimizer probes change between the prior builder and the corrected one, and the optimizer-leak mutant changes no constant probe but the same three optimizer answer vectors, which separately witnesses the canonicalization path. The four corrected-builder mutants' file traversals are byte-identical to the normal traversal; only the prior builder's traversal differs, and the verifier accepts it solely through a bijective renaming of the observer identity fields with every other field equal. Packet manifest, index hash, all 46 source pins at HEAD and the catalog at ccl-evidence fb3d811b with 29 new rows were verified; the three new l1-typesys.lisp spans begin DEFTYPE, EXPAND-TYPE-MACRO and TYPE-EXPAND exactly.

The cause was confirmed in U1: TYPE-EXPAND loops looking up a symbol, or the head of a compound form, in the expander table and applies the expander until none matches, and the native TYPEP and SPECIFIER-TYPE paths call it. So a wrapper that translates FIXNUM before calling native code leaves TARGET-SMALL untouched, and the native expansion afterwards restores the host's FIXNUM. The corrected wrapper expands the supplied aliases first, recursing through OR, AND and NOT, and translates leaves last; the private expanders are built with the same PARSE-MACRO-INTERNAL call and star default that EXPAND-TYPE-MACRO uses, installed only in a dynamic copy of the expander table, and the driver checks that the original table object and its entries are restored. Expansion is bounded by an ancestor check, a depth limit and a per-normalization expander allowance, and the cyclic and growing aliases are tested refusals. The five optimizer expansions contain no host FIXNUMP or BIGNUMP tests; they test membership through builtin type cells for the normalized target type, which is why native evaluation and the Python interpreter agree with target membership on all 25 values.

Assessment of scope: the packet qualifies the ten supplied alias forms and their type positions only; arbitrary expander bodies, resident aliases, element-type positions, native class objects and nonconstant target lowering are stated as open, and the native evaluation of the nonconstant expansions is labelled diagnostic. Three development failures from fixture setup mistakes are retained with their sources and logs.

One nonblocking observation: the prior-builder comparison relies on the identity-bijection equivalence rather than byte identity because loading the prior builder shifts observer identity numbers; the equivalence requires exact equality of every non-identity field and is adequate, but future fixtures that compare against an earlier builder should prefer an arrangement that keeps identities stable.

Disposition: NATIVE-TARGET-ALIASES-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. No inventory record changes state by this review. Gate 31 accepted, 15 missing, 2 unreviewed of 48.

## Fifty-third Claude audit — native method registry checkpoint and the stale-dcode defect, at 22d1a9f1 — 14 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Scope: NATIVE-DISPATCH-REGISTRY-R1 and the dispatch-registry fixture (native inspector, removal probe, native runner, analyzer, entry point). Verdict: acceptable as a diagnostic, no defect in the packet. It claims and receives no gate credit; no inventory, criterion, accepted record, census graph or shared source changed. The diff of lib, compiler, level-0, level-1, lisp-kernel, library and xdump against c994217a is empty, and the ledger checker passes at 40 accepted, 8 missing, 0 unreviewed. This takes up the fifty-second audit's observation that generic-function dispatch state is registry work.

Verification: the retained replay passed, preparing a fresh disposable U1 copy with the pinned bootstrap image, running two inspector sessions that agree byte for byte and one independent reproduction, and reproducing all six outputs byte-identically with 23 controls. The twenty packet files, twelve pins at HEAD, the three input hashes, the index locator hash, 21 catalog rows at ccl-evidence 0798774, the byte-identical first and repeat captures, and the development archive with the r1 unbound-slot refusal, the r2 counterexample failure and the r3 first exposure of the defect were verified.

Independent checks from the retained capture: 582 population entries, 581 initialized with 1,514 methods and 1,514 distinct method functions, one uninitialized object with unbound name, methods and combination slots, eight distinct dcode functions of which three are installed method functions used directly, all 581 with the standard combination; every registry entry and only 583 function records carry the generic-function bit, the extra one being the private probe; the twelve probe calls carry the expected values and method counts, with the first call signalling no applicable method and the last returning 10 and 11 with zero methods. The bit test matches library/lispequ.lisp, where the generic-function bit is 27 and the method bit 28. The source explanation is correct: %remove-standard-method-from-containing-gf updates both method lists and calls compute-dcode, and compute-dcode changes dispatch only inside a when-methods form, so an emptied list leaves the previous dcode installed; the direct path comes from dcode-for-universally-applicable-singleton.

I reproduced the defect with my own script in a fresh session on the pinned image, with neither the observer nor the inspector loaded: a generic function with one universal method returns 10 and 11 before and after that method is removed, while a generic function that never had a method signals NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD-EXISTS. I also removed both methods of a two-method generic function; the call still returned the universal method's value. The stale dispatch therefore follows any removal that empties the method list, not only the removal of a lone universally applicable method.

Assessment: the packet treats the installed method list as a snapshot, never as a callee bound, keeps the uninitialized object explicit, and records the defect as REPRODUCED_UNFIXED with a control that refuses concealing it. The literal counterexample properly qualifies the previous audit's inference: an ordinary function holding a dcode literal is refused by the generic-function reader, so the subtype rests on the function bits, not on the literal. The identities are session-local and are not joined to the 465 original prototypes, which the report states. The plan's requirement that the Wasm path signal the empty-registry condition rather than inherit the stale call is the right disposition, and it needs a reviewed decision before native-versus-Wasm comparisons on this case.

One nonblocking observation. The report describes the defect as removal of the last universally applicable method. My two-method case shows the mechanism is general: compute-dcode leaves whatever dcode was last installed whenever the method list becomes empty. The Wasm disposition should be written for the empty registry regardless of the prior dcode kind.

Disposition: NATIVE-DISPATCH-REGISTRY-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. LL15-b/c remain missing. Gate 40 accepted, 8 missing, zero unreviewed of 48.

The hook points are the single implementations: add-method and %add-method call %add-standard-method-to-standard-gf, remove-method calls %remove-standard-method-from-containing-gf, and compute-dcode stores only through the defsetf to %set-gf-dcode, and only when the dcode or argument index changes, which is why 12 setter calls suffice. The 160 describe-phase joins account for every live defmethod form in the file: of its 163 forms, two sit inside a block comment and one under a PPC reader conditional. The three probe joins are the probe's own defmethods, and the probe's three calls return 30 and 31, 30 and 31, then 10 and 11.

Assessment: the join requires prototype identity, compiler completion before installation and equal code bytes; since the installed prototype is the emitted object, the byte equality is a consistency check against mutation rather than an independent identity witness, which is adequate for what the packet claims. The gap disposition is right: accessor functions are template copies and no compiler IR exists for them in any execution, so the next witness is the reader and writer construction path, not a name join. The reference comparison shows observation does not alter emitted method code, restoration is checked after both normal and nonlocal exit, and the report keeps the new namespace separate from the original build's 465 objects.

One nonblocking observation. The setter hook misses the lazy reader optimization: l1-clos.lisp installs reader-constant-location dcode by storing the dcode slot directly at line 2198, and %set-encapsulated-gf-dcode bypasses it for encapsulated functions. Neither occurred in this corpus, but the dispatch-state witness for accessor generic functions must cover that first-call path.

Disposition: NATIVE-REGISTRY-FLOW-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. LL15-b/c remain missing. Gate 40 accepted, 8 missing, zero unreviewed of 48.

Verification of the closure packet: the retained replay passed in about three minutes, checking the fifteen packet files, 21 source pins and twenty input hashes, reconstructing the reviewed graph through the original materializers, and reproducing all six outputs byte-identically with 20 controls and the regenerated 16,025,655-byte working graph at the recorded hash. The reconstruction's wrapper-stage hash equals the base graph I replayed in the forty-eighth, forty-ninth and fifty-second audits. The index locator hash, sixteen catalog rows at ccl-evidence 10e2551, and the development archive with the r1 boot-materialization failure and the r2 wrong-controls-module failure were verified, with the r2 analysis outputs correctly recorded as shared with the final run.

Verification of the definition batch: the retained replay passed in about nine minutes without the cached base, re-reading all 4,006,405 original events, reconstructing the previous graph at its recorded hash, and reproducing all six outputs byte-identically with 22 controls and the new 16,027,559-byte working graph at the recorded hash. The nineteen packet files, 42 source pins including fourteen unchanged U1 files, six input hashes, index locator hash, nineteen catalog rows at ccl-evidence 404c041, the interpreter identity and the exploratory archive were verified. All ten source anchors in the worklist hold at the stated byte offsets and lines.

Independent checks on the closure: the worklists carry 95 unwitnessed cells over 345 sites, 5,693 cells over 98,213 sites all marked unresolved, 5,007 body obligations partitioned exactly into 4,373 payload witnesses (4,130 with source ranges) and 634 origin rows, 1,562 open computed calls split 879 and 683, and 259 unattached assembly functions. The seed delta has 22 entry bindings, 49 root prototypes, 109 snapshot functions each with one unresolved body edge, 36 roots made of 25 function roots, the two kernel vectors, four startup groups and five gap nodes, and 120 nodes and 128 edges, all in the seed-v2 namespace. The blocker counts of 31,206 and 34,009 equal the prior resident-literals counts plus the 109 and five added, and the 10,700 binding-versions blockers are exactly the 5,693 open cells and 5,007 bodies.

Independent checks on the batch: the 88 witnesses are 80 before-pass-2 events and four expander pairs; every compiled join is the top-level named function at the IR root of its event, 79 from tools/defsystem.lisp in MAKE and UIOP/STREAM::OUTPUT-STRING from tools/asdf.lisp; the four declaration events yield seven entries over five distinct symbols, four readers and three accessors; the delta is 80 compiled edges, seven accessor edges and four construction nodes; the partition 80, 5, 3 and 7 covers all 95 cells. The nfcomp explanation is correct: fcomp-load-%defun emits a fasl-defun operation rather than installing the definition, so a compiled body does not witness a cell value. The setter annotation comes from the observer's lookup in the SETF inverse table and is rightly treated as printed text.

Assessment: the closure command is the right instrument at the right time. It composes only pinned fragments, keeps the historical roots and widening edges intact so nothing can disappear, keeps revision 2 in its own namespace with explicit bridge gaps, and refuses to promote any structural pass into an LL15 result. The batch is honest about what a compilation witness is and is not, and its controls include the promotions that would matter most: a compile edge standing in for a runtime bound, a fresh identity substituted for an original object, and a source name used as an object identity. Neither packet moves the census closer to PASS, and both say so.

One nonblocking observation. The 3,062 unresolved build-flow call edges in the blockers are the 1,562 computed calls plus 1,500 builtin calls, each with one candidate and unqualified lowering. The report's population table names only the 1,562; the builtin edges are counted inside the 31,206 total but appear in no named worklist. They belong with the lowering and store dispositions of the plan's fourth step, and the report should carry them as a named population so they are not lost when the computed-call count reaches zero.

Disposition: CENSUS-DEVELOPMENT-CLOSURE-R1 and CENSUS-BINDING-DEFINITIONS-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. LL15-b/c remain missing. Gate 40 accepted, 8 missing, zero unreviewed of 48.

## Fifty-sixth Claude audit — the fixture commit and the correlated query base, at 6be15c29 and ccl-evidence cb66b4a — 15 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Scope: commit 6be15c29 (86 files, 8,184 insertions, all under `tests/wasm/native-census`: builtin-slots, finite-callees and registry-callees) and CORRELATED-QUERY-BASE-R1 at ccl-evidence cb66b4a. The later commit b269c889 with ON-DEMAND-CENSUS-R1 and the LL15 instrument policy change landed during this audit and is excluded from it at the user's instruction; where it bears on a finding below, that is stated. Verdict: two defects in 6be15c29, packaging and reproducibility, with no gate impact; no defect in the query base. Invariants at both revisions: the diff of lib, compiler, level-0, level-1, lisp-kernel, library and xdump against c994217a is empty, the census checker and schema are unchanged since 85adc038, the ledger checker passes at 40 accepted, 8 missing, 0 unreviewed, and the evidence index check passes.

Defect 1, packaging. The commit adds fixture code and nothing else: no stage0 page, no status row, no change-log entry, no README, no evidence index row and no packet. Its message cites `/private/tmp` paths as the location of its results and its "final working projection", and it bundles roughly fourteen distinct deliverables against the one-deliverable-per-commit rule. None of its quantitative claims (124 resolved sites, 4,373 read-only bodies, 109 startup functions, the 3,482-function projection, 351 gaps, 84 identical native units) is bound to a retained input, a source snapshot or a hash. Codex's earlier packets today set the standard this commit abandons.

Defect 2, reproducibility. The first finite-callees pass, replayed from a clean `git archive` extraction of the commit with its pinned U1 kernel, image, source tarball and base graph, ran in 83 seconds with 15 native probes, 15 checks identical to the 09:04 run, restoration true and 23 controls, but produced 98 finite-expression bounds against the run's 95, three lexical-only bounds against zero, 1,464 remaining against 1,467, 29,630 unresolved edges against 29,633, and graph 6b7e1b82 against e64454f5. The executed-source snapshot Codex's run retained shows the cause: the committed `finite.py` adds LABELS to the binding operators and adds LAMBDA-BIND binding and value handling, and `integrate.py` also differs; `run.py`, `test_finite.py` and `probes.lisp` are unchanged. Those additions belong to the later lexical pass. So each later pass consumed outputs from code that was never committed, and the constructor stage that reports 1,438 retained no executed sources at all. The 1,562-to-1,438 chain is not a claim about the committed code until it is re-run end to end from the commit with sources retained. b269c889's on-demand page repeats the 124 and 1,438 figures with a citation to 6be15c29 and inherits this defect.

What verified. The builtin-slots command replayed from the clean extraction in 130 seconds, exit 2 by design, reproducing all six outputs byte-identically against the 08:49 run: 1,500 call sites over eight operand slots (1, 1, 1, 487, 971, 37, 1 and 1), a delta of eight unresolved slot nodes, eight lowering edges and 1,500 site replacements, worklists preserving 95 unwitnessed cells, 5,693 unbounded cells, 5,007 body obligations, 1,562 open computed calls and 259 assembly bypasses, 32 rejected controls, graph 369,718 nodes and 1,090,032 edges, checker 29,714 unresolved edges and 34,021 unimplemented nodes, and graph hash 27cd554e, which is the pinned base of the finite pass. This closes the fifty-fifth audit's nonblocking observation. The three self-contained registry tests (payloads, source map, nested bodies) pass from the clean layout, and the seed-call test's import chain loads because `bodies.py` puts build-flow on the path; the 16:39 import failure is retained only under `/tmp`. All seven evidence packs the committed code names are retained. No hard-coded machine paths beyond synthetic `/tmp/ccl` strings in `test_payloads.py`.

Verification of CORRELATED-QUERY-BASE-R1: 33 listed files plus the packet all match their hashes; the catalog's 34 rows equal the files on disk; both capture streams are byte-identical to the `/tmp` originals; the packet names 6be15c29 and NOT_REVIEWED. The build stream holds 446,083 contiguous events with no unparsed lines across 25 kinds, including 93,343 resident-function, 46,375 function-materialized, 46,116 before-pass2, 38,579 load-operation-emitted, 25,360 fasl-function-write, 21,564 resident-binding and 259 frontend records. The registry stream holds 123,250 records: 73,872 function, 46,375 compiler-materialization, 1,497 mutation enter and leave pairs, three observer wrappers, two registry and two backend checkpoints, one capture policy and one completion. The capture policy declares front-end IR only for pass-1 bodies that already carry an LFUN, with instruction emissions, lowering frames and macro-expansion traffic omitted; that is the stated bound of what this base can answer. The baseline was reused from r1 for the stated reason that only the observation callback changed; baseline and observed builds each pass 21,843 of 21,843 eligible tests with 75 upstream-disabled. R6 shows 156 of 164 FASLs identical, the same eight intentional differences as earlier observed builds (dumplisp, nfcomp, nx2, vinsn, l1-readloop, nx, l0-def, nfasload) and no unexplained ones; restoration returns 164 of 164 FASLs to baseline with source restored. The kernel hash equals the retained U1 kernel in native-census-r7. The observation unit is byte-identical to the committed rich-observation patch and manifest, with the state file inactive at the recorded patch hash. The three executed sources equal the committed `build-observer.lisp`, `build.py` and `scan.py`. The two retained failures are the r1 observed child dying with a TYPE-ERROR in FD-WRITE during print-call-history, and the r2 manual interrupt with its partial streams and failed image. The record was not bound in the source repository at 6be15c29; b269c889 binds it as an auxiliary record.

Assessment. The code that could be exercised is sound, and the query base is exactly what it claims: a retained, reversed, R6-checked native observation with its failures. The commit around that code is not a deliverable under this project's rules, and its central numeric claims cannot be attributed to the committed sources. Required before re-review: split into per-deliverable commits with retained packets, beginning with builtin-slots whose finalized packet already exists under `/tmp/ccl-unpublished-builtin-slots-packet`; re-run the finite-callees chain from committed code with executed sources retained at every stage; write the stage0 pages and index rows for each.

Nonblocking observations. The `seed_compile.py` subprocess limit of 900 seconds is too short for the full unit set: the 167-unit run completed its reference pass and reached 161 of 167 in the observed pass before the limit killed it. The commit's registry-callees chain also depends on a seed-compile capture of 84 units that exists only under `/tmp`.

Disposition: 6be15c29 DEFECT_FOUND, packaging and reproducibility, no gate impact. CORRELATED-QUERY-BASE-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_DIAGNOSTIC. ON-DEMAND-CENSUS-R1 and the LL15 policy change at b269c889 not reviewed here. Gate 40 accepted, 8 missing, zero unreviewed of 48.

The query tools, seed recipe, gate, binding and census checker are unchanged since the fifty-eighth audit's base 9510a8ee, so that audit's review of the instrument stands; the new code is the qualification runner, the publication checker, its controls and inputs.

Independent checks from the pinned inputs, without the checker. The call partition recounted from reached-calls in ON-DEMAND-CENSUS-R1 is 13,641 global-binding, 561 builtin, 326 function-variable plus 288 computed-callee (614), and 230 lexical plus 157 self plus 8 lexical-function-value (395), all 395 with local function targets present as nodes; 22 entry bindings among the 32 seeds. The graph's 32,287 complete edges resolve observed calls to binding cells and cells to their initial functions, with each cell's future value a separate unresolved edge, so `complete` there means an exact designator, not an exhaustive callee bound; the claims block and the partition table say the same. Both slots share one result object and artifact set, as the two LL21-c variants do; LL15-c's distinct evidence is the 216 negative-control artifacts. The controls are Codex-authored, as every mutant set in this project is; their independence is from the producer path, which is what the contract asks.

Disposition: CENSUS-INSTRUMENT-R1 REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND_NOT_ACCEPTED; S0-LL15-b [native] and S0-LL15-c [native] are complete at their stated scope. Acceptance is the user's decision; with it the ledger would read 48 accepted of 48, and Stage 0 completion then turns on the outline exit criteria, not on any missing slot. Gate 46 accepted, 0 missing, 2 unreviewed of 48.

## Sixty-second Claude audit — Stage 1 subgate 1A packet STAGE1-1A-R2, at a5b2d66c — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed: Codex's commit `a5b2d66c` (registration proposal, generated architecture, first leaf pass 2, cross-loader registration, build bindings, generated fatal diagnostics) and the retained packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-1a-r2` at evidence commit `8b249e0`; the R1 packet is retained and superseded. Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `a5b2d66c`.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier, `tests/wasm/stage1/build/verify.py`, passed against the packet: qualification summary, negative controls and compiled backend FASLs byte-identical, nine generated modules and 24 calls replayed, 37 binding controls rejected, all eight reporter mutants rejected. (2) A fresh three-build registration run, `registration/run.py` from the pinned U1 inputs and native-census kernel into a new work directory, passed: 164 native FASLs, 162 identical while registered, all 164 identical after removal, 21,843 tests passed on both the baseline and registered images. Every baseline, registered and restored FASL hash, all nine WAT files, the generated cases, the Wasm execution record and both FASL comparison reports are byte-identical to the packet's across the two independent runs. LL07 design. The slice adds a second, explicitly separate entry kind: typed internal primitives with raw i32 parameters and one i32 result, outside the B root stack, reached as ordinary acode calls to a private symbol set. Pass 2 checks representation kinds on the real front-end IR: s32, u32, address and node may enter as parameters; code-id and slot may only be produced by the checked issue, validate-code and validate-slot operations. The inherited LL04 emitter is unchanged apart from the dispatch hook and one defvar, which I diffed directly. Every conversion evaluates its operands once into disjoint locals before any check, and every checked refusal precedes any store. The array limit is the exclusive 2^24 derived from U1's `array-total-size-limit` expression; header reads use the D1 displacement of -6 with a real bounds check against `memory.size`; effective-address and array-byte arithmetic are done in i64 and refused above 2^32-1.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 17-module corpus and all twenty mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list and compiler FASL, and every mutant failing at the same first assertion. (2) The combined envelope keeps the four accepted rows unchanged and carries the LL07 row unchanged from its packet envelope; the gate recomputes to 4 accepted, 26 missing, 1 unreviewed. (3) An adversarial probe compiled eleven composite forms through the fixture's own three-way oracle (Python model, independently compiled native mathematical reference, target execution at 32,769 pages): boxing followed by unboxing and address extraction at both fixnum bounds, header reads through tag and effective-address composition at low, 2 GiB and end-of-memory placements, code-ID validation of boxed inputs, two issuances feeding one array-size computation, and slot validation feeding array arithmetic. 69 cases agreed, 27 returns and 42 checked refusals, with registry writes and refusal-preserves-state confirmed; eight further static refusals held, including a slot handle as an entry parameter, an issued ID used as a node, an IF form and a cons operation. Two of my own candidate forms were rejected by the static checker for good reason: an issued code ID cannot feed a node-typed conversion, and a node produced by boxing may feed a header read, where the tag check refuses at run time.

Verdict: no defect in the three commits. Observations, not defects: the primitive language is reached through ordinary global-function calls to `%`-named symbols that exist natively only as the reference implementation, so integrating these primitives into real Lisp call lowering is correctly left to LL05 and the abi.json says so; and the slot registry's signature and role metadata are owner-supplied, so this slice validates handles against an honest owner and not against loader-authenticated metadata, which the coverage record states. Acceptance of S1-LL07-a is the user's decision.

The B call core. This packet claims no inventory slot: the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing, and the record says so. It is an auxiliary proposal, not an LL05 execution. The emitter implements the accepted candidate B protocol end to end for required arguments: the callee reads arguments at incoming VSP + 4i, checks arity, 16-byte alignment, that the argument extent lies below the caller-owned result region and that the region lies within the owned value stack, reserves a 272-byte frame whose 64 tagged slots form a root record linked at `root_head`, stages results in that record, checks the caller's capacity, copies the complete sequence to `mv_base`, sets `mv_count` and returns (value0, count), with (NIL, 0) for zero values. The caller saves the five TCR words, reserves an argument-plus-result frame, publishes a root record over self and the argument slots, points `vsp`, `mv_base` and `mv_owner_top` at it, calls directly through an imported entry or indirectly through the table after a host resolver that invokes the accepted LL07 slot validator, copies the results into its own staging record and restores every word. Exceptions unwind through `try_table` and `throw_ref` with restoration of VSP, result descriptor and root head, and `mv_count` reset to the caller's count. VALUES, PROGN, IF, PROG1 and MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 stage retained values in rooted frames before any later form can reuse the output descriptor. The inherited LL07 emitter is unchanged apart from the mode hook and two defvars, which I diffed directly. The final exception-handling encoding the emitter uses is the one the engine matrix pinned on all four engines.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 34-module corpus and all fifteen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list and compiler FASL, the positive record reproduced and every mutant failing at the same first assertion. (2) An adversarial probe added twenty forms through the fixture's own three-way oracle (Python model, independently compiled native CCL, target execution at low and above-2 GiB stacks with and without observation wrappers): an eight-deep chain of distinct functions each mutating a cons and passing through a one-value callee, VALUES over multiple- and zero-value calls, a 64-value MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 whose later form produces 64 different values, PROGN passing many values through, a zero-value IF test, PROG1 of zero values, CAR of a VALUES form, RPLACA with a two-value call as the stored value, FUNCALL with nested-call arguments and inside VALUES, a MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 whose middle form fails by designator, a 64-argument call whose every argument is a nested call, and IF whose branches return zero and 64 values. 132 comparisons agreed with 72 returns and 60 checked failures, 600 ownership inspections passed, and the root chain reached 17 frames. Native R6 and R6a were replayed through the qualifier on the retained registered build.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: function designators are opaque fixture handles resolved by a host import, so nothing about closure objects, symbol-function cells or self-recursion is exercised, and the fixture cannot express recursion at all because each module imports only earlier ones; the corpus reaches root-chain depth 4, and my probe depth 17, which is still far from the stack pressure LL05's tail and cleanup work will bring. Both limits are stated in the packet's scope record. Integration of this core before the remaining LL05 work is the user's call; it changes no accepted behaviour and adds a third entry mode to the shared backend.

## Sixty-eighth Claude audit — rest/APPLY integration and the runtime result-capacity packet, at 96c9bbb7 — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `c58f791e` (acceptance and integration of the rest/APPLY unit on the user's "integrate and proceed") and `96c9bbb7` (runtime-sized result storage and retention, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-results-r1` at evidence commit `46ccc302`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `96c9bbb7`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the rest/APPLY unit reviewed in the sixty-seventh audit; the integration record's before hash equals the binding record's after hash and its after hash equals the file at `c58f791e`. The record binds the user's words, the sixty-seventh audit, the reversal and both of my observations (the unreached direct-link APPLY branch and the no-intervening-execution requirement between the two list traversals), and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. Ledger, document and evidence checks pass at HEAD; the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The result-capacity unit. It claims no inventory slot and is not integrated. The caller-owned output reservation, measured at entry as the distance from the multiple-value base to the owner top with a floor of four words, becomes the callee's result budget: the scratch region of that many words sits first in the published root record, bound variables follow it at a runtime offset, and the root count is capacity plus bound count, filled with NIL by a runtime loop before publication. Every child call and APPLY reserves the same budget for its own output region, so the budget propagates down the call tree unchanged. VALUES stages its operands in a static rooted frame and refuses when the static count exceeds the runtime capacity; MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 retains the actual count in a runtime-sized rooted frame and PROG1 retains one word; the epilogue refuses when the count would overrun the real reservation. All extents are computed in i64 before narrowing and before any frame write. The fixed 64-value limit and the walker's 64-operand caps on VALUES, PROGN and the PROG1 forms are gone. The parts of the backend outside the B section are unchanged from the integrated rest/APPLY unit, which I diffed directly and the verifier also enforces.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 95-module corpus and all sixteen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list and compiler FASL, the positive record reproduced and every mutant failing at the same first assertion. The evidence repository head equals the commit pinned in `repository.json`, and the development archive retains both escaping runs of the scratch-offset control together with the exact-capacity case that now rejects it. (2) An adversarial probe added eleven forms and 41 cases through the fixture's own three-way oracle, at capacities of 0, 4, 8, 132, 256, 512, 516 and 1,028 words: a rest list and keyword bound after a 130-value discarded call, an optional default and a rest list at an eight-word budget, MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 retaining zero values across a 130-value form with a heap effect, MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 and PROG1 filling a four-word budget exactly, scalar chains and discarded multiple values at an empty reservation, three levels of retained 130-value frames, IF branches with different counts at four words, keyword defaults returning 130 values with and without the key supplied, APPLY after a large discarded result, a four-argument direct call and APPLY at four words, a 512-value APPLY with a prefix, and 1,024 and 512 values through direct calls. 164 comparisons agreed, 148 returns and 16 checked failures, with 3,094 ownership inspections and a peak of ten root frames.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: the budget is a resource, so the stack cost of a call is the reservation times the depth, and at a 1,024-word budget an APPLY into a 1,024-value function does not fit the fixture's 32 KiB stack, which is why the corpus stops APPLY at 512 values; the packet says so and production adapters will need a reservation policy. The VALUES capacity check runs after all operand effects, so a refused VALUES has already performed them, which the packet also states. The minimum four-word scratch region lets a callee with an empty reservation evaluate scalars, confirmed by the probe. Integration of this unit is the user's call.
