## Seventieth Claude audit — callable-object integration and the lexical-closure packet, at 725a45c3 — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `eebe30ad` (acceptance and integration of the callable-object unit on the user's "accept, integrate and proceed") and `725a45c3` (escaping lexical closures and shared captures, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-closures-r1` at evidence commit `a18c237c`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `725a45c3`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the callable-object unit reviewed in the sixty-ninth audit; the integration record's before hash equals the result-capacity record's after hash and its after hash equals the file at `eebe30ad`. The record binds the user's words, the sixty-ninth audit, the reversal and both of my observations (owner-supplied registry rows and the unused slot-validation module, which this derivative removes), and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. Ledger, document and evidence checks pass at HEAD; the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The closure unit. It claims no inventory slot and is not integrated. Pass 2 now compiles a function and all of its inner functions as separate B modules under one source name, using CCL's own inherited-variable lists and root-variable identities to decide which bindings are captured. A captured binding lives in an 8-byte D1 cons cell whose CAR is the value; every captured binding of an activation (required, optional, supplied-p, keyword, rest and LET variables) gets its cell in one checked allocation at entry, so untaken LET branches also cost a cell, which the packet states. A closure is a 24-byte function object of the accepted layout followed by a D1 simple-vector environment of cell pointers, allocated in one checked block and given the inner module's imported code ID; the empty environment is NIL. An inner module's prologue validates SELF as a function object, validates the environment header and extent, and checks every cell pointer for a non-NIL cons tag inside memory before any captured load or store. Reads and writes of a variable go through one address function: cell CAR when captured, a private rooted slot when the variable is a LET binding or an assigned parameter, and the caller's argument word otherwise, so SETQ can never alter the caller's argument storage. LET stages its initializers in a rooted frame before binding, LET* binds sequentially, SETQ returns its value. The source validator gained lambda, FUNCTION of a lambda, LET, LET* and SETQ of an in-scope variable, and refuses FLET, LABELS, SETF, declarations and loops; U1's rewrite of a directly called literal lambda into LAMBDA-BIND remains refused at pass 2. The parts of the backend outside the B section are unchanged from the integrated callable-object unit, which I diffed directly and the verifier also enforces.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 177-module corpus and all seventeen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list, layout record, module index and compiler FASL, the positive record reproduced and every mutant failing at the same first assertion. The evidence repository head equals the commit pinned in `repository.json`, and the development archive retains the three recorded failures. (2) An adversarial probe added fourteen forms and 35 cases through the fixture's three-way oracle with byte-exact allocation expectations: a captured LET binding in an untaken branch, which allocates its cell and no closure; parent writes read back through both the variable and the closure; SETQ inside a LET* initializer; a closure whose keyword default reads a captured variable; a closure parameter captured by a grandchild created in an optional default, with and without the default taken; a closure passed to another module for invocation; captured aliased keyword and supplied-p variables; sibling setter and reader closures stored in a rest list; SETQ as a call argument beside a closure read; a captured rest list mutated before the closure reads it, including the empty case failing after allocation; a capture-free closure; a rest-taking closure invoked through APPLY; two activations of the same factory keeping separate cells; a setter at a four-word budget; and the 129-capture closure at its exact budget. 140 comparisons agreed, 132 returns and 8 checked failures, with 4,068 ownership inspections across 204 modules, and every allocation count matched to the byte. Two of my initial byte expectations were my own errors, corrected after the emitter and native agreed against them.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: applying a literal lambda is rewritten by U1 into a destructuring call and refused at pass 2 under the heap-constant reason rather than a dedicated one, so the declared LAMBDA-BIND boundary holds but the reason code is incidental; the "bound variables are roots" inspection now takes the expected root count from the emitter's own module index rather than from the source-derived count the earlier fixtures used, which weakens that one check's independence, though the root-count mutant is still rejected; and the fixture materializes static function objects for inner modules that nothing references. Integration of this unit is the user's call.

## Seventy-first Claude audit — closure integration and the local-function packet, at c2218c0d — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `6a868dfe` (acceptance and integration of the closure unit on the user's "accept, integrate and proceed") and `c2218c0d` (FLET, LABELS, lexical recursion, inline lambda binding and literal APPLY, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-local-calls-r1` at evidence commit `6f686760`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `c2218c0d`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the closure unit reviewed in the seventieth audit; the integration record's before hash equals the callable-object record's after hash and its after hash equals the file at `6a868dfe`. The record binds the user's words, the seventieth audit, the reversal and all three of my observations, and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. Ledger, document and evidence checks pass at HEAD; the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The local-function unit. It claims no inventory slot and is not integrated. FLET and LABELS bind each local function's variable to a closure built by the existing closure emitter, so a local function is an ordinary function object reached through a lexical cell rather than a global function cell. Because CCL's native backend calls local entries directly and therefore omits the function-cell captures, the emitter now plans environments itself: it starts from CCL's inherited-variable lists, adds the callee's cell to every function that makes a lexical call it does not own, and propagates every cell up through the lexical parents that construct each closure until the owner is reached; the union of these environments is the captured set, so a group's cells exist, NIL-filled, before any of its function objects is built, which is what makes forward references and escaping recursive groups keep one identity per binding. Self-calls dispatch on SELF; other lexical calls read the callee's cell and dispatch through the accepted resolver. U1's LAMBDA-BIND stages every argument in a rooted frame, binds the required parameters, builds any rest list in one checked allocation and evaluates the remaining initializers in order, so a directly called literal lambda is inlined without a closure. A literal APPLY is rewritten before pass 1 into a private LET that binds the lambda and applies the variable, and the rewrite touches expression positions only, so a variable named APPLY is left alone; the earlier version that walked binding syntax is retained as a recompiled negative control. Quoted symbols still name global cells even when a local function shadows the name. The source validator admits FLET, LABELS, `(function local)` and a lambda in operator position, and refuses local RETURN-FROM, duplicate names, out-of-scope FLET references, local macros and declarations. Two of my seventieth-audit observations are resolved here: the expected root counts now come from a pre-emitter IR witness recorded by a forwarding wrapper around pass 2 and derived in Python by an independent lexical-ancestor analysis, with a metadata-only mutant proving that this check does not read the emitter's own numbers; and the loader no longer materializes static objects for inner modules. The parts of the backend outside the B section are unchanged from the integrated closure unit, which I diffed directly and the verifier also enforces.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 241-module corpus and all sixteen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list, layout record, module index, IR witness, root contracts and compiler FASL, the positive record reproduced, every mutant failing at the same first assertion, and both development regression controls rejected. The evidence repository head equals the commit pinned in `repository.json`, and the development archive retains all seven recorded failures. (2) An adversarial probe added ten forms and 37 cases through the fixture's three-way oracle with byte-exact allocation expectations: a LABELS function that returns a closure calling itself, taken and not taken; a FLET nested inside a LABELS function calling a sibling of the outer group through a captured parameter; a literal APPLY of a lambda with an optional, a supplied-p flag and a rest parameter at four argument counts, and the same lambda called directly and inlined; a FLET created inside an optional default and returned, with the default taken, overridden by a one-argument function and overridden by a two-argument function; two LABELS functions with rest parameters recursing into each other through function values and APPLY at three list lengths; a lambda in operator position receiving a local function; a FLET parameter shadowing a captured variable; a LABELS function assigning a captured LET variable to a freshly allocated list; the same local function object stored twice into heap cells and recursed through CAR; and 150-deep LABELS recursion and 101-deep mutual recursion at a four-word budget. 148 comparisons agreed, 132 returns and 16 arity failures, with 5,608 ownership inspections, 264 modules and a peak of 303 root frames. Three of my byte expectations were wrong and the emitter was right each time: CCL's own IR marks a function as inheriting the variables of any local function it calls directly, and a literal APPLY allocates a closure rather than inlining, both of which the IR witness and the packet already state.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: a directly called literal lambda is inlined without allocation, while the same lambda under APPLY costs a closure, so the two forms have different heap costs though identical semantics, which is fine for correctness and worth remembering when tail transfer is designed; recursion still consumes Wasm and Lisp stack per level, as the packet states and the next unit addresses. Integration of this unit is the user's call.

## Seventy-second Claude audit — local-function integration and the proper tail-call packet, at 31efdf9f — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `5bbde980` (acceptance and integration of the local-function unit on the user's "accept, integrate and proceed") and `31efdf9f` (proper Wasm tail calls and stack-temporary literal APPLY, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-tail-calls-r1` at evidence commit `ab13524c`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `31efdf9f`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the local-function unit reviewed in the seventy-first audit; the integration record's before hash equals the closure record's after hash and its after hash equals the file at `5bbde980`. The record binds the user's words, the seventy-first audit, the reversal and both of my observations, and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. Ledger, document and evidence checks pass at HEAD; the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The tail-call unit. It claims no inventory slot and is not integrated. The public B entry becomes a wrapper: after the accepted arity and geometry checks and a complete preflight of the whole initial frame, it builds one continuation context at the owner top holding the caller's five saved words, a temporary-callable size, a root record and a copy of the arguments, points VSP and the root chain at that context and calls an internal three-argument tail entry; on return or exception it alone restores the caller. A call in tail position, after evaluating and rooting its designator and arguments and resolving the callee, checks the paired tail table, rechecks the reusable extent in i64, copies the arguments over the context's argument area with an overlap-safe copy, clears padding, publishes the new SELF and root count, restores the original output reservation, retires its temporary roots and issues `return_call_indirect`. Tail position flows from the body through IF branches, PROGN tails, LET, LET*, FLET, LABELS and inline-lambda bodies, and is cut off by every scalar context and by the first form of MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1, so a pending effect or retained value keeps an ordinary frame. Named, FUNCALL, APPLY, local, self and mutual calls share the path. A literal APPLY is now marked before pass 1 rather than rewritten into a LET, and its anonymous callable, which cannot be observed as a Lisp value, is built in zero-filled stack storage in the caller's frame; a tail transfer copies that object behind the new arguments and rebases its environment pointer, so the uncaptured case allocates no heap and the captured case pays only its cells, while a closure escaping from the applied body still owns heap storage. The paired tail table and its entries are owner-supplied, like the registry. The parts of the backend outside the B section are unchanged from the integrated local-function unit, which I diffed directly and the verifier also enforces.

Replays. (1) The documented verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, references hydrated from the accepted 1A packet, native qualification replayed, the 270-module corpus and all nineteen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical WAT, Wasm, native oracle, refusal list, layout record, module index, IR witness, root contracts and compiler FASL, the positive record reproduced including the 36 chains of 100,000 steps within a 2 KiB stack, every mutant failing at the same first assertion, and both inherited regression controls rejected. The evidence repository head equals the commit pinned in `repository.json`, and the development archive retains the five recorded failures, including the real ordering defect where the first wrapper wrote context words before its full preflight. (2) The supplemental composition probe, which rebinds V1 to the literal-APPLY caller and walks 100,000 nested singleton lists, replayed to a byte-identical result: 200,001 observed tail transfers per observed run, no heap, and the caller restored after the terminal arity condition. (3) An adversarial probe added ten forms and 22 cases through the fixture's three-way oracle inside the ordinary 32 KiB corpus stack, where a 3,000-step chain cannot complete without genuine tail transfer: named self-recursion, LABELS, FUNCALL through a function value, mutual recursion and APPLY of a function value at 3,000 steps; a tail call from inside a LET body; a chain alternating a stack-temporary literal callable with a named tail call at 2,000 steps, and the captured variant paying exactly eight bytes per activation; a tail call through a FLET closure at exactly 24 bytes per activation; a rest-taking function re-passing three arguments per step; keyword arguments swapped on every step over 1,001 steps; 130 values returned through 500 tail steps; a retained multiple-value form followed by a tail call; a closure escaping from a tail-position literal APPLY and invoked afterwards; and a non-tail nested call kept shallow. All 88 comparisons agreed on the first run, with allocation exact to the byte.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: tail legality is decided purely syntactically today, which is correct for this slice because no dynamic binding, cleanup or handler extent is admitted yet, and the packet says the rule must be revisited when those are; the stack-temporary callable is addressed from a root slot, so the collector work will have to recognise stack addresses in root records or the literal-APPLY case will need a different home, which the packet lists as an obligation; and every ordinary call now costs one context of 48 bytes plus its arguments above the caller's reservation, a fixed overhead the performance measurement should include. Integration of this unit is the user's call.

## Seventy-third Claude audit — tail-call integration, lazy installation and direct continuations, at bc38a410 — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed three Codex commits: `b610e006` (acceptance and integration of the tail-call unit on the user's "accept. integrate and proceed"), `6ccdf05d` (lazy installation of generated B entries, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-lazy-calls-r1` at evidence commit `567f6c2f`) and `bc38a410` (arguments prepared in their continuation with compiled calls entering the internal body, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-direct-context-r1` at evidence commit `ef7335f2`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `bc38a410`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the tail-call unit reviewed in the seventy-second audit; the integration record's before hash equals the local-function record's after hash and its after hash equals the file at `b610e006`. The record binds the user's words, the seventy-second audit, the reversal and all three of my observations, and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. Ledger, document and evidence checks pass at HEAD; the ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The lazy-installation unit. It changes no compiler or kernel source and claims no slot. A single-Worker loader admits a trusted catalog binding each code ID and version to its slot, module digest, profile, import inventory and both export roles, and places a stub pair in the public and tail tables; a stub calls the synchronous installer and then forwards SELF, count and, for the tail role, the continuation with a real tail instruction. Before instantiation the loader checks the digest, the profile, engine validation, the exact environment imports, that every other import is an immutable i32 global from the symbol, keyword or code namespaces, the import manifest, and exactly two exports with the B and tail signatures at the recorded indices; its own narrow reader refuses start, data, element, defined memory, table, global and tag sections and any function import, so instantiation can execute no user code. The installer refuses reentrancy, checks the shared registry row and both table identities before and after instantiation, publishes both entries with no suspension between the two stores, and on failure leaves both stubs in place for retry, with registry and table identity failures keeping designator code 4 and other failures using code 7. Import dictionaries are copied without invoking getters and admitted records are cloned, so the owner cannot change an admitted identity afterwards. The generated modules, native oracle and R6 evidence are pinned by digest to the accepted tail-call packet, and the harness is the accepted tail-call harness with only its installation step rewritten, so the entire target corpus must reproduce the accepted result exactly with every module cold.

The direct-continuation unit. It claims no slot and is not integrated. This is the optimisation I described to the user after the seventy-second audit, and the user directed it be done now rather than after timing. For an ordinary compiled call the caller reserves its result area, then one continuation with the callee's arguments, initialises the continuation's root record, evaluates the designator and arguments once in order directly into their final slots, resolves the callee, sets the callee's output descriptor and enters the internal three-argument body through the checked tail table; its own restoration state stays in Wasm locals. That removes the second argument copy, one root record and the public wrapper activation from every compiled-to-compiled call, reducing the child reservation from 64 plus twice the padded arguments to 48 plus the padded arguments. Ordinary APPLY still stages its prefix and list while it measures the list, then copies the prefix once and spreads the list straight into the final slots, keeping the staging roots linked until the final slots are filled and retiring them immediately before entry. Tail transfers are unchanged and still reuse the context. The public wrapper survives only at host boundaries, and the harness replaces every public table entry with a rejecting guard, so the whole corpus must pass with zero public dispatches. The parts of the backend outside the B section are unchanged from the integrated tail-call unit, which I diffed directly and the verifier also enforces.

Replays. (1) The lazy verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, the accepted base manifest, every pinned dependency and tool, then a fresh run reproducing the harness, catalog, stub, malformed binaries, execution, controls, mutant and summary records byte for byte, with 2,870 cold installations per mode over 267 distinct modules, all 2,964 comparisons and the 36 long chains equal to the accepted eager result, 35 installation controls (30 refusals and 5 positive behaviours including retry after a generated caller unwinds, immutable admitted inputs and no memory writes on publication) and 14 loader and stub mutants rejected. (2) The direct-continuation verifier passed: the 276-module corpus and all twenty mutant compilers recompiled through CCL with byte-identical outputs, every mutant failing at the same first assertion, both inherited regression controls rejected, and the unchanged lazy loader composed over the new binaries with the same observations. Both evidence repository commits match the pinned hashes, and the development archives retain the six lazy failures and the two direct-context escapes with their limits stated. (3) An adversarial probe added seventeen forms and 32 cases through the direct-context fixture's three-way oracle: the 3,000-step tail chains from the seventy-second audit rerun on the new call path; nested ordinary calls with a call result, a fresh cons and a further ordinary call as arguments; ordinary APPLY with three effectful prefix operands whose order is visible in the heap, with a 70-operand prefix into rest-taking and exact-arity callees, and with an APPLY nested inside an APPLY prefix; a literal APPLY with a prefix and a rest parameter; an error raised while evaluating an ordinary argument after a heap effect; forty nested ordinary calls each wrapping a fresh cons; and 130 values returned into an ordinary argument position. All 128 comparisons agreed on the first run with zero public dispatches, and the lazy loader then composed over the probe's 298 modules with 3,120 cold installations and identical observations.

Verdict: no defect in any of the three commits. Observations, not defects: the lazy loader authenticates bytes against the owner's catalog and is not code signing, which it states; the same-Worker publication atomicity is real but cross-Worker publication remains open, also stated; and in the direct unit one exploratory mutant restoring an empty result reservation between calls could not be observed because nothing polls between the restoration and the next entry, which Codex correctly declined to count as a rejected control and recorded as a collector-time obligation. Integration of both units is the user's call.

## Seventy-fourth Claude audit — lazy and direct-context integration and generated UNWIND-PROTECT, at 268af760 — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `4b86aad8` (acceptance and integration of the lazy-installation and direct-continuation units on the user's "approve integrate and proceed") and `268af760` (generated UNWIND-PROTECT cleanup extents, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-unwind-protect-r1` at evidence commit `4a6af76b`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `268af760`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the direct-context unit reviewed in the seventy-third audit, and the three new files under `runtime/wasm32` (loader, narrow binary reader and stub) are byte-identical to the lazy unit's. The direct-context record's before hash equals the tail-call record's after hash and the file at `b610e006`; both records' after hashes equal the live files and the proposal locators in the evidence repository; both packet manifests match. Each record binds the user's words, the seventy-third audit, the reversal and my three observations, and states that no LL05 slot is accepted. The non-B prefix of the backend is unchanged from the bindings unit. The ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The UNWIND-PROTECT unit. It is isolated, claims no LL05 or LL19 slot and is not integrated; its backend differs from the integrated one only by the new emitter, the operator admission and the exception-local declarations. CCL's real front end produces the `unwind-protect` operator with the protected form in a catch-marked lexical environment and the cleanup as a progn; the emitter admits it only through `b-multiple`. Before entering the extent the emitter reserves a tagged root record of `align16(8 + 4·capacity)` bytes through the accepted retained-frame helper, then checkpoints the explicit stack cursor, root head, VSP, MV base, owner and count in Wasm locals. Both arms are compiled with tail position off, so no call in either arm can transfer away pending cleanup, while their callees keep ordinary tail transfer. A final-encoding `try_table` with `catch_all_ref` surrounds only the protected form; on a caught exception the landing pad stores the exception reference in a per-site `exnref` local, restores the checkpoint, runs the cleanup and rethrows the same reference. The cleanup and the rethrow lie outside the `try_table`, so a cleanup error replaces the original and reaches the next enclosing extent, and no extent can catch its own cleanup or run it twice. On normal exit the count and values are copied into the retained record, the checkpoint is restored, the cleanup runs, and the count and values are copied back. Nested extents each own their record, checkpoint and exception local; a failed reservation throws before the extent is entered, so its cleanup correctly does not run. The heap allocation pointer is deliberately not rolled back. Engine traps are not caught and are not used as Lisp errors.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 307-module corpus and all eighteen mutant compilers recompiled through CCL byte-identically including the IR witness and independent root contracts, the positive execution equal to the retained record (3,504 comparisons, 132 cleanup-entry layout inspections, 12 resource and payload checks, zero public dispatches), every mutant failing at its retained first assertion, both inherited regression controls rejected, and the unchanged lazy loader composing over the new binaries with 3,644 cold installations per mode. (2) The development archive retains the four recorded failures: the discarded-CAR oracle mismatch, the 1,024-word reservation exhausting the 32 KiB fixture stack, the two restoration mutants that escaped value-only checks before the cleanup-entry observer was added, and the interrupted run missing the cleanup counters. (3) An adversarial probe added 25 forms and 46 cases through the unit's three-way oracle: SETQ of an outer binding inside the protected form and the cleanup; a closure allocated inside a cleanup that writes a captured cell; UNWIND-PROTECT inside both arms of MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1; zero values then a 130-value cleanup and 130 values then a 130-value cleanup; a cleanup error on the normal path; three nested extents whose cleanups mutate one cons in an order only inner-first cleanup produces, on both paths; an extent in an argument position followed by an effect and an error; extents in a parallel LET initialiser, an optional default, an APPLY argument and a FUNCALL function position; recursion with pending cleanup to depth 40 failing at the bottom, and a recursion failing in the middle of an improper list where the surviving cars distinguish inner-first from outer-first cleanup; a 3,000-step tail chain executed inside a cleanup on both paths; an extent inside a closure body called locally and after escaping to another module; an FLET function with its own extent called from a cleanup; heap objects allocated in the protected form surviving its failure; a nested extent inside a cleanup whose own cleanup fails; retained values across a heap-allocating cleanup; a rest list applied inside the extent with arity failures; and 130 retained values followed by an error in the next argument. Two of my first attempts were my own fixture errors: a constant `(car 5)` in a discarded cleanup position was rewritten by CCL into an IR shape the emitter refuses, and native CCL removes an unused CAR so three discarded error sites never fired natively, the same behaviour Codex recorded in its development log; both were corrected by making the errors effectful. All 184 comparisons then agreed on the first execution (84 returns, 80 type errors, 16 arity errors, 4 designator errors) with allocation exact to the byte, zero public dispatches and 336 modules, and the lazy loader composed over the probe's modules with 3,832 cold installations and identical observations.

Verdict: no defect in either commit. Observations, not defects: the checkpoint lives in Wasm locals and the handler chain is not yet published in the TCR, so a future signalling path or debugger cannot see these extents until that publication exists, which the unit states; the retention reservation is `align16(8 + 4·capacity)` per simultaneous extent, so deep recursion with pending cleanup and a large result budget is bounded by the explicit stack, which the retained 1,024-word failure shows and the unit states; the exception payload is held in an `exnref` across cleanup, so a moving collector must treat it as a root, already listed as an open collector obligation; and the harness's cleanup-entry observer is specialised to one corpus function, which is adequate for the mutants it was added to catch but is not a general layout oracle. Integration is the user's call.

## Seventy-fifth Claude audit — UNWIND-PROTECT integration and generated CATCH/THROW, at 3d711f76 — 16 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `df8dc488` (acceptance and integration of the UNWIND-PROTECT unit on the user's "approve, integrate and proceed") and `3d711f76` (generated CATCH/THROW with published control records, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-16-stage1-b-catch-throw-r1` at evidence commit `7631ac29`). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `3d711f76`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` is byte-identical to the UNWIND-PROTECT unit reviewed in the seventy-fourth audit; the record's before hash equals the direct-context record's after hash, its after hash equals the file at `df8dc488` and the proposal locator in the evidence repository, and the packet manifest matches. The record binds the user's words, the seventy-fourth audit, the reversal and all four of my observations, and states that no slot is accepted. The non-B prefix is unchanged from the bindings unit. The ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The CATCH/THROW unit. It is isolated, claims no LL05 or LL19 slot and is not integrated. CATCH and UNWIND-PROTECT now share one control record of `align16(48 + 4·capacity)` bytes: a raw header (previous head, kind, capacity, count, root address, saved unwind state, version marker) followed by a root record holding the tag and the value buffer. The record is reserved and checked before it is linked through the production TCR's handler checkpoint at offset 140, and the unwind state at offset 148 distinguishes ordinary execution, a pending nonlocal exit and another pending exception. CATCH evaluates its tag before establishing the extent and compiles its body with tail position off inside a `try_table` that catches only the shared `nonlocal_exit` tag; a caught exit whose payload is not this record's address is rethrown by reference, so identity is preserved across intervening catches. THROW roots its tag in a one-slot frame while evaluating its values, then walks the control chain with a generated validator (16-byte alignment, stack bounds, version marker, root-record linkage, admitted kinds, strictly decreasing addresses), matching only kind-1 records by EQ tag; a missing catch is the checked code 8 raised after tag and values are evaluated, a corrupt chain code 9. It checks the count against the target's capacity, copies the values into the target's rooted buffer, sets the unwind state to nonlocal exit and raises the exit carrying the raw record address. Every control frame retires itself on both paths: normal completion restores the enclosing head, its saved unwind state and the root head, so a handled nested exit inside a cleanup restores the pending state; exceptional completion classifies the exception by rethrowing it inside a private `try_table` and restores the head and roots before rethrowing. Cleanup landing pads now also restore the head to their own record and set the unwind state from the exception class. The public wrapper saves and restores the host's head and unwind state on both paths. The loader gains a distinct exit-tag capability and a new profile, and its narrow reader admits `exnref` in internal signatures only; imports and export roles remain exact. The rest of the backend is unchanged from the integrated cleanup unit, which I diffed directly.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 350-module corpus and all eighteen mutant compilers recompiled byte-identically including the IR witness and root contracts, the positive execution equal to the retained record (4,440 comparisons, 19,280 control-chain inspections, 18 unwind-state observations, 22 corrupt-chain probes, 150 cleanup entries, zero public dispatches), every mutant failing at its retained first assertion, both regression controls rejected, the ten loader controls and four loader mutants reproduced, and the extended loader composing over the new binaries with 4,804 cold installations per mode. (2) The development record retains the three failures (a fixture allocation expectation, an effect that misused the cleanup-only observer, and the reader's missing `exnref` admission) and the three exploratory mutants that received no rejection credit. Integration is the user's call.

## Seventy-sixth Claude audit — CATCH/THROW integration and generated dynamic special bindings, at ccfe2237 — 16 September 2026

Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `ccfe2237`.

Integration. The integrated `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp` and the three runtime files (loader, narrow reader and stub) are byte-identical to the CATCH/THROW unit reviewed in the seventy-fifth audit; every before hash equals the file at `a3759409`, every after hash equals the file at `a11554aa` and the proposal locator in the evidence repository, the backend's before hash equals the cleanup record's after hash, and the packet manifest matches. The record binds the user's words, the seventy-fifth audit, the reversal and all four of my observations, and states that no slot is accepted. The non-B prefix is unchanged from the bindings unit. The ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The special-binding unit. It is isolated, claims no LL05 or LL19 slot and is not integrated; the loader files are byte-identical to the integrated ones, which the verifier also enforces. CCL's real front end supplies `special-ref`, `bound-special-ref`, `setq-special`, LET and LET* variables carrying the special bit, and `%decls-body` for LOCALLY; the source walker admits only SPECIAL declarations, records the declared names, and refuses special lambda parameters, other declarations and PROGV. A declared symbol must come from the compiler's private package with a restricted round-tripping name, and is imported through the existing symbol namespace. Each symbol's binding index lives in its own tagged cell, distinct from the raw TCR index; the generated `dynamic_slot` validates the symbol header, the index representation and reserved zero, the current thread's vector pointer, capacity and extent before every access. A slot holding the no-thread-local-binding marker selects the symbol's global value cell, the unbound marker raises the checked code 10, and SETQ writes to the same location. Each dynamic binding evaluates its value first, then reserves a 32-byte record on the explicit stack holding the previous chain head, the tagged index, a two-slot root record with the symbol and the previous slot value, and a version marker; it is linked into the TCR's binding chain at offset 112 only after initialisation, and the new value is stored last. LET stages every initialiser before any binding takes effect and LET* publishes each before the next initialiser. A LET with any special variable wraps its body, compiled with tail position off, in an extent that snapshots the chain head, stack cursor and root head, and on both normal and exceptional exit calls `unbind_to`, which pops records back to that head, validating each record's bounds, marker, decreasing address and index consistency, and restoring the saved slot value literally, so a saved no-local-binding marker restores the global cell. Because each extent unbinds during exception propagation, a THROW passing through bindings restores them before any cleanup or catch outside the extent runs, and a cleanup inside the extent still sees the bound value. Callees keep direct continuations and real tail transfer. The remaining backend text is unchanged from the integrated CATCH/THROW unit, which I diffed directly.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, loader byte-identity, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 390-module corpus and all nineteen mutant compilers recompiled byte-identically including the IR witness and root contracts, the positive execution equal to the retained record (5,292 comparisons, 8,252 binding-record inspections reaching 40 simultaneous bindings, 44 metadata and capacity refusals leaving the symbols and vector untouched, zero public dispatches), every mutant failing at its retained first assertion, the three regression controls including the package-identity guard rejected, the inherited loader controls reproduced, and the unchanged loader composing over the new binaries. (2) The development record retains seven failures, including the package collision in which a private and a foreign symbol of the same printed name compiled to one import, now refused before publication, and a verifier refusal that caught an unintended loosening of the older leaf admission check, after which the reviewed prefix was restored byte for byte; I confirmed that prefix equality independently. (3) An adversarial probe added 24 forms and 39 cases through the unit's three-way oracle, each case also checked by the harness for a restored binding chain, a restored vector and the expected global values: a recursion binding the same symbol at each of 40 levels and throwing from the leaf to a catch outside them all, then reading the global; a recursion setting the innermost binding from the leaf and reading at every level; a cleanup outside the binding reading the restored value after an error inside it; SETQ inside a binding leaving the global untouched, SETQ of an unbound symbol creating a global value, and SETQ in an inner binding not leaking to the outer; reading an unbound symbol inside another binding, and reading it again after its own binding exits; parallel and sequential initialisers reading the outer binding; a LET* failing in its second initialiser after the first was published; a closure reading a special after the binding that surrounded its creation exited, and while another binding is active; a 3,000-step tail chain executed while a binding is active; a binding established inside a cleanup while a nonlocal exit is pending; three-deep same-symbol rebinding; lexical and special variables in one LET; a 130-value initialiser; a default parameter reading the caller's binding; an error in the binding body; APPLY with a special argument; and cross-symbol LET* initialisers. All 156 comparisons agreed on the first execution (128 returns, 20 type errors, 8 unbound refusals) with allocation exact to the byte, 19,468 binding inspections, zero public dispatches and 416 modules, and the loader composed over the probe's modules with 6,252 cold installations and identical observations.

Verdict: no defect in either commit. Observations, not defects: the binding vector has owner-assigned capacity with no growth, so admitting a new special symbol is a fixture decision until the general symbol installer and growth path exist, which the unit states; the symbol import namespace is shared with function symbols, so a special whose name matches a function symbol resolves to that symbol's binding-index cell and is refused at run time by the index check rather than at admission, which is safe but late; each special read makes two nested runtime calls, a cost to include in later measurement; and the record chain lives on the explicit value stack with the control records, so binding depth shares the result budget. Integration is the user's call.

The special-parameter and PROGV unit. It is isolated, claims no slot and is not integrated; the loader files are byte-identical to the integrated ones. The binding emitter is split so that any symbol can be bound from a computed value, and the special extent from the previous unit is reused. In a module whose lambda list has any special variable, the whole of entry, parameter binding and body is wrapped in one extent and the body is compiled with tail position off; required specials are bound in order from the incoming words, optional parameters and their supplied-p variables bind in order with defaults seeing earlier bindings, and a rest special is bound after its list is built. Keyword parameters are handled by staging matched values and supplied flags into the private slots, then installing the bindings in lambda-list order, evaluating a missing default in the environment established so far and binding a supplied-p after its parameter, so no later keyword is published early. Inline literal lambdas evaluate their operands first, then wrap binding and body in an extent, which is the development leak Codex found and a retained mutant reproduces. Local functions inherit the module path. PROGV requires the exact IR shape U1 produces, a `check-symbol-list` call wrapping the symbols form before the values form. The symbols list is rooted, validated by a generated walker that checks a finite proper list with Floyd's cycle test, each element's symbol header, fixnum flags without the constant or global bits, an owner-assigned binding index and the full record budget of 32 bytes per binding, then the values form is evaluated into a second root, and the list is validated again because the values form may have mutated it. Inside the extent the emitter walks both lists with checked CDRs, binding each symbol to the corresponding value or to the unbound marker when values run out, ignoring excess values, re-rooting the symbol cursor after each binding; repeated symbols stack records and restore in reverse. Eligibility failures raise the checked code 12, malformed list tails the type code 5, and an exhausted record budget code 2 before any value effect. The remaining backend text is unchanged from the integrated unit, which I diffed directly.

Fixture geometry. The harness writes a second, unread registry at address 512 with 8 bytes per module, and the code registry begins at 4096, so with more than 447 modules the two overlap and the first corpus case fails with a designator refusal. The unit's corpus has 443 modules and passed; my 19 added modules crossed the line. I confirmed the cause from the harness source, relocated that registry to address 49152 in a scratch copy of both the eager and the lazy harness, and reran; nothing else changed. This is not an emitter defect, but the next unit will hit it unless the fixture moves or removes that registry.

Verdict: no defect in either commit. Observations, not defects: the fixture's module ceiling of 447 is four above the current corpus, as described; a function with any special parameter loses tail position for its whole body, so self-recursion through a special parameter consumes stack per level, which the corpus's four-word budget for the 40-level case reflects and the unit states; PROGV's symbol cursor is re-rooted after each binding but the values cursor is a Wasm local, safe today only because the values list head stays rooted and no collector runs; and the PROGV guard binds this unit to the exact front-end IR shape, so a change in U1's PROGV expansion would refuse rather than miscompile. Integration is the user's call.

The integration record's before hashes equal the lexical-exit record's after hashes, its after hashes equal the files at `2b5459c4` and the proposal locators, and the withdrawal record names that commit and record and states that all restored bytes match its before hashes, which I confirmed. The withdrawal was made on my three observations, none of which was a defect, and the seventy-ninth review stands at its stated scope. The non-B prefix is unchanged from the bindings unit. The storage correction. It is isolated, claims no slot and is not integrated; the loader differs from the integrated one only by its profile string, which the verifier enforces, and the reader and stub are byte-identical. It answers all three observations. Each internal body now reads a result mode and a recipient descriptor from two previously unused continuation words, which the public wrapper clears. In ordinary mode nothing changes. In dynamic mode, entered only for MULTIPLE-VALUE-CALL producers and inherited by their descendants, a function reserves a 32-byte indirect root descriptor on the stack, marked with all ones so the root walker follows its buffer pointer and capacity, and every result production first ensures a buffer of the needed size in a thread-owned temporary arena described by the TCR's temp-stack cursor, base and limit, so a producer's value count is no longer bounded by the enclosing reservation. The arena allocator checks its bounds and block headers on every operation, computes extents in i64, initialises new words to NIL, reuses and coalesces free blocks, splits remainders and trims trailing free storage on release, and every function, producer, throw and tail transfer releases the blocks it owns. Ownership moves instead of copying: a callee delivers by re-owning its completed buffer to the recipient descriptor, a direct call or VALUES producer targets the continuation's argument words as its destination, catch, block and cleanup records in dynamic mode hold a descriptor and take ownership of the retained values, and a completed protected form needs no allocation before its cleanup runs. Only publication into the contiguous argument area copies, once. A literal callable and its environment now use the stack block already used for literal APPLY, relocated by the tail machinery through the context's ephemeral bytes, while captured cells and escaping inner closures stay on the heap. An executed-copy oracle wraps every copy instruction in a labelled derivative and counts the 520-byte copies on four cases, rejecting a semantically equivalent extra-copy mutant. The remaining backend text is unchanged from the integrated unit, which I diffed in full.
