(1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, the profile-only loader change, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 581-module corpus and all seventeen mutant compilers recompiled byte-identically including the IR witness and root contracts, the positive execution equal to the retained record (7,316 comparisons, 40 storage checks with exact bounds, guard bytes, cleanup effects under exhaustion and reclamation on both paths, 48 chains of 100,000 transfers, zero public dispatches), every mutant failing at its retained first assertion including the copy mutant through the copy oracle, the four regressions rejected, the loader controls reproduced, and the loader composing over the new binaries. (2) The development record retains seven failures, including the small-frame relocation mutant that escaped until a 64-binding frame exposed it, and fixture expectations corrected rather than the emitter. (3) An adversarial probe added 21 forms and 29 cases through the unit's three-way oracle, with the harness asserting after every case that the arena cursor returned to its base and, at every call entry, that each indirect descriptor's buffer is live, marked and within the arena: recursion to depth 40 in which every level runs a 130-value producer under a four-word reservation; interleaved allocations of 130, one and 130 words and a nested call inside one producer; a 130-value THROW from a dynamic producer into a static catch, and a small one; a special binding, a value binding, a PROGV and a 3,000-step tail chain inside producers; a literal callable in tail position relocated ahead of a 3,000-step chain, with and without a captured cell; a zero-value callee producer and a zero-value call inside a producer; forty scalar producers; an error after a producer had delivered directly; 1,024-value producers twice with a 130-value one between; a RETURN-FROM from a dynamic producer to a static block; an APPLY producer spreading a 130-element list built by another multiple-value call; and direct delivery through a self-call and a LABELS call at each of 40 recursion levels. One first attempt was my own fixture error: a leaf returning 130 values from a function whose caller reserved four words, which the target correctly refused. All 116 comparisons agreed on the first execution (112 returns, 4 type errors) with allocation exact to the byte, zero public dispatches and 605 modules, and the loader composed over the probe's modules with 8,260 cold installations and identical observations.

Verdict: no defect in any of the three commits. Observations, not defects: dynamic mode is inherited by every callee beneath a producer, so an ordinary computation under a producer pays an arena allocation for each result it produces, including scalars, which is a cost rather than a semantic change; the arena is bounded by the owner's temp-stack limit and its exhaustion is a checked refusal, which the storage checks cover; the indirect descriptors and the stack-resident callable are new root shapes the collector must learn, which the unit lists; and a producer's local descriptor is overwritten by the argument area before the root head is reset, safe today because no call or poll separates the two, and worth a comment at the site. Integration and renewed acceptance are the user's call.

## Eighty-first Claude audit — MVC storage correction R2, inline small results and stable producer roots, at 515e85ea — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed one Codex commit: `515e85ea` (R2 of the storage correction, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-b-mv-storage-r2` at evidence commit `cb085eac`; the index hash `b5860535…` equals the packet manifest's, which lists 6,095 files). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `515e85ea`.

The change. It is isolated, claims no slot and is not integrated; shared source stays at the withdrawal baseline, and the loader differs from the integrated one only by its profile string, now `v2`, which the verifier enforces. It answers the first and fourth observations of the eightieth audit. (a) The indirect root descriptor grows from 32 to 48 bytes with four inline value words at offset 32. A fresh descriptor points its buffer at those words with capacity four and NIL contents, so any result of at most four values is served without touching the arena. The ensure routine spills to the arena only above four values, copying the four inline words and never freeing the inline area; the deliver routine recognises a stack-resident source, one inside the value-stack bounds, and copies at most four words into the recipient's inline words or its existing buffer, refusing a stack source above four with the arena code, while arena sources still change owner without a copy. Control records in dynamic mode are 96 bytes. (b) The MULTIPLE-VALUE-CALL emitter reserves the producer descriptor below the continuation, after the 32-byte direct recipient, and stores its address in the recipient's word 24 as the surviving root; every producer reinitialises that descriptor instead of reserving one above the context's growing argument area. Direct delivery, in the deliver routine for call producers and in the VALUES producer, sets the root head to the surviving root before the overlapping copy; a callee whose recipient is direct restores that root in its epilogue instead of its entry root; and the scope release now follows publication of the argument roots. (c) The copy oracle brackets the direct copy with a handoff observer that requires the root head to equal the surviving root, the descriptor to lie below the destination, a valid chain on both sides and unchanged descriptor bytes. Two new mutants, forced arena allocation for small results and omitted retirement, are rejected, the second through that observer. The corpus gains five forms and four cases, the harness four zero-arena checks with the arena limit at its base, and the inherited lazy harness reader admits inline descriptors. The remaining backend text is unchanged from R1, which I diffed in full; the argument evaluator rebinds the producer target to nil, so calls nested in a direct producer's arguments deliver ordinarily.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, the profile-only loader change, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 587-module corpus and all nineteen mutant compilers recompiled byte-identically including the IR witness and root contracts, the positive execution equal to the retained record (7,332 comparisons, 56 storage checks, 48 chains of 100,000 transfers, zero public dispatches), every mutant failing at its retained first assertion including both new ones, the four regressions rejected, the loader controls reproduced, and the loader composing over the new binaries. (2) The development record retains three failures: an inline-source test that ANDed an address with a boolean, caught by the positive corpus; a forced-arena control that raised the intended checked refusal where the control runner accepted only assertion failures, wrapped rather than counted as passed; and the lazy reader rejecting the first inline descriptor, updated to the eager rule. (3) An adversarial probe reran the eightieth audit's 29 cases unchanged and added 41 forms and 38 cases through the three-way oracle: calls nested in a direct producer's arguments; four then five values through dynamic catches, blocks and cleanups; a scalar cleanup calling nested scalars in dynamic mode; a binding of five variables from four values and of two from five; one callee growing from three values to 130 within one frame and one shrinking from 130 to two, delivered directly, indirectly and through a catch; tail-position multiple-value calls in direct callees with two and five values; scalar recursion to depth 40 under a producer; special bindings and PROGV with four values; nested multiple-value calls of four and five; a literal callable with four arguments and one relocated in tail position ahead of a 3,000-step chain; four- and five-value THROWs from a direct callee to a static catch; an error after inline direct delivery; APPLY producers of four and five; self-call and LABELS recursion through tail-position multiple-value calls with four and five values at each of 40 levels; a 1,024-value call followed by an all-inline one; four values then 130 and 130 then four; direct delivery of five values from an arena source over the callee's frame; and zero-value producers between four- and five-value ones. All 268 comparisons agreed on the first execution (260 returns, 8 type errors) with allocation exact to the byte, zero public dispatches and 655 modules, and the loader composed over the probe's modules with 8,558 cold installations and identical observations. (4) A zero-arena run executed 27 further cases, 108 comparisons, with a patched copy of the eager harness setting the arena limit equal to its base for those cases, so any allocation would have been a checked refusal: the four-value paths above through catch, block, cleanup, binding, nested calls, special bindings, PROGV, APPLY, tail-position calls, the relocated literal callable, scalar recursion and self-call and LABELS recursion at 40 levels. All agreed.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, not defects: the handoff observer sees only 130-value direct deliveries (the eight observations are all from `rv_values`), so no inline direct delivery is observed by it, though with at most four values the destination cannot reach the callee's root record and retirement is not load-bearing there; dynamic mode is still inherited by every callee beneath a producer, but an arena allocation now occurs only above four values, and since the allocator walks every block from the arena base on each allocation and the release walks again, the inline path also avoids that linear scan; the price is 16 bytes per descriptor, 96-byte control records and a copy of at most four words on retirement in place of an ownership move; and the collector obligations are unchanged. Renewed acceptance and integration are the user's call.

## Eighty-second Claude audit — MVC storage R2 integration, and explicit condition dispatch, at a9f6349b — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `ffbf34d4` (renewed acceptance and integration of the R2 storage correction on the user's "accept, integrate and proceed") and `a9f6349b` (explicit SIGNAL, ERROR, HANDLER-BIND and HANDLER-CASE, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-b-conditions-r1` at evidence commit `dc451406`; the index hash `d9fcbd1e…` equals the packet manifest's). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `a9f6349b`.

Integration. The integrated backend and loader are byte-identical to the R2 unit reviewed in the eighty-first audit, and the reader and stub are unchanged. Every before hash equals the file at `e3c2073b` and the lexical-exit record's after hash, every after hash equals the file at `ffbf34d4`, and the record binds the user's words, the eighty-first audit, its four observations and the packet manifest, and states that no slot is accepted. The conditions commit leaves the shared backend unchanged. The ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

The conditions unit. It is isolated, claims no slot and is not integrated; the loader, reader and stub are byte-identical to the integrated ones and the profile is unchanged. A pre-expander runs before the source validator: it checks the input graph for sharing, cycles and dotted tails, validates every handler clause's type against a fixed nine-entry mask table, then invokes CCL's own HANDLER-BIND and HANDLER-CASE macros and walks their expansions, admitting `(the list %handlers%)` only while expanding, stripping DYNAMIC-EXTENT declarations, rewriting LIST to nested CONS, expanding POP, and rewriting CASE to a LET over EQ tests. The reserved special `%handlers%` maps to an owner-installed symbol. Quoted condition names compile to tagged masks; EQ compiles to an integer comparison. SIGNAL and ERROR compile to a dispatcher that mirrors CCL's native `signal` exactly: it roots the condition, the cluster, the handler cursor and the handler in a four-slot frame, validates the proxy's mask, binds `%handlers%` to itself for the extent, and for each cluster pops it into the binding before walking it as a plist, throwing the condition to the cluster for a NIL handler, throwing an index-condition cons for a fixnum handler, and otherwise calling the handler in dynamic result mode through a private 48-byte descriptor whose values are discarded and whose arena scope is released on both paths. A declined ERROR raises checked refusal 15. The condition runtime is emitted only into modules that signal. The remaining backend text is unchanged from the integrated R2 backend, which I diffed in full.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, loader identity, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests in each build), the inherited 587-module corpus byte-identical to R2 with its 7,332 comparisons, nineteen mutants, copy and handoff observations and four regressions, the 62-module condition corpus with 848 comparisons at two placements with and without entry inspection, 28 malformed-proxy refusals, ten dispatcher mutants rejected including the visible-cluster omission by its progress deadline, and cold installation of the condition corpus through the unchanged loader. (2) The development record retains twelve failures, including the initial native oracle whose outer HANDLER-CASE consumed declining signals, replaced by the debugger hook, and the assembly error that dropped the dispatch body. (3) An adversarial probe added 28 forms and 137 cases with literal expectations that the native oracle must reproduce before the target runs: a cluster whose first handler does not match and second does; a signal inside a handler that sees no outer cluster; an ERROR inside a handler with no outer handler, refused while the cluster is masked with the binding chain restored; special bindings inside handler clauses and inside handlers; a HANDLER-CASE and a 130-value declining handler inside multiple-value-call producers; a five-clause HANDLER-CASE resolved for every proxy; nested HANDLER-CASEs of the same type; a throw from a handler to a catch inside the handler-bind body and through a cleanup; :NO-ERROR clauses of four and five variables and an indexed HANDLER-CASE with :NO-ERROR; unmatched signals returning NIL; handlers given as a linked function and as a function argument; a handler mutating a captured variable; a HANDLER-CASE as a MULTIPLE-VALUE-BIND source; an ERROR through 40 non-tail frames and a signal after 40 nested declining clusters and after tail chains of 40 and 3,000 steps; and a 130-value normal return through a single-clause HANDLER-CASE. All 548 comparisons agreed on the first execution (524 returns, 24 unhandled) with cold installation reproducing them. (4) A second probe tested the CASE rewrite on user source: three fixnum-key cases and `(case 5 (nil 1) (t 2))` agreed, and `(case nil (nil 1) (t 2))` returned 1 on the target where native CCL returns 2.

Verdict: one defect. The pre-expander applies its CASE rewrite to the whole source rather than only inside the handler expansions, so CASE, which the declared source language does not admit and the validator previously refused, is now compiled with EQ-on-key semantics; a NIL key, which names no keys in Common Lisp, becomes a match on NIL. The retained counterexample is `(lambda (x) (case x (nil 1) (t 2)))` with argument NIL: native 2, target 1. The fix is to gate the CASE, LIST and POP rewrites on the expansion context as the THE rewrite already is, or to refuse user CASE before expansion; CCL's HANDLER-CASE expansion uses only integer and T keys, which the rewrite handles correctly. Observations, not defects: a quoted condition name evaluates to its mask fixnum rather than a symbol wherever it appears; LIST is now admitted in user source with correct semantics; every SIGNAL pays a four-slot frame, a special-binding extent for `%handlers%` and a proxy validation per handler comparison; and the harness's malformed-proxy refusals arrive as codes 4 and 5 rather than the Lisp condition path, the same gap as every other checked refusal. Acceptance is the user's call; I recommend the defect be fixed first.

## Eighty-third Claude audit — conditions R2 source boundary, and per-callee result scratch, at e0ed1978 — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `cccd5d89` (conditions R2, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-b-conditions-r2` at evidence commit `f73815b5`, index hash `c031bf92…` equal to the packet manifest's) and `e0ed1978` (per-callee result scratch, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-b-result-scratch-r1` at `e045e19c`, index hash `c3c97864…` equal to the manifest's). Neither touches the shared backend, loader, reader or stub, which remain the accepted R2 storage unit; neither claims a slot. Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `51de5565`, whose unit directories equal those commits. The ledger is unchanged at 5 accepted and 26 missing.

Conditions R2. It fixes the eighty-second audit's defect. Before invoking CCL's handler macro, the pre-expander now walks every user-supplied subform, the handler-bind clause expressions and body, the handler-case form, clause lambda lists and bodies, with expansion privilege off, then expands the rebuilt form and walks the expansion with privilege on; CASE, LIST and POP join THE in refusing outside that privilege. Because the pre-walk rewrites or refuses every privileged form in user code before the expansion is walked, the second walk cannot admit anything the first refused, including nested handlers and lambda-list defaults, which a flag alone would have missed. Fourteen source refusals cover user CASE with NIL and integer keys, LIST and POP at top level, and CASE, LIST, POP and THE inside protected forms, handler functions, clauses, :NO-ERROR clauses, nested handlers and defaults. Three controls remove the three guards, grant privilege to user bodies, and reproduce my counterexample exactly, requiring native 2 and target 1 from the unguarded compiler. A result-mode witness counts descriptors, allocations and releases on six scenarios without changing the protocol.

Result scratch. A conservative walk of each function's own IR admits an operator allowlist without calls, control transfers, multiple-value consumers or unknown operators, and bounds every VALUES form at four; it walks initializers and lambda-list defaults, and an inner lambda is a separate module with its own proof. A proven-small function enters with dynamic storage off and the fixed four-word scratch whatever its caller's mode, so it creates no descriptor and releases no arena scope, while every delivery decision reads the continuation's mode word directly: a dynamic recipient receives the frame's scratch through the deliver routine's stack-source path, including direct producer delivery with the surviving root, and an ordinary caller is served as before. A wrong proof would degrade to a checked capacity refusal rather than corruption, because a call from such a function prepares an ordinary-mode callee context. An independent pre-emitter witness lists each function's operators and VALUES arities, and the fixture requires its decision to equal the emitted entry choice; 112 of the 587 inherited modules are proven small, and I confirmed the five-value, 130-value and self-calling helpers are not. Seven controls are rejected: always-small, wide VALUES, calls admitted, defaults skipped and initializers skipped by the witness join, inheriting everywhere by the operation counts, and delivery following the local mode by the scalar-nesting scenario. Eighteen scenarios cover zero and four values, retention, branches, mutation, special parameters, unknown initializers and defaults, closures, a cleanup and live rebinding of a small function to a 130-value one. The focused witness shows two nested scalar callees at zero descriptors and one release, down from two and three, with zero arena blocks in both versions.

Replays. (1) Both verifiers passed: source pins, packet bytes, loader identity, native R6/R6a reproduced in each (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the inherited 587-module corpus with 7,332 comparisons and nineteen mutants, the 62-module condition corpus with 848 comparisons, 28 proxy refusals and ten dispatcher mutants, the fourteen scope refusals and three scope controls, the result-mode witness and its rejected blanket-mode counterexample, and for the scratch unit the eighteen storage scenarios (72 comparisons), seven storage controls and the witness join, with cold installation in both. (2) The development records retain the exploratory mode-off mutant that refused a one-valued case first, the corpus adapter that dropped binding overrides before native evaluation, and the delivery mutant that escaped until the scalar-nesting scenario observed the distinct recipient. (3) Against the scratch unit I reran all three probes of the eighty-first and eighty-second audits unchanged: the 67-case storage probe (268 comparisons) and the 27-case zero-arena run (108), whose producers, handlers and callees include proven-small helpers such as the one- to four-value returners, the rest-list and 130-argument takers and eleven handler lambdas delivered directly, through catch, block, cleanup and binding records, into discard descriptors and after tail chains, and the 137-case conditions probe (548 comparisons) with cold installation. All agreed on the first execution, and the compiled refusals of the conditions probe include my counterexample and the other thirteen scope refusals.

Verdict: no defect in either commit; the eighty-second audit's defect is fixed. Observations, not defects: five of the seven proof controls are rejected only by the witness join, which detects divergence between the Lisp rule and its Python copy rather than a wrong execution, so the execution-level evidence for the proof rests on the scenarios and the checked refusal that a wrong proof would produce; the proof excludes every call, so most user functions stay dynamic beneath a producer and the unit states that general demand propagation is still open; the witness's operator list is not retained in the packet, only its decision; and the result-mode witness measures operation counts, not time. Acceptance of both units is the user's call.

## Eighty-fourth Claude audit — conditions R2 and result-scratch integration, and the LL05 qualification, at c9a417ad — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `97326319` (acceptance and integration of conditions R2 and the result-scratch unit on the user's "If I should accept, I do. Integrate and proceed. try to close out LL05.") and `c9a417ad` (the combined S1-LL05-a and S1-LL05-b qualification, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-ll05-r1` at evidence commit `60bfa26a`; the index's packet hash `752ac800…` and the ledger's results hash `e975836a…` equal the files). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `c9a417ad`.

Integration. The integrated backend, loader, reader and stub are byte-identical to the result-scratch unit reviewed in the eighty-third audit. Every before hash equals the file at `fe219e35` and the storage-R2 record's after hash, every after hash equals the file at `97326319`, and the record binds the user's words, the eighty-third audit, both packet manifests, the previous record's hash and the four observations, and states that no slot is accepted. The qualification commit leaves the shared backend, loader, reader and stub unchanged; the ledger moved from 26 missing to 24 missing and 2 unreviewed, with the five accepted records unchanged.

The qualification. It adds implicit call-error signalling to the integrated backend and qualifies the complete B corpus as one unit. Arity, keyword and designator checks no longer throw their fatal code directly: the emitter routes kinds 1, 4 and 16 to a private helper, the resolver and function-cell reader catch their own designator refusal and call the same helper, and the public wrapper's arity check is dropped in favour of the body's. With no handler chain the helper rethrows the old fatal code, so every inherited expectation is unchanged. Otherwise it saves the interrupted stack, result and root state, builds a four-slot frame and its own 48-byte result descriptor above the failing frame, which stays live, allocates a 16-byte condition vector on the heap with a class mask of program-error for arity, program-error with simple-condition for malformed keywords, type-error for a non-function object and undefined-function for T or a symbol without a function, roots it, runs the generated cluster dispatcher on it, and on decline throws the fatal code; any transfer out of a handler passes through its catch-all, which restores the saved state and releases its scope before rethrowing. Keyword validation now runs before parameter specials are published and before rest allocation, so a handler sees the caller's dynamic values, which a premature-publication mutant and a native-first binding-order witness cover. Ten error controls are rejected, including the bounded-scratch transfer that failed under a producer during development. The loader controls are requalified against the current binaries with 36 installation cases including a public-stub closure-SELF and count case, and fourteen loader and stub mutants. A coverage file joins each assertion to its populations, and an ABI binding pins the accepted B decision. Both result records carry all six required roles. The remaining backend text is unchanged from the integrated unit, which I diffed in full.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, loader identity, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the inherited 587-module corpus with 7,332 comparisons and nineteen mutants, the 62-module condition corpus with 848 comparisons, ten dispatcher mutants, fourteen scope refusals and the storage controls, the 46-module call-error corpus with 288 comparisons recompiled byte-identically, its ten error controls, the 36 full-loader cases and fourteen loader mutants, cold installation of every corpus, and the coverage and ABI-binding files reproduced byte for byte. (2) The development record retains eleven failures, including the real bounded-transfer defect and the public-stub mutant that escaped until compiled calls stopped using public entries, and a publication that first expected a local toolchain file, with post-publication verification that nothing else changed. (3) An adversarial probe added 31 forms and 46 cases with literal expectations that native CCL reproduced before the target ran: an arity handler reading the caller's special binding while the failing frame is live; arity errors inside producers caught outside and transferred by throw; a designator error caught inside a producer with five values delivered into a dynamic catch; keyword errors after argument effects, with allow-other-keys true and explicitly false, and an odd keyword list whose handler reads the special the parameter would have bound; undefined-function against type-error for a fixnum, NIL, T, a symbol without a function and a cons, through FUNCALL and MULTIPLE-VALUE-CALL; ERROR resignalling the private condition into an outer handler; SIGNAL of it declining to the fatal arity code; an arity error handled inside a cleanup that runs during a handler's transfer; an arity error after tail chains of 1 and 3,000 steps; 130 values returned from an arity handler; a handler-case as a binding source; 40 nested declining clusters around an arity error; wrong-count calls of a closure from a factory; APPLY with an odd keyword list and with too few and too many arguments; two handlers in one cluster; an arity error inside an explicit signal's handler; MULTIPLE-VALUE-CALL with too many values and an arity error inside a nested dynamic call; a special parameter's function called with no arguments; and three consecutive errors with state restored between them. My first attempt used a literal lambda called with the wrong count, which the native compiler rejects at compile time, so it was replaced by a factory. All 184 comparisons agreed on the first execution after that (180 returns, 4 arity) and cold installation reproduced them. (4) A separate probe applied a function to a non-list final argument and to a dotted list: native CCL reaches a type-error handler and returns, while the target raises the checked type refusal.

Verdict: no defect against either assertion. S1-LL05-a's clause that bad designators and arities follow the Lisp condition path holds whenever a handler chain exists; with none, the helper preserves the checked fatal code, which the unit declares as the debugger boundary rather than a debugger. S1-LL05-b's stubs, substitutions, closure self, overflow and bounded tail chains with preserved extents are covered by the requalified loader controls and the inherited corpus. Observations, not defects: APPLY's proper-list validation is executed but its failure is a checked type refusal, not a Lisp condition, which the scope lists under later checked-error work, and the retained counterexample above shows the divergence from native; the condition objects are private 16-byte vectors with a class mask, not CLOS instances, so any code that inspects a condition beyond its class is outside this qualification; every implicit error allocates on the heap and a signal costs a frame, a binding extent and a proxy validation per comparison; and the helper's saved-state restore covers stack, result and root fields but relies on the failing function's own exception path for its binding and control records, which the probes exercised. Acceptance of both LL05 records is the user's call.

## Eighty-fifth Claude audit — LL05 acceptance and integration, and the S1-LL10-a constants qualification, at d025089c — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed six Codex commits: `7867d132` (acceptance of S1-LL05-a and S1-LL05-b and the constants plan), `c0804819` (integration of the call-error backend), and `a72aa1bd`, `55138810`, `b0c012fe` and `d025089c` (the constants encoder, identity linker, snapshot transport and the complete S1-LL10-a qualification, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-ll10-r1` at evidence commit `0a6c7413`, whose packet hash `bcd0790c…` and results hash `e450087e…` equal the index and ledger). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `d025089c`.

Acceptance and integration. The acceptance record binds the user's "save that as a document for later. then process claude's response, accept and integrate.", my audit commit `e629f7d3` and the hash of the review file at that commit, the results file hash, both LL05 records with their contract hashes, and my four exclusions verbatim; the ledger's accepted count became seven with the combined accepted-results file bound by hash. The integrated backend is byte-identical to the call-error unit reviewed in the eighty-fourth audit, the loader, reader and stub are unchanged, and every before hash equals the file at `7867d132` and the result-scratch record's after hash while every after hash equals the file at `c0804819`. The four constants commits leave the shared backend, loader, reader and stub unchanged; the ledger is 7 accepted, 23 missing and 1 unreviewed.

The constants unit. It is isolated and claims no acceptance. Its compiler derives an isolated backend from the LL05 backend, pinned by hash, through textual anchors and an appended extension: the function object grows from 24 to 32 bytes with a tagged pool word at raw offset 24, a per-function pool is a D1 simple-vector of the function's literals collected from the front end's IR by identity with child pools appended, closures and temporary callables copy the pool pointer at construction and relocation, and each literal load validates the rooted SELF's header and the pool vector's header before indexing; constant-free code gains nothing. A form-based compiler entry preserves reader identities so cycles, sharing and NaN payloads never pass through printed source, and the walkers skip literal graphs. An exporter writes an identity graph over all pools at once, so an object shared by several functions is one object. A byte encoder driven by the layout contract produces pointer-free object bytes for fixnums, bignum limbs, floats as raw bits, characters, strings as character codes and every D1 specialized vector; a linker allocates every object before resolving references, so forward references and cycles need no recursion; and a snapshot transport captures an owned interval, validates a complete snapshot against a trusted digest, relocates exact object-start pointers and owner symbols, and publishes with one write. The origin Worker runs the corpus at 1 MiB, mutates a literal through generated code, then captures; two fresh Workers restore at 2 MiB and 2 GiB from bytes only, inspect the cold function's pool before installation and observe the mutation. Signalling an immediate format string is now refused, closing a scope regression the development found.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: source pins, packet bytes, native R6/R6a reproduced (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the 87-module corpus and header probe recompiled byte-identically with 233 native-derived comparisons across the three placements, the four recompiled compiler mutants and seven image mutants rejected, the encoder, linker and snapshot verifications with their controls, the inherited 587-module, 62-module and 46-module corpora with independent allocation expectations for the larger objects, the 36 loader cases and fourteen loader mutants, and 2,340 deterministic files. (2) The development record retains the eleven failures, including the real scope regression, the stale allocation expectations, the tail probe that reserved too many result words, and the first publication rejected for a missing options role. (3) An adversarial probe added 19 zero-argument cases to a scratch copy of the corpus, executed at all three placements through the unit's own native-derived oracle: two distinct equal strings returned and compared with EQ, the same string compared with itself, a cycle's cdr identity, a child closure comparing its parent's literal with a literal cons's car, a literal in an optional default, APPLY of a literal lambda to a literal list, a bignum and a double inside literal conses, a literal cons mutated by one function and observed by another at every placement, forty distinct strings consed at run time, two LABELS functions with their own pools, a grandchild closure comparing a captured literal with its pool entry, a 3,000-cons literal walked by a 3,000-step local tail chain and returned whole, a general vector of NIL, T and a fixnum, a character in a literal list, and EQ of two textually equal bignums. Two first attempts were my fixture errors: a cross-module call, which this corpus's native side cannot evaluate, and returned closures, which the native side does not invoke as the target harness does, so the probe copy now invokes them too. All 57 probe comparisons agreed at the origin and both restored placements, alongside the 233 corpus comparisons, with 126 header-probe checks and 113 cold installations per restore.

Verdict: no defect against the S1-LL10-a assertion. Golden fixtures for every listed family preserve identity, sharing, widths and subtags through generated code and round-trip the unexecuted cold function's pool, and the schema is checked by a target-executed header probe rather than a text extractor. Observations, not defects: all four compiler mutants are rejected at the same first executed case with the same designator refusal, so those controls show rejection but do not discriminate the emitter faults; cross-function identity of a shared literal is materialized once by the graph writer but has no native oracle in this fixture, because its native side runs no cross-module calls; symbols inside literal data need an owner-registry entry, so a keyword inside a quoted list is refused, which the scope declares; the restore-mode oracle rewrites every expected cons whose car is 7 to 11 to compose the persistence mutation, a fragility any future case with such a literal would trip; every literal load pays two header validations; and mutating a literal is a persistence probe that the unit states is not a portable claim, which is right, since Common Lisp leaves it undefined. Acceptance is the user's call.

## Eighty-seventh Claude audit — LL10 acceptance and integration, and repeated keyword aliases, at c4a9ff58 — 17 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed two Codex commits: `2d26e16a` (acceptance and integration of S1-LL10-a on the user's "accept and integrate") and `c4a9ff58` (repeated keyword aliases, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-17-stage1-b-repeated-keywords-r1` at evidence commit `394d8df9`, whose packet hash `aff1aab7…` equals the index's). Executed sources were replayed from a detached worktree at `c4a9ff58`.

Acceptance and integration. The acceptance record binds the user's words, my audit commit `3b6a2655` and the hash of the review file at that commit, the results file hash, the record's contract hash, and the exclusions of the eighty-fifth and eighty-sixth audits verbatim; the ledger is 8 accepted, 23 missing and 0 unreviewed with the results file bound by hash. The integrated backend equals the constants unit's generated compiler byte for byte, the loader carries the constants profile and equals the unit's, the reader and stub are unchanged, and every before hash equals the file at `3b6a2655` and the LL05 record's after hash while every after hash equals the file at `2d26e16a`. The alias commit leaves the shared backend, loader, reader and stub unchanged; the ledger is unchanged by it.

The behaviour. Native CCL admits distinct keyword parameters that share a keyword name, binds only the first such formal from the first matching argument pair, and lets later aliases evaluate their defaults in lambda-list order with false supplied-p flags. I confirmed this on the pristine baseline kernel and image, interpreted and compiled: the lambda list `(&key ((:x a) 1) ((:x b) 2))` called with `:x 9` and with `:x 9 :x 7` returns 9 and 2; three chained aliases called with `:x 9 :x 7` return 9, T, 9, NIL, 9, NIL; and two aliases of `:allow-other-keys` called with an unknown key, `:allow-other-keys t` and then `nil` return T, T, 2, NIL. The proposal changes two anchored sites in the accepted compiler generator: the validator admits duplicate keyword names while still refusing duplicate variables, and the keyword scan emits the supplied-value match only for the first formal bearing each name, so later aliases are never marked supplied and take their defaults through the unchanged staging and binding code; unknown-key detection is unaffected because the first alias still marks the name known, and unique names emit exactly the code they did before. Twenty-two native-derived forms cover absent, NIL and repeated actuals, three and non-adjacent aliases, implicit and explicit names, dependent and effectful defaults, direct, indirect, APPLY, literal, multiple-value and escaped-closure calls, special parameters and unwind, and repeated `:allow-other-keys`, unknown keys and odd lists, each at three placements within the constants corpus. Three recompiled mutants, binding every alias, binding the last alias and letting the last actual win, fail their focused native oracles, and restoring the old validator refuses the first new form.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed: packet hashes, 114 source pins, the native qualification replayed (162 unchanged FASLs, 164 restored, 21,843 native tests), the proposed and mutant compilers recompiled, the inherited corpus rerun with all 1,178 positive files byte-identical to accepted LL10, and 3,336 deterministic files identical to the packet. (2) An adversarial probe added 12 forms through the unit's own runner, each at three placements with native CCL supplying every expectation: a special first alias with a lexical second whose default reads it; a default that tests the first alias's supplied-p flag, called with and without the key; direct multiple-value delivery of the key pairs; an alias after a different key with keys given out of order; APPLY over a consed key list with a rest parameter; an unknown key followed by the alias and `:allow-other-keys t`; an odd list after the alias reaching the program-error handler; a LABELS function passing its second alias back to itself; an escaping closure capturing both aliases; four aliases with effectful defaults showing three defaults run in order; NIL supplied to the first alias; and another key repeated between two repeated aliases. Three first attempts were my fixture errors: a non-linkable `(function values)`, and a rest list containing keywords returned as a value, which the export refuses for want of owner registration, the declared limit. All 36 probe comparisons agreed, within 347 corpus comparisons, and the four controls were rejected again over the extended corpus.

Verdict: no defect. Observation, not a defect: the standard's description of keyword parameter processing, taken literally, binds every parameter whose name matches a supplied pair, which would give 9 and 9 here; native CCL gives 9 and 2, and this project's criterion is native CCL, so the proposal matches the oracle it is measured against, but the behaviour is a CCL characteristic rather than an ANSI guarantee and portable code should not rely on it. Acceptance is the user's call.


## Eighty-eighth Claude audit — generated LL19 control and recovery at 3eddbe69 — 19 September 2026

The proposal. The generator derives from the accepted repeated-keyword backend through the APPLY prerequisite and adds, by anchored replacement: condition instances in D1 standard-instance and slot-vector layouts allocated from the owned area and looked up through a sealed twelve-row registry whose slot order and defaults every native compilation exports from pristine U1 and the harness checks row by row; generated readers for datum, expected type and cell name gated by ancestry mask; restart-case and restart-bind expanded to owner-registered restart objects under `%restarts%`, with a fresh catch tag per restart-case, first-match lookup by object or name, dynamic retirement and control-error on a missing or expired restart; a debugger hook invoked only in ordinary error-service mode with itself bound to NIL, its depth saved and restored around any transfer out of the hook; checked cons, SVREF, unbound-special, missing-catch and improper-APPLY paths entering the condition path with native CCL's classes and datums, the no-handler cons case keeping the accepted legacy tag; a stack guard for all three stacks that signals STORAGE-CONDITION inside a configured reserve and re-arms on transfer, with hard exhaustion and damaged bounds remaining fatal; a separate 16-byte CSP entry per control record pushed before and popped in both exit arms; and an explicit poll that runs the collector service under any interrupt level, claims only the interrupt bit, and re-enables through an unwind-protect around the level binding. The refusal of user CASE forms matches the accepted conditions corpus's scope refusals, so nothing accepted is narrowed.

Replays. A first attempt used `typep`, which the scope refuses by design. All 16 comparisons agreed.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) the debugger depth is kept in the TCR word the schema names `scratch1` and the reserve-in-use flag in `scratch0`, both documented as runtime-private scratch words; no other Stage 1 emitter touches them, so nothing clobbers them today, but integration should give both named fields in the TCR schema; (b) a single reserve flag serves VSP, TSP and CSP, so while one stack's reserve is in use the soft check on the other two is suspended until the transfer re-arms it, a deviation from native CCL's per-stack guards worth recording with the runtime obligations; (c) every executed fatal diagnostic comes from bootstrap mode, and no case reaches the declined or unhandled path with the ordinary error service active, so the label the assessment distinguishes is never produced; a positive case belongs with the debugger boundary before it is relied on. ## Ninetieth Claude audit — generated LL17 bindings and binding-vector growth at c9c94639 — 19 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Reviewed Codex's `c9c94639` (S1-LL17-a, packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-19-stage1-binding-vector-r1` at evidence commit `b69d478f`, packet hash `f4c6131d…` equal to the index entry, envelope `b071de69…` and combined pending results `dd3a476d…` equal to their files, published `NOT_REVIEWED`). The shared compiler, loader and upstream kernel are unchanged by the commit; the review worktree was detached at `c9c94639`. Codex's later `6d919904` records only the follow-up acceptance and touches no LL17 file.

The proposal. The generator derives from the accepted LL19 compiler by anchored replacement: a private pre-expansion maps `SYMBOL-VALUE` and `SET` at head position to checked intrinsics unless an enclosing `FLET` body or `LABELS` shadows the name, with `FLET` definition bodies correctly outside their own shadow; the intrinsics root their evaluated operands in the frame, return `NIL`, `T` and themselves on read, refuse `SET` of `NIL`, `T` and any symbol whose constant bit is set through the accepted implicit-error path, and otherwise read through the Lisp unbound path or write through the current binding slot with global fallback. The runtime replaces the accepted `$dynamic_slot`, `$special_location` and `$special_read`: the binding index is validated separately, index zero and out-of-range indices fall back to the global cell on read and `SET`, and creating a binding at an index beyond the capacity grows the vector geometrically from 16 to at most 2^24-1 elements as a D1 simple vector, with all extents in i64, bounds against the allocation base, limit and memory size, an alias check against the existing vector, a full copy, fill of new slots with the native no-binding marker, and publication of the allocation cursor, element pointer and capacity only at the end with no call or poll inside. Binding records keep symbol, index and old value, so `$unbind_to` recomputes the slot from the current vector. The native oracle is pinned to `l0-symbol.lisp`, `arm-symbol.lisp`, `arm-exceptions.c` and `lispequ.lisp`; the constant bit tested at raw bit 3 is native `$sym_vbit_const` 1 as a fixnum, and native `SET` on a constant reports `SIMPLE-ERROR`, which the corpus expects.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 176 source pins, proposal derivation, native references into the accepted 1A packet, the native qualification (5 operator tables, 17 module profiles, 21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs, 17 negative controls), the positive run with 40 cases and 160 comparisons, two real Worker suspensions and eight vector evacuations, 24 owner and resource cases with 96 comparisons, twelve recompiled mutants each reaching execution and failing an assertion, the six publication controls, the inherited B, condition and call-error corpora and the loader cases, and 3,842 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's except for the packet hash, which Codex computed before publication added the inventory and role controls. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) A probe through the unit's runner added five cases at four placements with native CCL supplying every expectation: binding the higher-index symbol first so one growth serves both, an `FLET` whose definition body calls the global `SYMBOL-VALUE` while the body calls the local one, `SET` of a bound special observed in a cleanup after a throw, and the corpus's own `v_many`, which is compiled but never executed. All 20 comparisons agreed. `(setf (symbol-value s) v)` and `#'symbol-value` are refused at compile time as unsupported source, so neither is silently miscompiled.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) `v_many` is defined in the corpus sources and compiled but has no case, so the packet's 29 modules include one that is never executed; it belongs in the corpus or out of it; (b) `$unbind_to` still resolves slots through `$dynamic_slot`, which can now allocate; no unbind can reach that path because capacity never shrinks and a failed growth pushes no record, but a non-growing lookup would make the invariant explicit; (c) `setf` of `symbol-value` and function objects for the two names are outside the claimed scope and refuse; the `setf` form is the idiomatic way to write a special, so a later unit will want it. Acceptance is the user's call.

## Ninety-first Claude audit — LL17 integration at 7a0f23e1 and the collector core at 500cee93 — 19 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Two Codex commits since audit 90. (1) `7a0f23e1` accepts and integrates LL17 on the user's "Accept, integrate and proceed": the integrated backend hash `380bd2b8…` equals the retained LL17 proposal and its generator, the loader, reader and stub are unchanged at their LL19 hashes, the three audit-90 observations are carried in the integration record, and the ledger moves to 10 accepted, 21 missing, 0 unreviewed. (2) `500cee93` is an auxiliary collector-core proposal (packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-19-stage1-collector-core-r1` at evidence commit `f4bb0f9f`, packet hash `3f105584…` equal to the index entry, catalog and source-index hashes equal to their files, published `NOT_REVIEWED` with no LL18 credit). The shared compiler, loader and upstream kernel are unchanged by it; the review worktree was detached at `500cee93`.

The proposal. A freestanding C service compiled to Wasm copies live D1 conses and an allowlisted set of node and raw object kinds between owner-supplied disjoint semispaces. It inventories every object start by walking the allocation area before following any root, so a tagged interior word is never promoted to an object; forwards through a binary search over that inventory with tag-versus-shape checks; refuses references into to-space; scans the root chain from `root_head` using the actual descriptor shapes, including the indirect result descriptor with its inline or arena slot buffer, with visited-identity cycle detection rather than an address-order assumption; treats the active multiple-value words, the binding vector as a raw interior root (moved as a vector when heap-resident, element-rooted when bootstrap), and owner-registered extra slots; handles stack-resident callables as non-moving with their environment and pool as roots; stages every root update until the whole graph is copied and validated, so a refusal leaves source space, roots and TCR publication unchanged; and publishes the new base, cursor and limit last. The compiler proposal, derived from the integrated LL17 backend by three anchored replacements, makes unbinding use a non-growing lookup, reloads captured-cell access through the continuation's rooted SELF at context word 40 instead of a Wasm local cached at entry, and reads local self-calls from that same word. Both cached-address paths were exposed by actual movement and are retained as compiled controls.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 189 source pins, proposal derivation, pinned toolchain hashes, native references into the accepted 1A packet, the native qualification (21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs, 17 negative controls), the collector rebuilt from source, 75 core checks including twenty random cyclic graphs and nine atomic refusals, 22 generated modules with 14 native cases, 56 comparisons and 34 collections, eight C mutants each failing a named independent assertion, the growing-unbind control and the two stale-address compiler controls, the inherited B, condition and call-error corpora, and 2,499 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) A probe through the unit's runner added four cases at four placements with eight collections and native CCL supplying every expectation: a captured variable reassigned after movement and read through the closure; a closure returned from a closure and called after movement; a heap-resident binding vector moved and read through `SYMBOL-VALUE`; and a condition object moved while its handler runs, then read through `TYPE-ERROR-DATUM`. All 16 comparisons agreed. A fifth case polling inside a `RESTART-CASE` body refused the collection with code 2: the restart object's header kind is outside the allowlist, so the service refuses atomically rather than moving it.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) live restart objects refuse collection, so no poll inside a `RESTART-CASE` extent can collect until the allowlist admits that kind with a scanner; the README states the allowlist is narrower than CCL's heap kinds, and this names the first accepted kind it excludes; (b) tagged temporaries held in Wasm locals across a poll remain unprotected by design and are deferred to LL06, which is the main reason this unit carries no LL18 credit; (c) every captured-cell access now reloads through the context word, which is correct but costs a load chain per access; the README already notes liveness analysis as later work. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user's call.

The proposal. Three compiler corrections by anchored replacement over the integrated backend, each exposed by actual movement during development and each retained as a compiled control: `EQ` stages both operands in a root frame and compares the reloaded words, where the old emitter held the first operand in a Wasm local across a collecting second operand; captured-variable binding evaluates the value before loading the heap cell address, with no call, allocation or poll between that load and the store, where the old ordering loaded the address first; and the implicit-error service keeps its condition in two formerly padding words of its existing 32-byte frame, now declared as roots five and six, and reloads it for `SIGNAL` and the debugger, where the old code reused a Wasm local after a declining handler could collect. Lexical `SETQ` already staged its value and now benefits from the same store ordering; `LET` stages parallel initial values in a root frame; `CONS`, `SVREF`, symbol access and calls already root their operands. I found no other emitter that inlines two tagged operands without a frame. The scanner admits the six-slot istruct kind 130 that the accepted restart constructor emits, scanning six node fields and not the alignment word, refusing other istruct counts, and treats canonical `T` as a pinned identity like `NIL` rather than a stack-temporary callable, which the low fixture's numeric ranges had made it resemble.

Replays. (2) A probe through the unit's runner added four cases at four placements with eight collections and native CCL supplying every expectation: a parallel `LET` of two specials whose second initial value collects while the first is staged; a collection in a `HANDLER-CASE` body before the error it catches; two pending multiple values, both conses, carried through a cleanup that collects; and a collection inside a `RESTART-CASE` body followed by an error whose handler invokes the restart with the datum. All 16 comparisons agreed. A fifth case using an `&aux` parameter is refused as unsupported source; that refusal predates this unit.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) `&aux` parameters are a pre-existing source refusal of the B subset and are not claimed here; (b) the fixture's canonical `NIL` and `T` values lie inside the low stack's numeric range, which is what made `T` look like a stack callable; the README already says production region ownership must reserve their real objects outside all stacks, and that reservation should be an explicit owner obligation before any production placement; (c) `EQ` now costs a root frame per comparison; the README defers a no-safepoint proof that could remove unnecessary frames. ## Ninety-third Claude audit — live-restart integration at d30abc47 and collector owner admission at 62c26b37 — 19 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Two Codex commits since audit 92. (1) `d30abc47` accepts and integrates the live-restart follow-up: the integrated backend hash `f12ca4fe…` and runtime collector source hash `8af4e901…` equal the retained collector-live proposal files, the loader, reader and stub are unchanged at their LL19 hashes, and the three audit-92 observations are carried in the integration record. It changes no shared file: the compiler, collector source and collector binary it runs are asserted byte-equal to the integrated ones. The review worktree was detached at `62c26b37`.

The proposal. A single-Worker owner module admits a declared layout before any collection: a copying, single-worker, non-EGC profile; disjoint eight-byte-aligned backed regions for the TCR, three Lisp stacks, C stack, scratch, root list, external slots, bootstrap bindings, pinned image regions and two semispaces; the compiled C stack extent matching the collector's exported stack pointer; four required external root families with unique aligned slots inside the external region; and the real NIL and T objects resident in pinned image regions outside every stack, which is the obligation audit 92 asked to make executable. Image regions are inventoried object by object with the same kind allowlist as the collector, and every tagged field becomes a root slot; raw payload, padding and scalar metadata do not. The TCR's sole tagged-root field, `next_method_context` at offset 188, is added as a root and the runner checks that the v2 schema names no other. Collection runs only inside a synchronous, non-nested owner boundary. `ensure` collects first, then, if space is still short, grows memory by a fresh semispace pair sized to the larger of twice the current capacity and live plus request, refreshes host views, and evacuates into the new pair; engine and policy refusals are explicit and a failed `ensure` leaves live objects and current roots valid. The composition then runs two functions compiled by the integrated backend, a cons constructor and a two-value reader, twelve constructions and thirteen reads across collections and one growth, with native CCL supplying the expectations.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 206 source pins, the parent packet's compiler, collector source, binary, qualification and toolchain references by hash, the fresh execution, and all 41 deterministic files identical to the packet's manifest; the record equals Codex's. The 41 owner checks cover sole-path survival through each root family, a second collection, immutability of the admitted manifest, reclaim before growth, live growth with view refresh, actual engine growth to 2 GiB plus one page, unsigned heap addresses above 2 GiB, twenty-two owner, engine and resource refusals with region snapshots proving preservation, growth exhaustion with live objects intact, and the generated composition; eight single-site owner mutants each fail at a distinct named diagnostic. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) Four owner scenarios I wrote against the integrated collector binary passed: a heap-resident binding vector with a live element plus image, TCR and host roots into the heap carried through an `ensure`-driven growth into a new pair; a second growth from the grown pair with a live chain intact and allocation resuming in the newest pair; a request larger than twice the current capacity; and a bootstrap binding vector whose heap element moves while the vector itself stays pinned.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) growth appends a new pair at the end of memory and retires the old spaces without reuse, so the memory high-water mark only rises; the README says so, and a production allocator will need a reuse policy; (b) `ensure` may have collected or grown before refusing, which the README states as preservation rather than rollback; callers must not assume a failed `ensure` left the heap layout unchanged; (c) the generated composition reaches the owner only at the public boundary with arguments already published, so no internal allocation slow path has been exercised, and the plan's next step names exactly that. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user's call.

## Ninety-seventh Claude audit — LL18-a integration at 16bb8557 and S1-LL06-a qualification at 85b2894a — 19 September 2026

(2) `85b2894a` executes inventory slot S1-LL06-a (packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-19-stage1-temporaries-r1` at evidence commit `9ccbcfaf`, clean tree; packet hash `88f5c76b…`, results envelope `ecb441f7…`, combined results `6ba21b44…`, catalog `0d1b3c67…` and source index `2c5c29f8…` all equal to the index; inventory status EXECUTED, disposition NOT_REVIEWED; ledger 11 accepted, 19 missing, one unreviewed; project gate and management check PASS at the commit). It changes no shared file; the review worktree was detached at `85b2894a`.

The proposal. Derived from the integrated compiler by anchored replacement plus an appended emitter file; the generated text equals the packet's compiler. The source validator gains a TAGBODY branch that admits symbol and fixnum tags, refuses duplicates, scopes the tag list to the TAGBODY body and clears it inside every nested lambda, and a GO branch that requires one argument naming a visible tag; PROG2 joins the admitted heads. The backend delegates U1's local TAGBODY and local GO operators. A TAGBODY collects its forms into segments split at tag labels, refuses if any declared tag lacks a label, and emits a loop whose body is the reviewed addressed-exit frame: inside it every segment whose index is at least the program counter runs in order, the counter is then set to the sentinel and NIL produced, and the loop exits on the sentinel. GO looks up its tag by IR identity in the enclosing frames' tag map, stores the destination index in the untagged counter local, zeroes the target record's value count, marks the unwind state and throws the addressed exit, exactly as RETURN-FROM does without the value save; the exit frame restores top, roots, value stack and result state and reloads zero values, and an inner control frame that does not match restores its own state and rethrows. The frame binds the emitter out of tail position, so no tail transfer occurs inside a TAGBODY. I confirmed that the loop re-establishes the frame at the same stack top on each iteration, that nested TAGBODYs reset their own counters, and that a counter is never a root.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 284 source pins including the integrated compiler, collector, owner, the LL18 integration record and the native front-end sources, the LL18 parent references by hash, the toolchain hashes, the native qualification of the exact proposal (21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs), the full runner with refusals, mutants and inherited recompilation, and 1,339 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Assessment counts: 30 native cases checked before target compilation, 36 modules, 120 comparisons over two placements with and without collection, 300 collections at the generated service entry, 1,530 control, 870 binding and 1,624 ownership inspections, four fatal-exit diagnostics, eight source refusals, eight recompiled mutants each reaching a named runtime assertion, six publication controls, twelve role omissions rejected, and 152 inherited WAT and Wasm files byte-identical under the new compiler. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) Ten probes through the unit's harness, each run below and above 2 GiB with and without collection: GO out of a BLOCK through an UNWIND-PROTECT cleanup; RETURN-FROM and THROW leaving a TAGBODY through cleanups; GO from a HANDLER-CASE clause; a loop of GOs under pending MULTIPLE-VALUE-PROG1 values; a GO through two TAGBODYs under a pending PROG1 value; GO from a LET* initform after an earlier binding; GO from a call argument after a staged argument; a special variable reassigned from a moving allocation on each iteration; and a backward GO issued from a cleanup form that runs three times. All 40 comparisons agree with native CCL, and the native oracle accepted every case first.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) GO from a nested function, including HANDLER-BIND handlers and RESTART-CASE clauses, is refused at admission even though native CCL supports the closed transfer through a catch frame; the scope states the exclusion, and the LL12-a nonlocal-transfer slot should say whether closed GO belongs to it; (b) every loop iteration reserves, guards and pushes a control record and every GO raises an exception, so an n-iteration loop performs n control-record cycles; the scope makes no speed claim; (c) the backend's tag map is not cleared when it emits a nested function, so correctness of closed GO refusal rests on the source validator alone; an admission of closed GO later must reset the backend map as the validator resets its own. Acceptance of the slot is the user’s call.

## Ninety-ninth Claude audit — LL06-a integration at a4efeff7 and closed-GO proposal at de589073 — 19 September 2026

Recorded by Claude directly. Two Codex commits since audit 98. (1) `a4efeff7` accepts and integrates S1-LL06-a: the compiler (`579bd157…`) equals the reviewed temporaries packet compiler by hash; the collector, owner, loader, reader, allocation service and stub are unchanged at their recorded hashes; the acceptance record binds the review file at `8d8dba0a` by hash (`2ad64e9e…`) and the integration record binds the acceptance record; both carry the audit-97 observations, with the withdrawn tag-map observation recorded as withdrawn; the acceptance packet and combined results hash as indexed and the ledger reads 12 accepted, 19 missing, none unreviewed. (2) `de589073` is an auxiliary compiler proposal (packet `ccl-evidence/2026-09-19-stage1-closure-transfers-r1` at evidence commit `d3adf0bd`, clean tree; packet hash `9043f941…`, catalog `fc94aa8d…` and source index `efb9cd5a…` equal to the index; published NOT_REVIEWED; no LL12 credit). It changes no shared file; the review worktree was detached at `de589073`. The evidence index and repository records were re-serialized with sorted keys in this commit, so their diffs are large; a semantic comparison shows exactly one auxiliary record added and nothing removed or changed.

The proposal. Derived from the integrated LL06 compiler by anchored replacement plus an appended emitter file; the generated text equals the packet's compiler. Three changes. First, the source validator no longer clears the tag scope inside a nested lambda, so a GO in a nested function reaches U1, whose front end lowers a closed tag into a LET* of a fresh cons catch tag and an index variable, a CATCH around the local TAGBODY, a THROW of the index from the nested function, and an outer single-tag loop that redispatches the index through local GOs; every operator in that lowering is already admitted, the backend's per-function tag map still starts empty, and a local GO that ever crossed a function would refuse by identity. Second, a GO whose tag entry carries a loop label becomes a store of the segment index and a Wasm branch. Third, a TAGBODY is branch-lowered only when an IR proof holds: every body form is a tag label, a declaration body, a PROGN of such forms, an IF whose test contains no local GO and whose arms are such forms, a local GO to a tag of this same TAGBODY, or any other form containing no local GO at all; literal data and separately emitted function bodies are not traversed. A branch-lowered TAGBODY is a plain Wasm loop of segments with no control record, no stack reservation and no exit exception, exiting by falling off the loop body; every other TAGBODY keeps the reviewed addressed-exit implementation. I confirmed that the IF emitter establishes no frame state around its arms, that a branch is only reached from positions with no pending emitter frame, and that the redispatch loop of a closed TAGBODY, whose IF test contains the CATCH, always takes the unwind path while its inner dispatch loop may branch.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 299 source pins including the integrated compiler and the LL06 integration record, the LL06 parent references by hash, the toolchain hashes, the native qualification of the exact proposal (21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs), the full runner with refusals, mutants, shape checks and inherited recompilation, and 1,301 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Assessment counts: 30 native-first cases, 44 modules, 120 comparisons over two placements with and without collection, 130 collections, the unchanged LL06 corpus at 120 comparisons and 300 collections, nine instruction-shape checks with no control push or exit throw in the four branch modules and a required unwind frame in five others, 40 inherited non-loop WAT and Wasm files byte-identical, seven recompiled mutants each reaching a named failure, six source refusals and eight publication controls. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) Seven probes through the unit's harness, each run below and above 2 GiB with and without collection: a branch loop whose statement binds a lexical from a moving allocation and conses an accumulator; a branch loop binding a special each iteration; RETURN-FROM leaving a branch loop through an UNWIND-PROTECT cleanup; a closed GO thrown from a closure held in a special variable and called from a separate module, through that special extent; a closed GO crossing a CATCH and a HANDLER-CASE; a branch-eligible inner loop nested in a closed-tag TAGBODY whose closure GO targets the outer tag; and a hundred-iteration closed loop that collects on every iteration. All 28 comparisons agree with native CCL, and the native oracle accepted every case first. The generated shapes match the proof: the first two are branch loops with no control push, the cross-module case branch-lowers its inner dispatch loop under an unwinding redispatch, and the nested case takes the unwind path because its else arm targets the outer tag.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) a closed-tag TAGBODY costs a catch control frame plus an exit frame for the redispatch loop, and every closed GO is a THROW resolved through the catch search, so closed transfers are not cheaper than local unwinding transfers; the scope makes no speed claim and should say this explicitly; (b) the proof is all-or-nothing per TAGBODY, so one GO to a foreign tag in statement position sends every GO of that TAGBODY to the unwind path even though its own backedges could branch; a per-GO decision would be a later refinement; (c) re-serializing the evidence index and repository records inside a content commit produced a seven-thousand-line diff for one added record; keeping serialization stable, or re-serializing in a separate commit, would keep those diffs reviewable. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user’s call.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 321 source pins including the integrated compiler, the closed-GO integration record and the native arglist and backtrace sources, the closed-GO parent references by hash, the toolchain hashes, the native qualification of the exact proposal (21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs), the full runner with compiler and runtime mutants, snapshot controls and default-mode compatibility, and 614 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Assessment counts: sixteen generated modules; three fresh Workers, one origin at 1 MiB and two snapshot restores at 2 MiB and 2 GiB with keywords at different addresses; 48 metadata records read before any code is installed; 33 native signature comparisons; 29 generated invocations including shared and distinct mutable cells checked against the native sequence and restored closures mutated after the former stack is overwritten; twelve refusals of truncated, missing-arity, missing-debug and wrong-pool function objects; installation leaving every owned heap byte unchanged; seven recompiled compiler mutants with distinct oracles; two runtime faults; six malformed snapshots refused before publication; eleven publication controls and twelve role omissions rejected. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) A probe drove the disposable U1 compiler with the proposal on eight shapes outside the corpus and compared each arity record with native CCL's own function-args and key vector in the same process: three required with two optional including a supplied-p variable, rest without keys, two keys with a supplied-p variable, empty keys with allow-other-keys, rest with an aliased key and a plain key under allow-other-keys, a three-level closure whose grandchild captures four variables, and a closure capturing an uninterned variable. All seven admitted shapes agree with native, the grandchild's debug record lists four captures with slot indices in environment order, the uninterned capture carries a NIL package, and all ten generated modules assemble. A key named by a non-keyword symbol is refused at source admission, which is the existing keyword-admission scope and not a metadata path.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) only closure constructors install the metadata words from generated code; top-level function objects receive theirs from the owner's materialization, which this harness performs by copying the pool's first two elements, so the production materializer must do the same or every entry will refuse with code 4; that obligation belongs with the LL14 loader work and should be stated in the scope; (b) entry validation is a per-call cost in metadata mode and checks the fixed counts but not the key vector or the debug record contents, as the scope states; no timing claim is made; (c) non-keyword key names are refused at admission, so an arity record never carries one; the scope's key-vector claim should state that limit, and debug capture order is environment-slot order rather than source order, which the slot indices make explicit. Acceptance of the slot is the user’s call.

Integration checks. The compiler at `0bba1054` hashes `a303b865…`, equal to the retained LL12-a proposal file and to the recorded after-hash; the before-hash `7fc03d62…` is the closed-GO integration; the diff is confined to that file and is the reviewed metadata code.
