The acceptance record binds review commit `aaf91545` and review hash `12ed1330…`, which the review file hashes to at that commit; the integration record binds the acceptance record hash `9e2b3dc0…` and the previous integration `0c09e119…`, both verified, and carries the three audit-100 observations into runtime-obligations.md. The evidence repository moved to `cd76afcb` and then `4328fdc7`, clean; the catalog, both source-index snapshots, the acceptance packet, accepted-results, combined through-LL12 results, the LL11 packet, its results envelope, the combined through-binding-installation results and the inventory at `1c1a3468` all hash as indexed.

LL11-a fixture. A synchronous single-Worker installer takes a manifest, the owner's expected binding list, a catalog and a byte provider. Before any write it checks the generation and predecessor hash chain, the boot/runtime phase, that manifest bindings equal the expected list as a set of package-and-name pairs with no duplicates, that manifest module rows equal catalog rows by name, that slots are fresh, unreserved, below the registry capacity and empty in both tables and the registry, that the bytes validate under the accepted loader and their independently decoded function-body extents equal the catalog, that each named function object has the six-word header, the claimed module's code word, version 4, a pool whose first two elements equal the object's arity and debug words, an arity record equal to the catalog and native-derived arity with keyword identities from the owner's table, a debug record with the declared capture count, and cells tagged and in extent; and that each binding's previous value equals the live symbol cell. Registry rows and symbol cells are journaled; on any failure the journal is replayed and staged table slots nulled. Old loaders and modules are retained. Native CCL supplies eight signatures and the behavior of real symbols in two packages across two rebindings; Python re-decodes body extents from the binary sections without the installer.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 334 source pins including the unchanged compiler, the LL12 integration record and parent references, toolchain hashes, native R6/R6a reused by exact compiler and runtime hash, the full runner with the seven installer mutants and the assessment, and 41 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Assessment: nine modules; two Workers at 1 MiB and 2 GiB; 16 native signature comparisons; 48 invocations; eight transactions installing 18 modules; 64 atomic refusals across 32 labels per placement including an import failure after seven modules installed; seven installer mutants with distinct oracles; eleven publication controls and twelve role omissions rejected. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) A probe drove the retained installer, loader and modules at both placements through 21 refusal shapes outside the corpus: a wrong module claim for the function whose bytes equal another module's, a closure claimed as its parent module, a metadata word pointing at another function's arity record, a corrupted symbol header, a wrong function version word, an unregistered address in a keyword vector, a non-boolean arity flag, a wrong debug version, an unregistered symbol in both lists, a duplicate in the expected list, reordered module rows with a wrong previous value, a NIL closure environment, a NIL capture cell, a capture cell outside memory, a predecessor two generations back, registry capacity below the requested slot, a dirty registry row, an occupied tail-table slot, a memory-capability mismatch raised after the registry rows are journaled, and boot-slot reuse in a fifth generation. Every refusal carried the expected diagnostic and left the registry, TCR, image, symbol and function objects, both tables and installer state identical; the four publications and the values after redefinition equal the native sequence, and the old top-level code still returns 7. Two probe expectations of mine were wrong and corrected: relabeling one function row is a wrong identity, not a duplicate, and the replacement module imports no code globals, so an empty code map cannot fail it.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) manifest module rows are matched to catalog rows by name, so row order is immaterial to admission; (b) the completeness check compares the manifest with the owner's own expected list, so it detects a manifest that drops an alias but not an owner that omits one from both lists, which is the trusted-owner scope as stated; (c) instances compiled during a refused transaction are discarded with their loader, and a slot freed by rollback is reusable, while slots of retained old modules are never reused, as the scope states. Acceptance of the slot is the user’s call.

LL11-b proposal. The compiler extension adds the native class `ccl::no-applicable-method-exists`, a subclass of `error` in `l1-error-system.lisp`, to the mask table at 8192 and to both admission lists; replaces the fixed twelve-row registry check with a tagged, bounded check admitting exactly twelve or thirteen rows; stores the two constructor operands into the GF and ARGS slots at mask 32796 in both emitter modes; and intercepts three fixture-private link names in the multiple-value call path, emitting a rooted two-operand constructor and typed slot readers through the existing `$condition_field` mask check. The dispatch model is portable Lisp: a six-element cons record (methods, direct cell, encapsulated flag, inner cell, callable, trace), a closure that dispatches through the record, a body that tests the method list before consulting either cell, a table selector that carries a universal method as fallback while searching EQL keys, recomputation to a direct method, the table or the missing entry, removal by identity, addition replacing an equal key, and a missing entry that signals the real condition with a CONTINUE restart that re-invokes the callable. The native oracle builds a real generic function, mutates it with DEFMETHOD and REMOVE-METHOD under and without ADVISE, retains the stale-method values 10 and 11 after the last removal as the explicit U1 defect, obtains the required protocol values separately through NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD, and supplies specificity, replacement, cleanup, resignalling, arity and CONTINUE witnesses; class shapes are exported from the same session.

Replays. (1) The verifier passed from the detached worktree: packet hashes, 358 source pins including the integrated installer, the LL11-a integration record and the native CLOS, dcode, error-system, encapsulation and removal-probe sources, toolchain hashes, native R6/R6a of the exact proposal (21,843 native tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs), the full runner with nine compiled faults and the inherited condition corpus, and 1,076 deterministic files identical to the packet; the record equals Codex's. Assessment: 30 modules; two Workers at 1 MiB and 2 GiB; 144 invocations; 78 semantic comparisons against native values; 60 installations; both native defect modes retained apart from the required protocol; eight registry refusals for short, oversize, wrong-kind and twelve-row registries; nine compiled faults with distinct oracles including the development-found universal-first selector; 107 inherited modules with 184 comparisons under a twelve-row registry; eleven publication controls and twelve role omissions rejected. Both ledger tools pass at the commit. (2) A probe appended four functions to the runtime source, compiled the 35-module corpus through the proposal compiler with the fixture's own driver, and drove it at both placements in direct and encapsulated modes: a CONTINUE handler re-entered after the restart transfer that installs a wrong-key method first and the universal method second returned 30 and 31 with two methods registered; an `error` clause caught the new condition and read its GF slot while a preceding `type-error` clause did not fire; the constructor and both readers worked without signalling; after a raw store with only an EQL method registered, both a matching and a missing key ran the stale code until recomputation restored the table; a poke of the direct cell while encapsulated was ignored; and a reader applied to a cons refused at the code-4 boundary. The fixture's own scenarios also passed with the extra modules installed.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) the correction lives in the fixture's generated dispatch model; CCL's own `l1-dcode.lisp` publication, pinned as a source, still carries the stale-dcode defect the packet retains, so compiling native CLOS later inherits it unless that source is changed, which this unit does not claim; (b) the guard tests only for an empty registry, so a stale store with a non-empty registry still runs the stale code, as the scope states; (c) the private readers refuse non-instance arguments at the code-4 boundary rather than as a handleable Lisp error, which differs from native SLOT-VALUE on a misuse the corpus does not make; (d) the registry check changes the bytes of every condition-using module, so the inherited corpus is re-executed against native values rather than compared byte-for-byte. Acceptance of the slot is the user’s call.

## Hundred-and-third Claude audit — LL11-b integration at 01e3d10e and D1 integer service at b543ed5e — 19 September 2026

Scope. `01e3d10e` accepts and integrates the reviewed LL11-b empty-dispatch compiler; `b543ed5e` adds an isolated freestanding D1 integer service (add, subtract, multiply, ash, integer-length, truncate) as an auxiliary prerequisite for LL16 with no slot credit. Both reviewed from the detached worktree at `b543ed5e`.

Integration checks. Outside `doc/` the commit changes only `compiler/WASM32/wasm32-backend.lisp`, from `a303b865…` to `bd608480…`; that hash equals the retained proposal file in the LL11-b packet and equals a fresh regeneration of the fixture's `backend.py` over the pre-integration compiler. The eight runtime files hash as recorded and unchanged. The acceptance record binds review commit `83bd90ab` and review hash `98c0695c…`, which the review file hashes to at that commit; the integration record binds the acceptance hash `597f8f8d…` and the previous integration `c1cc390b…`, both verified, and carries the four audit-102 observations into runtime-obligations.md. The evidence repository moved to `09ade719` and then `c3a92d48`, clean; the catalog, the source-index snapshot, the through-LL11b accepted results and the integer packet hash as indexed. Ledger: 15 accepted, 16 missing, zero unreviewed; the integer packet is indexed NOT_REVIEWED with no gate credit.

Integer service. A 124-line C module compiled freestanding with no imported function, no data or element segment, a 64 KiB shadow stack below 128 KiB, and a decoded call graph of one exported worker calling four leaf helpers. Inputs are tagged fixnums or tag-6 pointers to subtag-7 little-endian two's-complement digit vectors inside an owner-authenticated region; the reader refuses wrong tags, unbacked or oversized headers, zero digits, fixnum-range values and redundant sign words, converts to sign/magnitude in scratch, and refuses magnitudes above 1,024 digits as capacity. Sign/magnitude add and subtract with compare, schoolbook multiply, left shift with an exact bit budget, floor-correct right shift with discarded-bit increment, integer-length via magnitude minus one, and bitwise long division produce results re-encoded as minimal two's complement with the fixnum boundary at ±2^29 and a sign word only when the top bit disagrees; both truncate values are encoded before one combined allocation check, and the four result words are written last. Owner refusals cover region alignment, backing, the reserved stack, pairwise overlap including the result words, workspace size and operation number, all before any read.

Replay. `packet.py verify` from the detached worktree pinned the eleven sources, the two toolchains and the native kernel and image, rebuilt the module and twelve mutants, executed native CCL at three policies and Node at 1 MiB and 2 GiB, and reproduced all 75 deterministic files; the verification record equals Codex's: 4,424 cases, 13,272 native comparisons, 8,848 target comparisons, 636 chain steps, 48 refusals, twelve faults rejected, the rebuilt binary `a56d7f7f…` equal to the packet's. Both ledger tools pass at the commit.

Probe. A differential harness with guard gaps around every owner region ran 6,592 further cases at both placements against the packet binary: every capacity edge (1,024-digit sums, 512×512-digit products at exactly 32,768 bits, 3·2^32766 versus 3·2^32767, shift counts of 2^32−1, 2^32 and −2^32, the 32,768-bit operand shifted by zero and by one); right shifts of ±2^k±1 by k−1, k and k+1 for k up to 32,768; fixnum crossings in every operation; and 6,000 random operands up to 1,024 digits with multiply and divide emphasized. Every value equalled Python, every allocation was minimal with zero padding, every refusal preserved inputs, allocation and publication, and no byte outside the output, scratch, result and reserved-stack regions changed. Structural extras: a canonical 1,026-digit input refuses with status 2; −2^32768 in 1,025 digits refuses with status 3; objects extending past the region, headers at region end, valid objects outside the input region, a malformed second operand after a valid first, scratch in the reserved stack, scratch over the result words, results over the input, results eight bytes from memory end, and a region end before its start all refuse as documented; the output limit exactly equal to the encoded size and the result words at memory end minus sixteen succeed. Two probe-built faults: a check of only the first result's allocation passes the fixture's whole corpus and all 48 controls and, at a limit between the two results, returns success after writing the remainder past the limit; removing the pointer alignment test produces a byte-identical binary.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) the `atomic-two-results` control uses a quotient that alone exceeds the limit, so no fixture control discriminates a first-value-only allocation check from the combined check the README claims; the shipped service does check the combined size, as the probe shows at limits 16, 24 and 32; (b) the `(p&7)` alignment test is unreachable once the tag is 6, and clang eliminates it, so the `misaligned-pointer` control exercises the header check; (c) a canonical input wider than 1,025 digits is refused as malformed, status 2, while the same magnitude in 1,025 digits is refused as capacity, status 3; (d) the largest multiply or divide operands in the corpus are 64 digits (the `quotrem-1024` rows and the 30-digit factorial chain), so schoolbook multiply and long division at hundreds of digits rest on the probe, not the retained corpus. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user’s call.

A default-mode compile of quoted NUMBER, REAL and TRUNCATE succeeds under the proposal and refuses with `:b-source` under the integrated compiler.

Verdict: no defect. Observations, none a defect: (a) native CCL at every policy treats a NIL divisor as an omitted optional argument and returns the dividend and zero, while the target signals TYPE-ERROR datum NIL expected REAL; the corpus has no nonnumeric divisor, and the target follows the standard where native does not, so the divergence needs a disclosed decision rather than a fix; (b) the explicit-constructor oracle tests the divisor before the dividend, so it reports DIVISION-BY-ZERO for `(truncate nil 0)` where real native and the target both signal TYPE-ERROR; the corpus has no such case and the added reference functions would disagree with the target on one if it were added; (c) `arithmetic-error-operands` on a type-error and `type-error-datum` on a division-by-zero signal TYPE-ERROR on the target and NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD-EXISTS natively; handlers for `error` agree, and this is the existing checked-reader convention extended to two more readers; (d) when both operands are nonnumeric native `ash` reports the count and native `+` at safety 3 speed 0 reports the right operand, while the target always reports the left; the corpus has one nonnumeric operand per case; (e) the quoted-symbol admission list is extended for all modes, not only under the opt-in special, so the identical default corpus does not show that default-mode admission widened by three symbols; (f) the no-handler nonnumeric refusal now reports 5, the type-failure kind at the fatal boundary, where audit 105 saw the service's 32; the numeral coincides with the registry fault code. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user’s call.

## Hundred-and-seventh Claude audit — integer-condition R2 at 2ff6752c — 19 September 2026

Scope. `2ff6752c` adds `tests/wasm/stage1/integer-condition-review/`, a superseding proposal for the integer-condition unit answering audit 106, with packet `2026-09-19-stage1-integer-conditions-r2` hashing `113308bf…`. No file outside `tests/` and `doc/` changes; the runtime service copy equals the integrated `integer-service.mjs`. The index marks R1 reviewed, not accepted and superseded, bound to review commit `71f7fc9d`; the evidence repository record advances to `c28ddd7c` with catalog `e382134e…` and source index `6b8998dc…`, all verified against the store.

Proposal. The generator pins the integrated compiler `03b81251…`; its output regenerates to the packet copy `d328c6bc…` and differs from the R1 compiler in three places: the quoted-symbol allowlist is extended only while the numeric special is bound, TRUNCATE stores tagged one over a NIL divisor in its private root slot after both operand expressions have completed and before the type checks, and ASH classifies the count before the value while the other operations keep left-first checks. The native oracle for the added reference functions constructs the condition only when the dividend is real and the divisor zero, otherwise calling native TRUNCATE, so a nonnumeric dividend with a zero divisor is a native TYPE-ERROR and a NIL divisor takes native's omitted-argument path. New native evidence files retain the five-policy check-order matrix, the NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD-EXISTS class of both readers on a type-error, and default-refused, numeric-admitted results for the three symbols. The corpus keeps every R1 case and adds NIL divisors across fixnum bounds and bignums in plain, short and dynamic multiple-value calls, the eight dividend and divisor combinations of NIL, T and zero with priority reported, both operand orders of nonnumeric pairs for `ash`, `+`, `-` and `*`, evaluation-effect cases and wrong-class reader calls caught by ERROR. Controls add a fault omitting the NIL normalisation, a fault restoring ASH's old order, an admission control that unconditionally widens the allowlist and is rejected by the native source-admission assertion, and the original biased oracle run against the same modules and rejected at the priority cases.

Replay. `packet.py verify` from the detached worktree pinned 403 sources, reconstructed the native inputs, requalified the retained R6/R6a artifacts, recompiled the positive, thirteen faulty, one admission and one biased-oracle compilers and the default corpus, and reproduced all 2,702 deterministic files; the record equals Codex's: 67 modules, 423 native cases, 1,693 comparisons, 477 collector copies, one growth, 252 inline checks, three checked refusals and a nested-boundary refusal per placement, seventeen rejected controls, 60 default-mode files identical, native R6/R6a passing with 21,843 tests and 162 of 164 FASLs unchanged. The retained policy matrix agrees with the audit-106 native probe: at safety 3 speed 0 native `+` and `*` report the right operand first, `-` and the other four policies report the left, and `ash` reports the count at every policy.

Probe. Twelve further forms were compiled through R2 with the fixture driver and corrected oracle and executed by the harness with mismatch collection: the divisor binding returned beside the quotient after NIL normalisation, nested and dynamic NIL divisors, zero divided by NIL, a NIL-divisor call inside a collecting DIVISION-BY-ZERO handler that resignals, a NIL-divisor result then divided by zero, both-nonnumeric pairs for `truncate` and `*`, `+` and `ash` with a NIL right operand, and `ash` of zero by NIL. 452 cases at both placements, 243 collections in the poisoning mode, roots, stack, handlers and binding vector restored, no mismatch.

Verdict: no defect. The six audit-106 observations are each fixed or retained as data: the NIL divisor now follows native, the oracle no longer precedes native's type check, the reader-class difference and the fatal kind 5 are documented with native evidence, the policy-dependent check order is retained without being converted into target expectations, and the quote admission is gated and controlled. Remaining as stated by the fixture: the target's left-first order for `+` and `*` differs from native at one policy, readers on the wrong class signal TYPE-ERROR rather than native's NO-APPLICABLE-METHOD-EXISTS, and OPERATION and OPERANDS follow the explicit constructor rather than the x86-64 trap. Acceptance of the auxiliary record is the user’s call.

index.json marks the owner packet ACCEPTED_AUXILIARY with review commit and
integration path; the three audit-108 observations are carried verbatim
into `runtime-obligations.md`; the runtime README documents the new profile.

**Proposal.** A freestanding C service `float_calculate` with ten i32
arguments over private memory: add, subtract, multiply, divide, six
comparisons and coercion to single or double over fixnums, canonical
bignums of at most 1,024 magnitude limbs, singles and doubles. Integer
conversion rounds once from guard and sticky bits; comparisons are exact
without rounding the integer; mask and safe words select the first enabled
flag in the order invalid, zero-divide, overflow, underflow, inexact; an
enabled condition publishes NIL with no allocation. The reviewed Stage 0
f64 detector is reused with only its memory maximum changed, and a new
f32 exactness witness uses TwoSum, exact 48-bit products and a
multiply-back division check with tininess after rounding. The corpus is
an independent rational oracle over the Stage 0 `ieee.py`. Native CCL is a
value witness under masked exceptions; scalar SSE witnesses f32 flags.
structure.py asserts the stack top global is 131072 with no data segment
after LLVM's jump table had moved it to 131088 in development; that
original failure and the rejecting `stack-floor` fault are retained.
Evidence catalog `62d42ffd…`, source index `5dabeeb8…`, packet `edf8a775…`
and evidence commit eaae5a8b match `repository.json` and `index.json`.

**Replay.** `packet.py verify` from the detached worktree: PASS, 96
deterministic files, 19 pins; summary equals the packet: 39,627 cases,
118,881 target comparisons at 128 KiB, 1 MiB and 2 GiB, 1,857 witness
calls per placement, 1,167 hardware witnesses, 63 refusals, six exact fits,
fifteen rejected faults, native 6,153 cases with 5,939 equal and 214
differences in exactly the two stated categories: 134 explicit
integer-coercion overflows and 80 integer-versus-infinity orderings where
CCL reports a huge bignum equal to infinity. The detector text differs
from the Stage 0 file only in the memory maximum.

**Probe.** 27,448 fresh cases through the retained binary and harness at
the same three placements: 13,056 signed integer-versus-float orderings
across fractions, signed zero, the fixnum bounds, 2^53 neighbours and the
smallest subnormal; 1,024-limb magnitudes coerced, compared with infinity
and the largest double, and used in mixed arithmetic; 648 integer-to-single
tie and near-tie roundings for 24- to 59-bit values in both signs; and
3,000 random cases from a fresh seed that, unlike the fixture's random
population, include the two coercion operations. 1,140 double-only
arithmetic values were also checked against Python's hardware doubles with
no disagreement. One family failed: coercing a negative double that
underflows to zero, for example `-4.67e-95`, to single. The service
publishes `80000000`, as do IEEE 754 and the x86 conversion; the fixture
oracle expects `00000000` because `corpus.coerce` converts the rounded
Fraction zero to a float and loses the sign, guarding only an input that
is already zero. The retained corpus has 24 double-to-single rows, none
negative, and its random cases exclude coercions, so the defect never
surfaced. With that expectation corrected the probe passes at all three
placements with 547 witness calls each. 24 further edge and refusal probes
behaved as the README states: a result record touching the output limit or
the input end is admitted, objects straddling the input end refuse with 2,
comparisons require the second operand while coercions ignore it, an empty
input extent admits fixnum comparison, unchecked division by zero
publishes infinity with no flag, a mask without the zero-divide bit
records the flag but publishes the quotient, the record may sit at the
128 KiB floor and the input at memory end, a mask above 31 refuses, the
fixnum floor -2^29 is accepted as a fixnum and refused as a bignum, and a
1,025-limb object is accepted with a zero sign limb and refused with a
magnitude limb.

**Verdict.** Service: no defect found. Fixture: one oracle defect and a
coverage gap. `corpus.coerce` must give the rounded zero the sign of its
source, and the corpus should include negative double-to-single underflow
and coercion operations in its random population. The packet's retained
claims stand for the corpus as run; a dependent unit should not build on
the oracle until corrected. Observation: the fifteen fault oracles match
by focused case name, so a fault is rejected whenever its own case fails
for any reason; the required positive run limits but does not remove that
looseness. Ledger unchanged: 15 accepted, 16 missing, 0 unreviewed.
Acceptance is the user's decision.

**Replay.** `packet.py verify` from the detached worktree: PASS; verification.json, execution summary, controls, eager rows, native.json and qualification summary byte-identical to the packet — 1,277 deterministic files, 442 source pins, 36 modules, 475 native cases × 4 settings = 1,900 comparisons, 744 forced collections, 1,488 assurances, eager/cold identical, 11 mutants rejected, 60 default-mode files identical to generic-dispatch R1, native R6/R6a PASS (21,843 tests per build, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs). Development attempts r1–r9 exist in /tmp; r9 equals the retained run; development.json lists seven failures and the passing pre-controls r5.

**Probe.** Native CCL (the census-r7 kernel and image, compiled lambdas so nothing constant-folds) against the adapter and, separately, against the generated `h_double`/`h_single`/`h_add`/`h_mul` modules through the retained harness with fifteen auditor rows at four settings:

| input | mode | native (compiled) | port |
|---|---|---|---|
| (float (1- 2^1024) 1.0d0) | 0 | +∞ | +∞ |
| (float (1- 2^1024) 1.0d0) | 7 (default) | +∞, no error | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (float (- 2^1024 2^970) 1.0d0) | 7 | +∞ | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (float (- 2^1024 2^970 1) 1.0d0) | 7 | max double | max double |
| (float (- 2^1024 2^970 1) 1.0d0) | 31 | max double, no error | FLOATING-POINT-INEXACT |
| (float 2^1024 1.0d0) | 0/7/31 | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (float (1- 2^128) 1.0s0) | 7 | +∞ | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (float (- 2^128 2^103 1) 1.0s0) | 31 | max single | FLOATING-POINT-INEXACT |
| (+ (1- 2^1024) 1.0d0) | 7 | +∞ | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (* (1- 2^128) 1.0s0) | 7 | +∞ | FLOATING-POINT-OVERFLOW |
| (/ 7.0d0 0.0d0) payload | 7 | (DIVISION-BY-ZERO / (7.0d0 0.0d0)) | (T 7.0d0) via h_collect, same |
| (= (1+ 2^1024) +∞), (= 2^1024 +∞), (< (1- 2^1024) +∞) | 7 | NIL, T, T | NIL, T, T |
| (< 7 9) | 7 | T | T (raw supports integer comparisons; integer `/` refuses 45) |
| (eq (float 1.5s0 1.0s0) 1.5s0) | 7 | T | T |

Native's `%bignum-dfloat`/`%bignum-sfloat` signal only when the integer length exceeds 1024 or 128 bits; below that they build the float from the top bits and round to infinity without consulting the enable word. The adapter reproduces the length rule but the raw service reports the IEEE overflow flag for the rounding band, and the live enable word (default 7) turns it into a condition. Rows 283–294 of the corpus cover exactly 2^128 and 2^1024; no row lies in the band.

**Finding (fixture claim and coverage, not a runtime defect).** The README states the adapter "preserves two native library conventions", that native explicit coercion errors are reproduced and that "the join refuses any other difference". For integers in [2^1024−2^970, 2^1024) and [2^128−2^103, 2^128), and for inexact integer-to-float rounding at mask 31, the generated code differs from native CCL under the default FP mode and the difference is neither in the corpus nor declared. Whether the port should adopt native's silent infinity or declare the band as a D6 difference is a compatibility decision like the exact-tiny one; either way the claim needs the band.

**Observations (no defect).** (1) Native's real x86-64 division-by-zero payload already equals the synthesized `h_collect` oracle, so the flet substitution was not needed for that case on this host. (2) Rooting is correct: the four-slot frame is linked before operand evaluation, operands are reloaded from the frame after the call, and the condition operand list is built inside a further frame; the auditor's rows passed with forced collection before every assurance at both placements. (3) Integer/integer comparison and same-format identity go through the raw service and the adapter as documented; integer division remains a checked refusal 45. (4) Unchecked rows are compared against native run with all traps masked, which is the modelling the README implies but does not state.

**Verdict.** No runtime defect in 3c8411f1 or 401c1334; one finding against the fixture's compatibility claim and corpus coverage (the native integer-to-float rounding band). Auxiliary; not integrated; no LL16 credit. Acceptance is the user's decision.

## Hundred-and-thirteenth Claude audit — generated floating calls R2 at 0590c6a2 — 20 September 2026

**Scope.** Codex's 0590c6a2 "Match native CCL integer coercion in generated floating calls" (new `tests/wasm/stage1/float-calls/review-followup/`, `doc/WASM/stage1/integer-float-coercion.md`, packet STAGE1-FLOAT-CALLS-R2), answering audit 112 under the user's decision "Match native CCL below the limits". Review worktree detached at 0590c6a2. No shared source was edited by the auditor.

**Evidence.** packet.json 7aa9cef401d22ec2eb121e9bb2a3cee2e371b206f8c25562e0ba02b02ce5e049, catalog 5539e32ac4b6cc8feddaff446c3dd11bf8c24ab4006d0992dd46d65400e54bdb, source index f162f2dccd5037612c97708b910eed636f5ca7256a8eb795ee9149ab21f87274, evidence commit b3f75569, 15 files. Inputs pin the R1 packet, its qualification summary, verification and compiler (e05c33de…, equal to `generate()` at HEAD), float-core R2 cases/detector/float.wasm, collector.wasm and integer.wasm. R1's index row is now REVIEWED_COMPATIBILITY_FINDING / SUPERSEDED with review commit 97f589ad.

**Change.** `primitive.py` derives `float-lisp.c` from the integrated `float.c`: the exported body becomes `static calculate(..., native)`; the two operand-coercion flag lines become `fa=safe&&!(native&&!a.kind&&length(&a.i)>60)?flags(status):0;` (and likewise `fb`); `float_calculate` re-exports with native 0 and `float_calculate_lisp` with native 1. `length` is the magnitude bit length, so the rule is by magnitude, not the D1 tag: integers of more than 60 magnitude bits (native x86-64 bignums) convert without conversion flags while fixnums keep hardware-style inexact; the rounded value, the tie and the rounding to infinity are unchanged, and later arithmetic flags, the D6 enable word, the adapter's explicit-limit error and the exact-tiny difference are untouched. `owner()` changes one call in a derived copy of `float-service.mjs`; the runtime file, compiler, loader and adapter are unchanged. `run.py` extends the R1 corpus with a boundary matrix (max-finite, below-tie, tie, above-tie, below-limit, limit, above-limit, inexact, native-fixnum below/limit/edge, D1 fixnum below/edge; both signs; masks 0, 7, 16, 31; both safety modes; explicit coercion and four operations in both operand orders) plus post-conversion arithmetic rows (invalid, division by zero, overflow, inexact) at eight masks, compares each directly with native, replays all 59,083 accepted raw cases through the original entry at three placements, and reuses R1's R6, compiled faults and default witnesses by exact compiler hash. `controls.py` rejects nine faults (old entry, old right-operand conversion, suppressed fixnum inexact, cutoff at 59 bits, suppressed arithmetic overflow and invalid, and the three R1 adapter faults re-run on the new primitive), each by function/id assertion.

**Replay.** `packet.py verify` from the detached worktree: PASS; verification, summary, controls, eager rows, native.json and raw-regression byte-identical to the packet — 1,076 deterministic files, 450 pins, 40 modules, 4,379 native cases × 4 settings = 17,516 comparisons, 7,768 forced collections, eager/cold equal, 9 mutants, raw regression 59,083 × 3. Development: probe-r1 is the R1 harness failing at h_single/664 (116 vs native's rounded largest finite single); r2 a harness authoring error; r3 the passing pre-controls run; r4 the retained run.

**Probe.** 261 integers: three random values at each bit length from 25 to 1,024 in both signs, 2^24+1, 2^53+1, 2^60−1, 2^60, 2^60+1, −2^60, −2^60−1, the single and double band and tie boundaries, and a constructed 60-bit fixnum (576460855382638528) whose single rounding differs between direct and via-double rounding. Each ran at masks 7 and 31 through explicit single and double coercion, `(+ n 1.0d0)`, `(* n 3.0s0)` and `(- 1.0d0 n)` in native CCL (census-r7 kernel and image, compiled lambdas with runtime arguments) and through the new primitive via the unchanged adapter: 2,610 rows, zero differences. The crafted fixnum gives 5d000001 on both sides, so native's fixnum-to-single conversion rounds once and the primitive's single rounding matches it. The fifteen audit-112 rows re-run through the generated modules now produce native's answers.

**Observations (no defect).** (1) The R1 index row's `review_commit` is a short hash where other rows use full hashes. (2) `summary.json` records `raw_regression_comparisons: 177249` as a constant derived from the asserted 59,083 × 3 case counts rather than from the report. (3) The h_collect synthesized oracle remains and is now declared as not claimed necessary. (4) The port's D1 fixnum boundary (2^29) is exercised only to show it does not affect the rule.

**Verdict.** No defect in 0590c6a2. The audit-112 finding is resolved under the user's decision, with the mathematical entry preserved and the compatibility entry matching native compiled CCL across the retained matrix and the auditor's independent sample. Auxiliary; not integrated; no LL16 credit. Acceptance is the user's decision.

## Hundred-and-fourteenth Claude audit — S1-LL16-a numeric qualification R1 at 1ade14aa, with integration f2b1af41 — 20 September 2026

**Scope.** `1ade14aa` “Qualify S1-LL16-a generated numeric operations”: new fixture `tests/wasm/stage1/numeric-qualification/` (run, packet, assess, benchmark, inspect_dispatch, qualification_controls, publish, scope, coverage, development), inventory status EXECUTED with runner, gate result 15 accepted / 15 missing / 1 unreviewed, index row STAGE1-NUMERIC-QUALIFICATION-R1, repository pointer. **Change.** The fixture reuses the float-calls R2 driver with the integrated compiler in place of the proposal derivation. Rows: the 4,379 accepted floating cases; all 4,553 integer-core cases regenerated from the Python oracle in a separate interpreter, each at native `(safety 0 speed 3)`, `(3 0)` and `(3 3)` with `debug 1` (13,659 rows, forms `+ - * ash integer-length` and `truncate` under `handler-case` for division-by-zero → 102); twelve benchmark rows (six 64-operation loops, checked and unchecked). Native `compile.lisp` gains `qualification-native-form`, which injects the `optimize` declaration by function-name prefix; native values are asserted equal to the Python oracle before target execution (`independent-integers.json`). Binaries are rebuilt from the integrated sources and asserted equal to the reviewed integer-core, float-calls R2 (`float_calculate_lisp` export) and float-core R2 detector binaries. Source pins: R2 pins plus exactly the drift declared in `integration-float-calls.json`. Controls: eight assessor edits, four altered WAT instructions (add→sub, ash→length, truncate→mul, float add→sub) executed against real native rows, poisoned tables over 13,416 integer rows × 4 settings, two dispatch-graph mutations, twelve role omissions. Benchmark: seeded paired trials, 1 s warmup and 30 × 250 ms samples per mode and workload, paired bootstrap with 10,000 resamples; declared descriptive only. Development attempts are real first-error logs (import collision, old detector memory declaration, assessor signature, embedded-script syntax error, a SIGTERM-stopped benchmark, a poison count that inherited the full headline).

**Replay.** The retained verifier (`packet.py verify`) ran from the detached worktree: pins and toolchain verified, compiler rebuilt in pristine U1, native and generated execution, raw rational corpus, dispatch decode, controls and fresh timing. Result PASS with 208 deterministic files byte-identical and 470 source pins; assessment 18,050 native cases, 72,200 comparisons, 31,000 collections, 76 modules, cold identical, 13,659 independent integer answers. Fresh timing: ratios 0.986–1.004, per-operation cost 79–81 µs, matching the retained run’s 0.995–1.001 and 79–81 µs.

**Probe.** Two poisoned-table runs over the retained execution copy, both generated tables cleared before the case loop, at all four placement/collection settings. (a) All 4,379 floating rows (`f_*`, `h_*`, checked and unchecked): 17,516 rows, 17,464 pass; the 52 failures are `f_shadow` (48) and `h_restart` (4), whose inner-module calls (`f_shadow_inner_1`, `h_restart_inner_*`) go through the table by design; every arithmetic, comparison, coercion, condition, restart, collect and type row passes without any table entry. (b) The 243 division-by-zero `truncate` rows excluded by the fixture: 972 rows, all pass, so `handler-case` delivery of the port’s DIVISION-BY-ZERO needs no table entry either. Fallback independence therefore holds beyond what the fixture ran.

**Observations (no defect).** (1) Target-side policy collapse: `q1_*` and `q2_*` modules are byte-identical for all six operations, and `q0_*__unchecked` differs from the checked module only in the `$float_slow` safety constant (for `truncate` the two are identical). The three native policies are three real compilations, but the generated side has two variants, so 18,212 of the 72,200 comparisons repeat `q1` target executions; “13,659 independent integer-policy answers” is a native-side count. (2) The fixture’s poison run covers integer rows only and excludes the 243 division-by-zero rows; `README.md` says “integer leaves” while `dispatch.json`’s scope says “numeric leaves”. The probe above shows the wider claim would hold. (3) Warm cost is about 80 µs per operation in both checking modes, so the checked/unchecked ratio of about 1.00 says the checking argument is invisible at the service-call scale rather than free; the retained `add` interval half-width is 2.8 percent against the 2.5 percent parameter in `benchmarks.json` (an ABI-comparison parameter; no claim is derived). (4) The LL16-a assertion says “under compiler-policy variations”; the port varies only the compile-entry checking argument and `scope.json` declares no OPTIMIZE admission extension. Whether that satisfies the assertion is part of the acceptance decision. (5) `run.py` rebuilds the primitive with `-Wl,--export=float_calculate_lisp` read from the float-core FLAGS by AST rather than from the R2 builder; the resulting binary is asserted equal to the reviewed one, so the difference is presentational.

**Verdict.** No defect in 1ade14aa or f2b1af41. The joined qualification executes the adopted numeric subset through the unchanged integrated compiler and runtime against independent oracles, replays identically, and its fallback-independence claim is confirmed and extended by the auditor’s probe. Acceptance of S1-LL16-a is the user’s decision; until then the ledger stays 15 accepted, 15 missing, 1 unreviewed.

## Hundred-and-fifteenth Claude audit — numeric allocation fast path R1 at 26d99600 and direct Wasm scalar numerics R1 at 579a9515 — 20 September 2026

Scope. Two Codex runtime proposals written after audit 114 under the user’s performance-fix instruction. Neither is integrated. Both commits change doc/ and tests/ only; the compiler hash e05c33de… and every shared runtime file are unchanged. The proposal files are produced by tests/wasm/stage1/numeric-fastpath/derive.py and tests/wasm/stage1/scalar-floats/derive.py from the integrated runtime; rederiving both at HEAD gives bytes identical to the retained proposal directories (fast path: collector-owner 473d365a…, float-service 8772ce62…, service ab69ce10…; scalar: collector-owner d7c0ee16…, floating-capabilities 4c723777…, scalar-service 90a4b589…, plus scalar.wat and scalar.wasm).

Evidence. Catalog 2f21f307…, source index dec421f4…, evidence commit 797bc7a5…; fast-path packet c0c0f224…, scalar packet bff8d225…; both manifests verified by the retained verifiers; toolchain node 25.6.1 and wat2wasm 1.0.39 hashes verified. LL16-a index row now records audit 114 (review commit 92bcd83b) as REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND; the fifteen accepted rows are untouched.

Change, fast path (26d99600). The owner’s per-assurance validation is split: #validateLive keeps every mutable check (semispace identity and cursor, value/temp/control stack bounds and cursors, memory maximum, canonical NIL/T, binding-vector shape and ownership, result extent); #validate adds image enumeration and root-list capacity and is now called only by #copy and growMemory. ensure and collect call #validateLive; image inventory happens once per collection instead of on every assurance. #region results are memoised over the immutable cloned layout. float-service.mjs and service.mjs reuse a DataView, refreshing on buffer identity change; service.mjs precomputes the two coercion limits. Controls remove the live check, restore scanning, cache image shapes, drop canonical checks and drop the view refresh; a result is reused as an operand after real growth so the stale-view fault is reached by actual engine growth.

Change, scalar (579a9515). scalar.wat implements the three-i32 floating.calculate import directly. Eligibility: owner not at a safepoint boundary and geometry eligible; op ≤ 11, safety ≤ 1; $live passes; root aligned, inside the value stack above the base slot, equal to root_head, count 4, result and padding slots NIL; fp_control ≤ 31 and, with safety 1, underflow/inexact not enabled; operands fixnum or owned/pinned finite single or double (both fixnums refused for arithmetic); finite result; sufficient space. Comparisons compare in f64 (exact for 30-bit fixnums) and publish T/NIL with status 3072. Arithmetic rounds both operands to single first when the width is 32, matching the Lisp primitive’s operation(); the result is stored as header 271 or 791, the allocation pointer bumped, then the root published, with no call between first write and publication. Same-format FLOAT returns the operand after validating the second object as a finite number, as the adapter does; other coercions ignore the second object, as the adapter does. Status words are 3072 (operation) and 1024 (coercion), equal to the Lisp service’s for every eligible case because classify() yields 0 for a finite result of finite operands and coerce() clears the inexact status when underflow/inexact are not enabled. Any miss calls the Lisp service unchanged, then refreshes cached semispace bounds and the eligibility flag. Pinned-span membership is a generated import-free Wasm binary search over the sorted disjoint copy of the pinned manifest; overlapping manifests set eligible=0. The owner gains a mutable boundary Global set inside atSafepoint and a read-only admission snapshot of the immutable stack and binding regions. floating-capabilities.mjs selects the scalar service when scalarBytes is supplied. $live checks the same TCR fields against the same bounds as #validateLive, field for field.

Replay. Fast path: verify PASS, 13 deterministic files at 477 pins; 72,200 comparisons and 31,000 collections per eager/cold run; 55 owner checks; 5 faults rejected; raw-owner 6,340 comparisons, 2,834 collections, one growth. Scalar: verify PASS, 151 deterministic files at 488 pins; 72,200 comparisons and 31,836 collections per eager/cold run; raw 145,804 comparisons, 56,200 collections, one growth; 10,138 region checks; 109 direct-path checks; 10 faults rejected. Fresh timing on the recorded host: reviewed runtime 74.5–75.2 µs/op, owner-fast 4.67–4.80 µs/op, direct 147.6–155.1 ns/op for the four double workloads; single 140.3 ns, mixed 131.3 ns; native 16.1–17.4 ns (single 4.2, mixed 22.9); service-only add 93.3 ns. All within the retained ranges.

Probes. (A) Raw corpus dispatch, counted through the fallback: in the plain low run 21,855 of the 36,451 float-only cases executed directly in Wasm against the retained status oracle and 14,596 fell back (every safety-1 case with underflow or inexact enabled, nonfinite operands or results, conditions); in the moving run all 36,451 fell back before any write under the forced shortage. (B) Generated eager corpus at a non-pressure setting: 10,540 floating calls across the 18,050 cases, 2,920 direct and 7,620 fallback; the twelve bench_* rows are entirely direct (128 calls each); the h_* rows are mostly fallback because they enable underflow/inexact or use bignums and nonfinite values. Under the pressure settings every call falls back by construction. (C) Differential fuzz: 300,000 random cases over all twelve operations, fixnum/single/double operands including fixnum extremes, 2^24±k ties, single and double subnormals, signed zeros, maxima and nonfinite values, masks 0/1/2/4/7/8/16/24/31, both safeties, operands in the heap or the pinned image; each case run through the scalar service and through the Lisp service after identical resets. Status word, published value bits, allocation size, root slots, padding, root head and refusal codes were identical in all 300,000 cases; 217,739 took the direct path.

Observations, none a defect. (1) Direct-path coverage by the inherited corpora is far below the headline counts: about 2,920 of 10,540 floating calls per non-pressure generated setting and 21,855 of 36,451 raw cases per plain run; the pressure and moving runs exercise only the pre-write refusal. The fixture reports state the inherited totals, not these counts. (2) The timing harness uses 20 trials of 125 ms after 500 ms warmup, below the v3 benchmark discipline (30 × 250 ms after 1000 ms); the fixtures label the numbers descriptive, and they must not be cited as discipline measurements. Native figures include GC; Wasm figures exclude collection. (3) The direct path never runs during movement because it never collects; correctness across movement rests on the slow path and on the post-growth refresh checks (growth-positive, stale-geometry, fast-assurance-after-growth). (4) Safety 0 with underflow or inexact enabled takes the direct path; this equals the Lisp service because safety 0 reports no flags, and fast-checks records it. (5) scalarAdmission hands the mutable boundary Global to any holder of the owner reference; that holder already commands the owner, so no new authority is created. (6) The fast-path README’s ~76 µs reviewed-runtime baseline is the observer-free figure; audit 114’s ~80 µs included the test observer.

Verdict. No defect in 26d99600 or 579a9515. Both are unintegrated runtime proposals; acceptance and integration are the user’s decision, and integrating the scalar service requires the production owner to supply the digest-bound scalar binary. Ledger unchanged: 15 accepted, 15 missing, one unreviewed.

## Hundred-and-sixteenth Claude audit — S1-LL18-b strong EQ tables under movement R1 at 89de9634 — 20 September 2026

Scope. Codex commit 89de9634 “Qualify LL18-b strong EQ tables across moving collection”, reviewed in the detached worktree at that revision. The commit adds tests/wasm/stage1/hash-tables/ (README, scope, coverage, development, compile.lisp, adapter.wat, hash.c, collector.py, corpus.py, generated.py, generated.mjs, check.mjs, assess.py, run.py, packet.py, publish.py) and updates STATUS, the gate result, index.json, repository.json, changes.md, inventory.json and plan.md. No shared compiler, runtime or kernel file changes. The two intervening user-acceptance commits, 1347bbc8 (integrate audit-115 proposals) and 83b07de3 (accept LL16-a), were checked in passing: the integrated collector-owner.mjs (d7c0ee16…), float-service.mjs (8772ce62…), service.mjs (ab69ce10…), scalar-service.mjs (90a4b589…), scalar.wat (54d412dc…) and floating-capabilities.mjs (4c723777…) are byte-identical to the reviewed proposal files, and scalar.wasm (1fff023a…) rebuilds from scalar.wat with wat2wasm 1.0.39 --enable-threads. The user asked that the node:crypto dependency be set aside for this review.

Evidence. Catalog files.json f691bf6f… and source index catalog/source-index-2026-09-20-stage1-hash-tables.json 339852e8… match repository.json at evidence commit 7cea481c; packet.json a843cd0f…, result envelope b801011f…, combined results ab01623b… and inventory.json ed76b3e9… match index.json and the gate result. The packet holds 216 files including sources.tar.gz at 96 pins, the derived collector.c, hash.c, adapter.wat, the compiler copy, toolchain hashes and development.tar.gz for the twelve development attempts.

Change. hash.c implements a fixed-capacity strong EQ backing vector as a native-shaped D1 object: header subtag 74 with element count 14+2n, the fourteen-word prefix (link, flags, gc-count, free-alist, finalization-alist, weak-deletions-count, hash, deleted-count, count, cache-idx, cache-key, cache-value, size, size-reciprocal) followed by alternating key/value words and a zero pad word; the prefix order matches xdump/hashenv.lisp, and TRACK=1<<30 and MOVED=1<<29 equal the kernel’s fixnum_bitmask(28) and fixnum_bitmask(27). Address-word mixing with bounded linear probing replaces U1’s bucket arithmetic; EMPTY (243) and DELETED (251) are immediate sentinels refused as keys. ht_run validates the shape (tag, element count, capacity 4–16384 power of two, exact extent, flags, fixed prefix words, count and deleted-count bounds, cache-idx consistency) and the owner’s disjoint scratch and result storage before any write; a full-table insertion of a new key refuses with FULL before rehash; when MOVED is set the buckets are rebuilt in scratch (checking that the live and dead counts agree with the prefix and that no key repeats), the cache invalidated, the bucket array copied back, deleted-count zeroed and the flag cleared; GET consults the cache first and refreshes it on a hit, SET updates the cache, REMOVE tombstones and invalidates. The four publication words are value, second value, count and a rehashed flag. collector.py derives the scanner from runtime/wasm32/collector.c: a tag-74 branch admits only strong tracked vectors of the declared shape (weak flags, missing tracking, bad capacity, bad counts and bad cache index are rejected before any copy), and the destination scan loop sets MOVED on the destination header when a payload word at an even index ≥ 14 (a bucket key) forwards to a different address; cache slots are traced as ordinary words but do not request a rehash; value movement does not request a rehash. adapter.wat is an owner-installed B leaf whose public entry refuses and whose tail entry checks argc and the context/root layout, dispatches by an imported operation Global, supports fixed, dynamic-indirect and direct-descriptor delivery, and for the fixture collect leaf calls the collector and poisons the retired space before publishing NIL. Nine modules from the unchanged compiler (tail call, APPLY, MULTIPLE-VALUE-BIND/PROG1/CALL, direct and indirect producers, and two moving forms that collect inside generated code and inside a collecting UNWIND-PROTECT cleanup) reach the leaves through ordinary function values.

Replay. verify PASS: 118 deterministic files at 96 source pins; 1,292 native cases; 2,584 primitive and 2,588 generated/native comparisons; 2,722 generated calls per placement over the nine modules (hash_call/hash_dynamic/hash_dynamic_indirect 227 each, hash_values/hash_preserve/hash_apply 226 each, both moving forms once) at the low and above-2-GiB placements; 88 collections; 95 inherited collector checks; 52 refusals; twelve runtime faults and eight publication controls rejected; native R6/R6a reused by exact compiler hash; both moving forms return the native values [T, T, 76, 92] and [76, 92, T].

Probes. (A) Differential movement fuzz, primitive path: 300 tables of capacity 4–64 with a key pool of conses, NIL, T, 0, −1 and the table itself; 552,271 random operations and 47,729 collections; every result, count word, deleted+count bound, bucket content, forwarded cache slot and the exact moved-key condition (flag set if and only if a heap key is live) matched an identity map; 54,869 full-table refusals left the table bytes unchanged; 43,235 rehashes. (B) The same fuzz through the generated callers with the fixture’s adapters: 120 tables, 165,877 operations of which 111,173 were delivered by the six ordinary generated forms (fixed 74,115, dynamic 37,058, direct 18,529 adapter deliveries), 14,123 collections, no divergence. Across both runs 398 collections had only immediate keys live and the flag correctly stayed clear. (C) Full-table refusal through generated code, which the fixture does not exercise: SET of a fifth key into a capacity-4 table through each of the six ordinary forms delivers checked call_error 44 (40+FULL), the table bytes are unchanged, and the caller’s root chain, value-stack pointer and handler list are restored; a collection and further operations then succeed.

Observations, none a defect. (1) The native prefix and flag bits are honoured as claimed; the hash mixing, the zero size-reciprocal and the fixnum-encoded cache index deliberately differ from U1’s %hash arithmetic, and the lock-free, weak and finalization words are fixed at NIL/0 and refused otherwise, as scope.json declares. (2) check.mjs expects the moved-key flag after a trace collection whenever the count is nonzero rather than whenever a heap key is live; the retained trace never collects with only immediate keys live, so the check is sound for that trace, and probe A/B tested the exact condition. (3) options.json carries hash_seed 395 inherited from the control packet convention; hash.c is unseeded and the corpus seed is 0x18b. Bookkeeping only. (4) The native oracle is itself checked equal to a Python identity map, so the semantic content of the trace is trivial; the substance of the qualification is relocation, cache maintenance and the generated calling forms, which the twelve faults and the auditor’s fuzz exercise. (5) The tag-74 scanner lives in the fixture’s derived collector; runtime/wasm32/collector.c is unchanged, and the post-acceptance integration foreseen in plan.md will be a shared-runtime change requiring its own review. (6) The fixture collect leaf is reached twice per placement (the two moving forms) and no leaf refusal reaches generated code in the fixture; probe C covers the refusal path. (7) Automatic growth, weak tables, the HASH-TABLE wrapper, CL surface lowering, iteration order and production installation are declared out of scope; no timing is claimed.

Verdict. No defect in 89de9634. Ledger unchanged at 16 accepted, 14 missing, one unreviewed; acceptance of S1-LL18-b is the user’s decision.

## Hundred-and-seventeenth Claude audit — STAGE1-PORTABLE-DIGESTS-R1 synchronous browser-portable runtime digests at d8544fb2 — 20 September 2026

Scope. Commit d8544fb2 “Qualify synchronous browser-portable runtime digest binding”: doc/ and tests/wasm/stage1/portable-digests/ only. Auxiliary runtime-only proposal, no slot credit; LL21 paused by user direction. Reviewed in the detached worktree at d8544fb2. The intermediate commit fdec5165 (user acceptance and integration of LL18-b after audit 116) was checked as well: runtime/wasm32/hash.c and hash-adapter.wat are byte-identical to the reviewed fixture files, the collector.c edit equals the fixture’s collector.py derivation text, and the portable run rebuilds collector.wasm to the retained LL18-b binary. Compiler, kernel and runtime files are unchanged between fdec5165 and d8544fb2.

Evidence. index.json entry STAGE1-PORTABLE-DIGESTS-R1: packet.json 3c295ee4cc70ed93015061bfb938bb6a36b902c7ad2437886521235d37c01af4 (34 files), verification b1f0165232110fd75cfd010d488dc3f6afde6fe477e396322cbc08006162b869; repository.json catalog 6ef96495…, source index catalog/source-index-2026-09-20-stage1-portable-digests.json 29c8b55c…, evidence commit 154ec959. All recomputed and equal. sources.json pins 40 files (runtime/wasm32/*, wasm32-backend.lisp, the fixture, and the three reused drivers) at the worktree bytes. tools.json pins node, clang, Chromium 145.0.7632.6 and Playwright Core 1.55.0; all four present at the recorded hashes. The three input archives (binding-installation, numeric-qualification, scalar-floats) match their packets.

Change. derive.py copies runtime/wasm32/*.mjs and applies assert-once replacements: five `node:crypto` imports become `import {sha256} from './sha256.mjs'` (collector-owner, integer-service, float-service `hash=sha256`, scalar-service, loader `export const sha=sha256`); Buffer sites become bytes.mjs helpers (binary.mjs header `hex`, scalar-service import-name encoder `utf8` with UTF-8 byte length, loader and installer `snapshotBytes`, installer manifest head `utf8(JSON.stringify(m))`). A static assert refuses any remaining `node:` or `Buffer.` text. sha256.mjs is a FIPS 180-4 compression with local state, full blocks read in place, one or two padding blocks, 64-bit big-endian bit length (high word floor(len/2^29), low word (len*8)>>>0). bytes.mjs accepts views (with offsets), DataViews, ArrayBuffer and SharedArrayBuffer, always copies snapshots, and hex-encodes. The Worker harness runs under COOP/COEP with a throwing `Buffer` getter and no Node shim; every asset is also hashed by Web Crypto in the Worker.

Replay. `packet.py verify` with the pinned tools on this host: PASS, 23 deterministic files equal; summary 358 shared checks, 391 Node differential rows, 96 Wasm binaries, 13 faults REJECTED, node/browser equal, binding replay equal to the retained installation result, eager/cold equal to the accepted scalar packet.

Probes.
A. Independent SHA-256 differential (scratch fuzz.mjs) against node:crypto with the packet’s sha256.mjs/bytes.mjs (byte-identical to the fixture files): every length 0–600 at random offsets as Uint8Array view, DataView, SharedArrayBuffer view and snapshot; block-boundary neighbourhoods 64k±{2,1,0,7,8,9,55,56,57,63} up to 8192 and 400 random lengths to 4 MiB; 3,000 random Unicode strings including lone surrogates against Buffer.from(s,'utf8') for both digest and utf8(); Float64Array and Buffer-slice views; and 512 MiB−1, 512 MiB, 512 MiB+64 and 1 GiB−3 bytes. 10,347 comparisons, 0 divergences. Descriptive: the 1 GiB input took 25.9 s here (about 40 MB/s), construction-time only.
B. Same-Worker mixed-placement reproduction (scratch sw/): the retained r2 variant (one module Worker running checks() then bindingCheck at 1 MiB then at 2 GiB) fails deterministically in the pinned Chromium with `conflicting-module: actual=OBJECT_EXTENT expected=MODULE_IDENTITY`. An instrumented probe copy of installer.mjs (scratch only) shows the refusing expression is `uint(p)&&uint(n)&&p+n<=v.byteLength` with p=4168, n=16, v.byteLength=2147549184 and v.buffer===memory.buffer, i.e. a mathematically true comparison evaluating false. Without the preceding checks() the same order passes; 2 GiB→1 MiB, 2 GiB→2 GiB, 1 MiB→1 MiB and 2 GiB alone all pass. Under `--js-flags=--no-maglev` the failing case passes; under `--no-turbofan` and `--no-turbo-inlining` it still fails; under `--no-maglev --no-turbofan` it passes. Node 25.6.1 (V8 14.1.146.11) runs both placements plus checks() in one thread and passes; Chrome 153.0.8010.36 on this machine runs the identical Worker and passes. A reduced accessor probe (DataView/SAB/TypedArray byteLength ≥ 2^31 after warm-up) and a structural mimic of install()’s span closure did not reproduce it, so the exact Maglev feedback shape is not isolated; the localization to the tier and build is.

Observations, none a defect.
(1) The derivation is semantics-preserving for every replaced site. Buffer.from(Uint8Array) copied but Buffer.from(ArrayBuffer) aliased; snapshotBytes always copies, so the loader and installer are strictly stronger, and the new loader-owned-snapshot and installer-snapshot checks and their two controls bind that.
(2) scalar-service’s import-name length changes from UTF-16 units to UTF-8 bytes. Identical for the ASCII names in use (eager/cold outputs and region-name encoding unchanged); correct rather than merely equal for non-ASCII.
(3) The open same-Worker finding is a spurious refusal in the pinned Chromium 145.0.7632.6 Maglev tier on an installer line this proposal does not touch. Its direction is fail-safe (refuse, not admit) in every observation; nothing here shows a spurious admission. It is not a defect of the proposal or of the runtime source, but it is a live browser-qualification fact: the accepted installer’s 2 GiB placement can refuse valid installs on that build once warmed. Firefox and WebKit binaries exist in the Playwright cache but are not exercised; the packet claims no engine matrix.
(4) The README sentence “Adding the arguments to the error text made that run pass” is not supported by the retained logs: browser-retry.log and browser-original-again.log both fail with the arguments present; the passing browser-debug variant is not retained. The cause is now established by probe B, so the sentence is superseded rather than material.
(5) The static host-dependency scan covers `node:` and `Buffer.`; a bare `instanceof Buffer` or `process` reference would evade it. A token grep of the retained proposal for Buffer, process, require and node: finds none, and the Worker’s throwing getter would trip any executed access.
(6) The packet’s SHA expectations come from NIST vectors and node:crypto, never from the implementation; the 512 MiB+9 case exercises the upper length word for real. Not FIPS validation, as stated.
(7) No performance claim is made; pure-JS hashing of the 96 binaries is construction-time work.

Verdict. No defect in d8544fb2. The proposal’s claims hold under replay and independent differential testing; the retained browser finding is localized to the pinned Chromium build’s Maglev tier and does not implicate runtime source. Ledger unchanged at 17 accepted, 14 missing, zero unreviewed (auxiliary record); acceptance and integration are the user’s decision.

## Hundred-and-eighteenth Claude audit — STAGE1-MATERIALIZATION-R1 production D2 template materialization for LL21-a at 434bcc46 — 20 September 2026

Scope. Commit 434bcc46 (`Qualify production D2 template materialization for LL21-a`) adds `tests/wasm/stage1/materialization/` (README, scope, coverage, development, backend.py, materializer.mjs, check.mjs, harness-installer.mjs, controls.py, assessment.py, native.py, run.py, packet.py, publish.py) and updates STATUS, the evidence index/repository/gate result, the Stage 1 inventory (S1-LL21-a EXECUTED, NOT_REVIEWED) and plan. Compiler, runtime and kernel are unchanged by this commit. The intermediate commits were also examined: c6452906 binds audit 117 (`review_commit` fa8ccc0e, review-file hash) and withdraws the README claim; eadec6c9 integrates the portable digests — the nine runtime modules (binary, bytes, collector-owner, float-service, installer, integer-service, loader, scalar-service, sha256) are byte-identical to the retained `proposal/` directory of the portable-digests packet and remain so at HEAD.

Evidence. Packet 26cb1846…, results row b0de2105… (1,288 artifacts; roles module 694, test 461, template 79, installed-binary 36, log 4, compiler 4, implementation 3, schema 2, abi 2, source, host-compiler, options), catalog 9e96eef3…, source index 842e2d2e…, combined results 8a552223…, inventory de09c06c…, evidence commit ebbdf26f. Toolchain pins (node 25.6.1, wat2wasm and wasm-objdump 1.0.39) match the accepted ENGINE-MATRIX-R2 executables by hash.

Change. `backend.py` derives the compiler proposal by assert-once replacement: a new `*wasm32-template-memory*` special, `~a` in the leaf and typed-primitive FORMAT strings, and conversion of the B prefix from WRITE-STRING to FORMAT with a fourth argument; each site emits `" shared"` unless the special is bound. The original B literal contains no tilde, so the conversion preserves text. `materializer.mjs` parses the import section itself (shortest-LEB and bounds checks), requires exactly one `env.memory` import with flag byte 1 and limits ≤ 65536, cross-checks against the accepted narrow reader and `WebAssembly.validate`, binds template digest, ABI inventory, WABT classification, materializer and engine policy into a record, patches the one byte to 3 for `full` or leaves 1 for `precompiled_callback`, and at install re-derives the expected record and bytes from trusted inputs before compiling the digest-checked snapshot.

Replay. `packet.py verify` in the detached worktree: PASS, 1,148 deterministic files, 94 source pins. Native R6/R6a PASS (21,843 tests, 162 unchanged and 164 restored FASLs, 17 negative controls, 9 replayed generated modules). Summary: 18 modules, 40 default outputs identical, six Workers, 162 metadata checks, 58 invocations, 36 refusals, 11 link cases, 10 primitive/leaf calls, 11 rejected faults (ten materializer omissions and one CCL-compiled compiler fault), seven publication controls, twelve role omissions. Shared and unshared observations equal after removing installation events.

Probes. (A) Linking: for each of the 36 installed binaries, dummy imports built from the module’s import list instantiate with memory of the recorded sharedness and raise `WebAssembly.LinkError` with the other; recomputed offsets (104 for B modules, 33 leaf, 35 primitive) and SHA-256 digests equal the records. (B) Parser: 25 crafted import sections — custom, type, table-with-maximum and tag imports preceding the memory; flag 3; no maximum; maximum 65537; minimum above maximum; two memories; wrong module or name; no memory; non-canonical minimum and section-size LEBs; section overrun; short import end; truncated name; invalid UTF-8 name; unknown kind; non-funcref table; limit flags 4; tag attribute 1; six-byte and high-fifth-byte LEBs — each refuses with the named reason or locates the flag byte. (C) Templates: all 18 template `.wat` files equal their baseline with the single “ shared” token removed; the 16 B modules include five nested closure children. (D) The retained r2 failure is wat2wasm refusing a memory definition before imports, as described.

Observations (none a defect). (1) The engine identity in D2 records is declared provenance: `install()` does not detect the executing engine; the S0 engine join is enforced at qualification by executable hashes. (2) The policy admits `atomics` for the unshared profile without a per-profile filter; harmless under the wait/notify ban, and no generated module uses atomic instructions. (3) Record equality is `JSON.stringify` order-sensitive; a re-serialised record refuses with TEMPLATE_RECORD, fail-safe but brittle for tooling. (4) The parser reads a fixed two bytes per imported global; a multi-byte typed reference would desynchronise and refuse after validation. (5) The new STATUS row follows prose after the Stage 1 table with no header row. (6) Scope holds: code materialization only, fixture owner installs eagerly, no unshared runtime services, browser, JSPI or packaging claim; the 2 GiB placement passes in unshared Node memory. (7) Slot credit for LL21-a follows acceptance; ledger currently 17 accepted, 13 missing, one unreviewed.

Verdict. No defect in 434bcc46. The D2 materialization claims hold under replay and independent linking, parser and text probes; the digest integration at eadec6c9 is byte-exact to the reviewed proposal. Acceptance and integration of the compiler switch and materializer are the user’s decision.

No other compiler, runtime or kernel file changed between afc7fd5b and ff3789f2.

**Evidence.** Results ad65bc5f…, packet 4ef1adbc…, catalog 99f605a0…, source index 6d02f233…, combined results 762e2d36…, inventory 39161408…, evidence commit 0a6a3462. Node 25.6.1, wat2wasm and wasm-objdump 1.0.39 at the pinned executable hashes; `inputs.json` pins the accepted LL21-a packet, compiler, policy and module list and the U1 inputs.

**Change.** One generated Lisp function per module with the two B roles (`entry`, `tail_entry`). `validate()` checks packaging/version, ABI and layout versions, the trusted expected inventory (name, code ID, generation, in order), unique safe code IDs, unique names, slots in (0, capacity) excluding reserved and unique, positive generations, module-level versions, binary digest, exactly two exports and two entries, role/export/function-index/table, structural signature against the type section, and body extents from `ranges.mjs`. `publish()` preflights occupancy, instantiates every module before writing any slot, and rolls back written slots on a mid-publication throw. Three revisions of `plain` (optional default 7→99, 101, 103) are compiled by the accepted compiler through an isolated copy of the LL21-a driver; native CCL rebinds a fresh symbol and supplies each answer. The execution harness installs generations 2–4 into slots 17–19 after the sixteen base modules, calls the retained original and each revision, dispatches an owner-selected live binding through the registry row, and repeats in Workers restored from the snapshot at 2 MiB and 2 GiB. Timing: thirty fresh processes for cold decode/instantiate/publish; thirty 250 ms samples after a 1 s warmup for warm instantiation and full validated install; retention via `--print-wasm-offheap-memory-size` at generations 1–4 under default and eager-Liftoff flags.

**Replay.** `packet.py verify` on this host: PASS, 190 deterministic files, 111 source pins, summary 19 modules, 16 base, four generations, nine redefinition observations, 19 controls, eight faults, 197,431 bytes; seven publication controls and twelve role omissions rejected; native R6/R6a reused by exact compiler identity.

**Probes.**
- A. Measurement arithmetic, independently: cold medians 1.9293/0.3816/0.0585 ms (ranges 1.81–2.37, 0.32–0.47, 0.05–0.07); warm instantiation 0.1839 ms (1,191–1,431 iterations per trial); warm validated install 37.12 ms (seven iterations per trial, 36.59–37.44). Each of the eight retention rows equals the sum of the `NativeModule` lines in its log, and the line count equals `unique_binaries`.
- B. Body ranges: an independent parse of the import, export and code sections of all 19 full binaries reproduces every one of the 38 entry ranges and function indexes. Every module differs from its template at exactly byte 104 (1→3). The sixteen base full binaries are byte-equal to the accepted LL21-a packet’s.
- C. Revisions: `plain_g2/3/4.wat` equal `plain.wat` with its single `(i32.const 28)` replaced by 396, 404, 412; the retained observations report old `[12, 28]` and current `[12, 396/404/412]` in all three Workers.
- D. Duplicate binaries: `twins_inner_2` and `separate_inner_1` have identical text and bytes. Per-module cold compile medians are about 90 µs except the duplicate at 26.6 µs and the first module at 342 µs; V8’s native-module cache is on by default, so nineteen modules retain eighteen NativeModules.
- E. Lazy versus eager: three fresh processes of the retained `bench.mjs` cold mode measure 1.93–2.01 ms for the set; with `--no-wasm-lazy-compilation` 7.93–8.23 ms. The cited cold compilation is decode/validation under the default lazy tier; function bodies compile on first call.
- F. README figures: the eager-Liftoff retention sentence (458,778→549,180 bytes) matches `ccl-granularity-r3/retention.json` in `development.tar.gz`, the discarded r3 report run, not the retained `retention.json` (458,786→549,124). The default-tier figures and the three STATUS timings match the retained records.

**Observations.**
1. The README misstates the eager-Liftoff retention range by 8 and 56 bytes, quoting the r3 run. The retained `retention.json` is the record; the fixture README should be corrected in the disposition commit (the packet copy is immutable).
2. “Cold compilation” in README and STATUS is lazy-tier decode; eager compilation is about four times that. Scope declares Node default-tier timings descriptive, so no claim is wrong, but the label should say decode/validate.
3. `validate()` does not require distinct binary digests across logical code IDs, and the engine deduplicates identical bytes. Independent installation holds per slot and instance, not per NativeModule; the retention rows disclose this through `unique_binaries`.
4. The redefinition corpus is three constant-only revisions of one function with equal encoded size; a revision with a different body shape or size is not exercised by the mapping or retention rows.
5. The expected inventory is a trusted input by declared scope; an inventory that itself omits a function is undetectable, as the scope states.
6. Full validated install at ~2 ms per module is dominated by JavaScript SHA-256 over template and binary plus record re-derivation; a one-time construction cost as stated.
7. The STATUS row for LL21-b is inside the table. Slot credit follows acceptance (19 accepted, 12 missing).

**Verdict.** No defect in the executable evidence of ff3789f2. One stale figure in the qualification README (observation 1). Acceptance of the packaging choice and integration of the owner/build proposal are the user’s decision. Ledger unchanged at 18 accepted, 12 missing, one unreviewed.

**Probes.**

Per operation: FIND-SYMBOL and INTERN use all six forms; MAKE-SYMBOL uses symbol_call 11, symbol_apply 11, symbol_preserve 2 only; SYMBOL-NAME and SYMBOL-PACKAGE use all six.
D. Differential fuzz of the retained `symbols.wasm` against the auditor’s model in one Worker: 7,500 operations over 220 names (ASCII, Latin, BMP and astral code points, empty, embedded NUL) and all five packages including INTERN into USER and CL; 6,737 successful operations checked for identity, status keyword, value count, allocated byte count, home package, value, flags and SYMBOL-NAME/SYMBOL-PACKAGE readback; 763 refusals (table full or image exhausted) with image, configuration and result words preserved; 636 interns filling A, B, KEYWORD, USER and CL tables to 128 each and the image to 65,536 bytes. 1. MAKE-SYMBOL through generated code reaches three of the six calling forms and never the two-value, dynamic or indirect delivery, because each MAKE-SYMBOL row advances the form counter by three (op 2, then SYMBOL-NAME and SYMBOL-PACKAGE); the op-2 adapter records 24 fixed, 0 dynamic, 0 direct calls. One-value dynamic and indirect delivery is exercised by ops 3 and 4 instead. Coverage note by construction, not a defect.
2. `initial-keywords.json` is empty: none of the 36 names used with KEYWORD pre-existed natively, so the pre-existing keyword path of the image builder carries only the three status keywords.
3. The six retained loads go 4→8, 8→4 and 2 GiB→4 MiB; no retained load targets the 2 GiB base. Probe D covers 4 MiB→2 GiB and 2 GiB→8 MiB.
4. The service’s string check admits any code point ≤ 0x10FFFF, including lone surrogates; declared as code points, noted for the production string contract.
5. Scratch exhaustion during admission would report SHAPE rather than SPACE; unreachable within the declared limit of 16 packages × 2 tables × 1,024 bytes against a 65,536-byte scratch.
6. Only three of the 582 rows exercise INHERITED status (USER × NIL, T, EXPORTED) and none interns into USER or CL; probe D adds both.
7. The `nil-symbol-pointer` fault is caught at admission by the image-extent check on the raw NIL symbol pointer rather than by a later canonical-NIL confusion; it still fails at the named assertion.
8. `adapter.wat` is the integrated hash adapter with the run signature, the two-value rule for ops 0–1 and the collector branch changed; it is a fixture leaf, not integrated.
9. STATUS row inside the table. Slot credit follows acceptance (20 accepted, 11 missing).

## Hundred-and-twenty-first Claude audit — STAGE1-SYMBOLS-REVIEW-R1 LL09-a coverage and character follow-up at cacdc64f — 20 September 2026

**Scope.** Commit cacdc64f `Close LL09 coverage gaps and match native character admission`: seven new files under `tests/wasm/stage1/symbols/review-followup/` (README, scope, development, directed.mjs, native.lisp, run.py, packet.py) and the ledger (STATUS row, index entry STAGE1-SYMBOLS-REVIEW-R1 NOT_REVIEWED as an auxiliary record without slot credit, R1 scope note, repository, changes). No compiler, runtime or kernel change. The follow-up answers audit 120: it derives from every pinned R1 source, overlays one clause on the C string guard (reject U+D800–U+DFFF), drives MAKE-SYMBOL through all six generated forms twice before and after relocation with per-operation delivery counters, checks the native pre-existing ALLOW-OTHER-KEYS keyword by FIND-SYMBOL before INTERN, retains loads from 4 MiB into 2 GiB in both primitive and generated runs, and compares eight character boundaries against native CODE-CHAR. Four execution controls and six report controls.

**Intermediate commit.** 9303e962 flips STAGE1-SYMBOLS-R1 to REVIEWED_NO_DEFECT_FOUND with review_commit 8c0dd65b and review_sha256 7640c24c…, equal to the hash of `doc/WASM/stage0/claude-review.md` at that commit; the scope carries the four audit-120 observations.
